# dillad — Communities, Content and Operations Implementation Plan (Plan 2 of 2)

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build everything a `dillad` instance needs above the delivery service — communities, channels with mode and visibility, roles and overwrites, bans and moderation, DMs and group DMs, private channels with batched Adds, server-readable channels with full-text search, envelope semantics (edit, delete, pin, reactions), attachments over a content-addressed blob store, backup and restore with the generation protocol, reports and franking verification, TLS with the 443 demux and TURN, the admin CLI, `dillad doctor` and the packaging an operator installs.

**Architecture:** Every route in this plan sits behind the same stdlib `ServeMux`, the same deterministic-CBOR codec and the same `E_*` error vocabulary Plan 1 built, and every permission decision runs through one resolver over roles and channel overwrites — the instance still never sees end-to-end-encrypted plaintext, so a permission is enforced on ciphertext metadata and on who may reach the delivery service, never on message content. Server-readable channels are the one place the instance stores plaintext envelopes, and they get their own table, their own FTS index (SQLite FTS5 and Postgres `tsvector`, one corpus, two engines) and their own receiver-side envelope validation.

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.27.0 (`github.com/jonasthim/dilla`), SQLite FTS5 through modernc v1.59.0 and Postgres full-text search through pgx v5.11.0, certmagic v0.25.4 and `pion/turn/v5` v5.0.13 for the 443 demux, `livekit/livekit-server` v1.13.7 through `internal/sfu` for calls, the Rust `dilla-testkit` remote-DS mode from Plan 1 for the scenarios, and a distroless container plus a systemd unit for delivery.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-dilla-design.md` (sections "System architecture", "Server (Go): API, realtime gateway, delivery service, permissions on ciphertext", "Data model", "Error handling and failure modes", "Testing and verification strategy", "Design addenda", roadmap W1–W3 and W5), with `protocol/01-groups.md`, `protocol/02-delivery-service.md`, `protocol/04-envelope-and-franking.md` and the `protocol/09-http-api.md` Plan 1 task 0 creates as the wire contract.

**What this plan consumes from Plan 1** (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-dillad-1.md`) — Plan 1 must be green through task 27a before task 1 here starts:

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements implicitly include this section. Values are exact, and they are the same
constraints Plan 1 runs under.

**Worktree and branch**
- Worktree: `/home/thim/Repositories/dilla/.claude/worktrees/sdd-dilla-protocol`, branch `sdd-dillad`, base `a0a63fc` plus Plan 1. Every path is repository-relative; every command is absolute.
- One PR for this plan. Commits are conventional-subject and `git commit -s`; the executor adds the trailers the repository already uses. No `cd … &&` chains, no shell variables, one command per line.

**Toolchain**
- Go: `/home/thim/.local/go/bin/go`, `go 1.27.0`, module `github.com/jonasthim/dilla`, invoked as `/home/thim/.local/go/bin/go -C /home/thim/Repositories/dilla/.claude/worktrees/sdd-dilla-protocol …`.
- Rust: `/home/thim/.cargo/bin/cargo` (scenario files and the testkit); Node 24 for `packages/protocol-vectors`.
- No sudo. No Postgres server on the dev box: every Postgres test skips locally with the reason `DILLA_TEST_PG is unset: Postgres tests run in CI's service container`, and runs in CI against `postgres:18.6-alpine3.24` with `POSTGRES_INITDB_ARGS: --locale-provider=builtin --builtin-locale=C.UTF-8`.

**Dependency pins**
- Unchanged from Plan 1. This plan adds no Go module beyond the ones Plan 1 pins; it promotes `github.com/caddyserver/certmagic` v0.25.4 and `github.com/pion/turn/v5` v5.0.13 to direct use (task 16) and `github.com/livekit/livekit-server` v1.13.7 through the existing `internal/sfu` (task 16).
- Tooling, never as a `go.mod` `tool` directive: sqlc **v1.31.1**, golangci-lint **v2.13.2**, govulncheck **v1.8.0**, goose CLI `github.com/pressly/goose/v3/cmd/goose@v3.28.0`.
- **Never written:** Go MLS code, cgo in the release binary (`CGO_ENABLED=0`), an ORM, a router library, `tool` directives in `go.mod`, `oklog/ulid`.

**Wire and data invariants**
- Every `/v1` body is deterministic CBOR, fixed-position arrays, `Content-Type: application/cbor`; every error body is `[code(tstr), detail(tstr), retry_after_ms(uint|null)]` with `E_*` codes.
- Every protocol-visible id is 16 bytes from `crypto/rand` (`BLOB(16)`/`BYTEA`), with the one derived exception P2-D31 records for a 1:1 DM's `channel_id`. Timestamps are integer unix seconds.
- Every SQLite table is `STRICT`; no `DATETIME`, `TIMESTAMP` or `BOOLEAN` column type on either engine; every new table lands as a goose migration whose number is fixed by "Migration file numbers" below (`00004` upward), checked against a listing of `internal/store/sqlite/migrations/` first.
- Body caps: 64 KiB on every CBOR route except `POST /v1/groups/{id}/message` (`max_ciphertext_bytes + 4096`) and the two envelope routes `POST /v1/channels/{id}/messages` and `PATCH /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}` (96 KiB, P2-D28); `blobs.max_blob_bytes` on a blob `PUT`.
- Envelope limits enforced on the readable path: ≤ 4 attachments, `thumb` ≤ 8192, ≤ 2 previews, preview `image` ≤ 16384, `mime` ≤ 255, `url` ≤ 2048, `title` ≤ 256, `description` ≤ 1024.
- Permissions are resolved once, in one resolver, over roles and channel overwrites; nothing in this plan reads end-to-end-encrypted plaintext.

**Testing**
- TDD in every task: the failing test first, run it and record the failure, then the implementation, then the run that passes, then the commit.
- Tests assert behaviour: the store against real SQLite and real Postgres, search against both engines over one shared corpus, the blob store against a real directory, the DS seams against the real delivery service of Plan 1.
- The module-wide test command is pinned and is never narrowed: `/home/thim/.local/go/bin/go -C /home/thim/Repositories/dilla/.claude/worktrees/sdd-dilla-protocol test -race -shuffle=on -timeout 15m ./...`.
- CI is fail-closed: no `|| true`, no `continue-on-error`, no `if: always()` on a gate step; every `upload-artifact` sets `if-no-files-found: error`.

## Deviations from the spec (verified)

Two tables. **P2-rows** are this plan's own departures from the binding contract `interfaces.md`,
each with the vendored source, gap resolution or engine behaviour that forced it. **D-rows** are the
wave-wide departures from the spec and the protocol documents, repeated here from Plan 1 because
every task below is written against them.

### A. Deviations recorded by this plan

`interfaces.md` is the binding contract. Every deviation below is recorded with its reason; nothing
else in this file departs from it.

**Six of these are amendments to `interfaces.md` itself, applied to that file in the same revision
as this one and marked there with `AMENDED`**, because the contract as drafted could not be built:
**P2-D26** (§6.2's `ds.Session` carrying `auth.Scope` while §6.3 returns `ds.Session` is an import
cycle), **P2-D27** (§4.3's `envelope_json TEXT`/`JSONB` cannot hold deterministic CBOR),
**P2-D28** (§5.3's 64 KiB cap is below §2.8's own worst-case envelope), **P2-D32** (§4.3's nullable
`backups.device_id` inside a `PRIMARY KEY` is rejected by Postgres and unenforced by SQLite),
**P2-D7b** (§4.1 has no way to read a member's roles) and **P2-D25** (§1's package table omits
`internal/ops`, which §10 names). Every other row below is a deviation recorded here only.

| # | Contract text | What Plan 2 writes instead | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| P2-D1 | §4.3 `007_readable.sql` names no `channel_hex` column, while §4.4 A's FTS5 table indexes `channel_hex` "written by the trigger" | `readable_messages` gains a real column `channel_hex TEXT NOT NULL` on **both** engines, written in Go at insert time as the 32-char lowercase hex of `channel_id` | FTS5's external-content mechanism reads indexed columns back from the content table (`SELECT <content_rowid>, <cols> FROM <content> WHERE <content_rowid> = ?`, sqlite.org/fts5.html), so a column named only in the virtual table cannot be resolved and `snippet()` fails. gap-69 §2.2 verified exactly this shape end to end under `modernc.org/sqlite v1.59.0` (there named `channel_tok`). The contract's name `channel_hex` is kept. |
| P2-D2 | §4.3 `008_blobs.sql` declares both `blob_refs` and `attachments` with the same key | `attachments` is **not created** | No method of §4.1 `store.Blobs` reads or writes it, and `blob_refs(blob_id, channel_id, uploader_device, mime, created)` carries exactly the columns §4.1's `PutBlobRef` takes. gap-47 §10 names the reference table once. A second, never-written table would make `sqlc` emit a dead model and put dead DDL in both engines. |
| P2-D3 | §4.1 `store.MLS` has `GetGroup`, `ListOpenGroups`, no lookup by target | `store.MLS` gains `GroupsForTarget(ctx context.Context, targetID id.ID, kind uint8) ([]GroupRow, error)` | §4.3 already declares `INDEX mls_groups_by_target(target_id, kind)` and no declared method uses it. A SQLite `STRICT` table refuses a `[]byte` bound to a `TEXT` column ("cannot store BLOB value in TEXT column") and Postgres refuses non-JSON in `JSONB`, so the contract's spelling cannot hold the bytes the route receives. Task 8 |
| P2-D28 | §5.3 caps every CBOR route other than `POST /v1/groups/{id}/message` at 64 KiB through `http.MaxBytesReader` | `POST /v1/channels/{id}/messages` and `PATCH /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}` are capped at `maxEnvelopeBody = 96 * 1024` | §2.8's own worst-case envelope is ≈ 74 KiB (4 attachments at 32+32+12+8+255+8192 plus 2 previews at 2048+256+1024+16384 plus a 4 000-byte body plus CBOR heads), so a maximal but **legal** envelope would be refused with 413 before `ParseEnvelope` ever ran. 96 KiB is the next power-of-two-ish bucket above the worst case with room for the CBOR framing. Task 8 |
| P2-D29 | §4.4 B fixes the Postgres divergence as `ALTER MAPPING FOR hword, hword_part, word WITH unaccent, simple` | the token-type list is extended to `hword, hword_part, word, asciiword, asciihword, hword_asciipart, numword, numhword, hword_numpart, email, url, url_path, host, file, sfloat, float, int, uint, version` | the `simple` parser emits `asciiword`, not `word`, for unaccented ASCII, so §4.4's three types would leave most English text unmapped and the two engines would disagree on the shared corpus `TestSearchAgreesAcrossEngines` pins. The numeric types are included so a token that mixes digits and letters tokenises the same way on both engines. Task 8 |
| P2-D30 | §2.1's `/v1` status table is a **closed** vocabulary and has no row for `E_ENVELOPE_SHAPE`, `E_ENVELOPE_TYPE` or `E_ENVELOPE_LIMIT`; `protocol/04` defines them as *receiver* codes for a client decoding a decrypted envelope | four rows join §2.1's table — `400 E_ENVELOPE_SHAPE`, `400 E_ENVELOPE_TYPE`, `400 E_ENVELOPE_LIMIT` and **`403 E_CHANNEL_MODE`** ("the operation is not allowed for this channel's mode") — and **Plan 1 owns all four**: Plan 1 task 0 writes them into `protocol/02`'s 25-row `## Errors` table and Plan 1 task 6 declares `server.CodeEnvelopeShape`, `CodeEnvelopeType`, `CodeEnvelopeLimit` and `CodeChannelMode`. This plan consumes them; no task here amends the vocabulary | a server-readable channel's instance **is** a receiver in protocol/04's sense, so it refuses exactly what a client refuses and the client can switch on one vocabulary; the three envelope codes mirror the Rust envelope module's codes and the `protocol/vectors/` reject corpus one-for-one. `E_MODE_READABLE` keeps §2.1's meaning — "text groups are not allowed for this channel, because the channel is server-readable" — and is therefore **not** reused for the opposite condition; task 8's refusal of a plaintext upload to an end-to-end encrypted channel is `403 E_CHANNEL_MODE`, the plan's only call site for it. **An earlier draft of this row said `400`** while its task-8 call site said `403`; the controller resolved the contradiction to **403** on 2026-09-24 — the body is well formed and the session is authenticated, so the refusal is a permission, not a parse. Task 2's refusal of a `PATCH` that would make a visible channel end-to-end encrypted stays `400 E_INVALID_REQUEST`, which is what its own test asserts. Recorded in `protocol/02` by Plan 1 task 0 with `protocol/07` change-table rows there. Resolves P2-7 |
| P2-D31 | R24/D2: every protocol-visible id — user, device, group, community, **channel**, message, blob — is 16 bytes from `crypto/rand` | a **1:1** DM's `channel_id` is derived: the first 16 bytes of `SHA-256("dilla dm v1" \|\| lo \|\| hi)` over the two sorted user ids. A group DM keeps a random id | §5.2 requires "a DM between the same two users is idempotent and returns the existing channel", and `protocol/01` makes `dilla_binding.target_id` the `dm_id`, which both clients must agree on without a round trip. A random id plus a uniqueness index would need a second round trip before either client can register its group. The id is a **name**, not a capability: task 3's resolver gates `GET /v1/channels/{id}` on channel membership, so computing another pair's DM id reveals nothing. **Ordering constraint:** task 6 must not ship the DM routes before task 3's resolver gate is in `Channels.visible`, because until then `visible` skips the membership check for `CommunityID == nil` |
| P2-D32 | §4.3 `backups(... device_id ID?, ... PK(user_id, kind, device_id, chunk_seq))` and §4.5's nullable-id override list names `backups.device_id` | `device_id` is `NOT NULL` with the **all-zero id** meaning "not device-scoped", and `backups.device_id` is dropped from §4.5's `pointer: true` list | PostgreSQL implicitly forces every `PRIMARY KEY` column `NOT NULL`, so a `kind = 0` root backup — which has no device — could not be inserted at all on the Postgres twin; SQLite tolerates the NULL in a rowid table's PK and therefore does **not** enforce uniqueness, so duplicate root rows are accepted. The sentinel makes both engines agree and keeps the composite PK. Task 10 |
| P2-D21 | §4.3 `mls_app_messages` has no franking key column, while `reports.franking_key_id` does | `mls_app_messages` and `readable_messages` each gain `franking_key_id` (16-byte NOT NULL, all-zero default for rows written before the column) | `protocol/04` keeps old franking keys "for verification" after a yearly rotation; without recording which key franked a message, every message older than the rotation becomes unverifiable. Task 17 |
| P2-D33 | §4.6's migration numbering, and this plan's own earlier note that "Plan 1 lands `00001`–`00006`", from which it allocated `00007_structure.sql`–`00014_reports.sql` | **one sequence across both plans** (controller ruling 2026-09-24, on Plan 1's deviation **B19**): Plan 1 lands `00001_init.sql`, `00002_mls.sql`, `00003_messages.sql`, and this plan continues from `00004` in the order its tasks run — `00004_structure.sql` (1), `00005_channels.sql` (2), `00006_overwrites.sql` (3), `00007_bans.sql` (4), `00008_channel_members.sql` (6), `00009_readable.sql` (8), `00010_blobs.sql` (10), `00011_voice.sql` (16), `00012_reports.sql` (17) | Plan 1a task 3 concatenates schema files `001`–`003` and `009` into **one** `00001_init.sql`, so the assumed six were never three files short of anything — they were three files too many, and every Plan 2 number was two high. Task 16's voice migration had no concrete number at all in the draft; it has one now. Every filename, `goose` invocation, `git add` line and `ls` expectation in this file is renumbered to match; each schema task's step 1 keeps its directory listing as a guard, not an allocation. Resolves P2-3 |

### B. Wave-wide deviations from the spec and the protocol documents

Repeated from Plan 1 (`interfaces.md` §0.1); the task that writes each into a document is Plan 1
task 0 unless a row says otherwise.

| # | Document text | Correct value | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | spec L412 "`dillad` (Go **1.26**…)" | **Go 1.27.0** (`go.mod` already says `go 1.27.0`) | facts-auth §0, facts-go-ci §0 |
| D2 | spec L572 "one schema, SQLite or Postgres; **ULIDs**; UTC timestamps" | **16 CSPRNG bytes**, `BLOB(16)`/`BYTEA`; timestamps are **integer unix seconds**; `oklog/ulid` never enters `go.mod` | R24, gap-7 §0, gap-64 §0, gap-65 §0 |
| D3 | spec L577 `schema_migrations` | goose's **`goose_db_version`**, unrenamed | R37, gap-67 |
| D4 | spec L575 `mls_welcomes(device_id, group_id, blob, delivered_at)` | three tables — `mls_welcome_payloads`, `mls_epoch_trees`, `mls_welcomes` (§4.4 D) | gap-13 §6 |
| D5 | spec L575 `mls_handshakes(… kind proposal\|commit\|welcome …)` | `kind ∈ {0 proposal, 1 commit, 2 external_commit}`; a Welcome is **not** a handshake | R5, gap-17 §0 |
| D6 | `protocol/02` L21 "HTTP endpoints are **JSON** unless stated; binary MLS objects are base64url strings" | every `/v1` request and response body is **deterministic CBOR**, fixed-position arrays, `Content-Type: application/cbor`; MLS objects are `bstr`. **Confirmed by the controller 2026-09-24**: the whole `/v1` API body encoding is deterministic CBOR, and Plan 1 task 0 replaces the JSON sentence outright | R3, R4, §2.1 reason |
| D7 | `protocol/02` L114 `413 too_large` "default **64 KiB**" | **131072 bytes** (128 KiB) of MLS ciphertext | R6, R32, gap-90 |
| D8 | `protocol/04` "Limits" — no `mime`/`url`/`title`/`description` bound; 10 attachments; 5 previews; `image` ≤ 32 KiB | ≤ **4** attachments, `thumb` ≤ **8 KiB**, ≤ **2** previews, preview `image` ≤ **16 KiB**, `mime` ≤ **255**, `url` ≤ **2048**, `title` ≤ **256**, `description` ≤ **1024** | R6, R32, gap-91 |
| D9 | `protocol/02` "Gateway frames (CBOR arrays `[type, group_id, payload]`)" | **`[op(uint), n(uint), group_id(bstr16\|null), payload(array)]`**, uint opcodes, every payload a fixed-position array | R4, gap-2, gap-3, gap-36 |
| D10 | `protocol/02` `mls.welcome` payload `{group_id, blob}` | `group_id` dropped (it is frame element 2) | R4, gap-43 |
| D11 | `protocol/02` `mls.commit_needed` `{epoch, proposal_refs, deadline_ms}` | `deadline_ms` stays **relative**, re-based when the frame reaches the connection writer; no `00-overview` timestamp amendment | R31, gap-40 (supersedes R4's absolute `deadline_ts_ms`) |
| D12 | spec L466 / `02` invariant 11 — heal has no endpoints | `GET` + `POST /v1/groups/{id}/heal`; the tree comes from the DS's own restored `public_group_state`, not from the member, except on the `reseed` path | R8, gap-12 §0 |
| D13 | spec chaos list L627 "30-day inactivity Remove" | **90 days** (`protocol/01`, `config.rs INACTIVITY_REMOVE_DAYS`) | facts-ds-contract §7 |
| D14 | `02` invariant 10 "Application ciphertext is kept until every member cursor … or 30 days" | that is **delivery** retention; **archival** retention is the community policy (default indefinite) in `mls_app_messages.expires` (NULL = retained) | R28, gap-79 |
| D15 | spec L564 "backups hold ciphertext only" | "backups hold no end-to-end-encrypted plaintext: they contain ciphertext, server-readable channel content, revealed report envelopes, TLS material and the instance keys" | R38, gap-207 |
| D16 | `02` `201 {group_id, seq}` and `GET /info {…, seq}` | the field is **`next_seq`** (high-water + 1); the bare name `seq` always means "the number assigned to this event"; **one** `seq` space per group across handshakes *and* messages | gap-16 §0 |
| D17 | R11(b) `public_group_group_info_validate(handle, group_info)` | `VerifiableGroupInfo::signer()` is `pub(crate)`, so the export **must** take `signer_leaf`: `public_group_group_info_validate(handle, group_info, signer_leaf)`. **Accepted by the controller 2026-09-24** | gap-8 §1.3 |

| D20 | R18 "ids are ULIDs generated with `crypto/rand` entropy" | superseded by R24 (16 CSPRNG bytes); the rest of R18 stands | R24, stated in R24 itself |
| D21 | R1/R82 verb lists disagree (`invite`/`version` vs `admin …`) | eight names: `serve init migrate doctor version backup restore admin`; `invite` is `admin invite …`; `verify` is `backup verify` | gap-82 §1 |
| D22 | `02` errors table has no `404` and no `429` row | `E_NOT_FOUND` (404) and `E_RATE_LIMITED` (429) become rows | R3, gap-9 §0, gap-49 |
| D23 | spec L560 `turn.enabled` always on | TURN is **off by default** in `behind_proxy` and then only on a separate operator-configured TCP port | R39, gap-4 |
| D24 | `02` invariant 5 freeze applies to every external commit | `POST /v1/groups/{id}/resync` (own-leaf external commit) is **exempt**, with two guards | R25, gap-23 |

## The contract with Plan 1

The three sections below are this plan's half of the contract: the CBOR bodies it fixes for the
`§5.2` routes, the exact Plan 1 symbols every code block here is written against, and how migration
files are numbered against whatever Plan 1 actually landed.

### CBOR bodies this plan fixes for the §5.2 routes

§5.2 lists the Plan-2 paths and gives bodies for four of them. The rest are fixed here, in the §5.0
encoding (deterministic CBOR, fixed-position arrays, `Content-Type: application/cbor`), and each is
recorded in `protocol/09-http-api.md` by the task that introduces it.

| Route | Request | Response |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /v1/communities` | `[name(tstr), policy(bstr), min_account_age_seconds(uint), require_mod_2fa(uint)]` | `201 [community_id(bstr16), role_everyone(bstr16), policy_version(uint)]` |
| `GET /v1/communities/{id}` | — | `[community_id, owner, name, policy, policy_version, min_account_age_seconds, require_mod_2fa, created]` |
| `PATCH /v1/communities/{id}` | `[name(tstr\|null), policy(bstr\|null), min_account_age_seconds(uint\|null), require_mod_2fa(uint\|null)]` | `[policy_version(uint)]` |
| `DELETE /v1/communities/{id}` | — | `204` |
| `GET /v1/communities/{id}/members` | — | `[[user_id, joined, nick, [role_id]]]` |
| `DELETE /v1/communities/{id}/members/{user_id}` | — | `204` |
| `POST /v1/communities/{id}/join` | `[invite(tstr\|null)]` | `[community_id]` |
| `POST /v1/communities/{id}/leave` | `[]` | `204` |
| `POST /v1/communities/{id}/channels` | `[kind(uint), mode(uint), visibility(uint), parent_id(bstr16\|null), name(tstr), topic(tstr), position(uint), slowmode_seconds(uint)]` | `201 [channel_id, mode, visibility]` |
| `GET /v1/channels/{id}` | — | `[channel_id, community_id\|null, kind, mode, visibility, parent_id\|null, name, topic, position, slowmode_seconds, seq]` |
| `PATCH /v1/channels/{id}` | `[name(tstr\|null), topic(tstr\|null), mode(uint\|null), visibility(uint\|null), parent_id(bstr16\|null), position(uint\|null), slowmode_seconds(uint\|null)]` | `204` |
| `DELETE /v1/channels/{id}` | — | `204` |
| `PUT /v1/channels/{id}/overwrites/{kind}/{target_id}` | `[allow(uint), deny(uint)]` | `204` |
| `GET /v1/channels/{id}/members` | — | `[[user_id]]` |
| `PUT /v1/channels/{id}/members/{user_id}` | `[]` | `204` |
| `DELETE /v1/channels/{id}/members/{user_id}` | — | `204` |
| `POST /v1/communities/{id}/roles` | `[name(tstr), color(uint), position(uint), allow(uint), deny(uint), hoist(uint), mentionable(uint)]` | `201 [role_id]` |
| `PATCH /v1/roles/{id}` | `[name(tstr\|null), color(uint\|null), position(uint\|null), allow(uint\|null), deny(uint\|null), hoist(uint\|null), mentionable(uint\|null)]` | `204` |
| `PUT /v1/communities/{id}/bans/{user_id}` | `[reason(tstr), expires(uint\|null)]` | `204` |
| `GET /v1/communities/{id}/bans` | — | `[[user_id, reason, by_user, created, expires\|null]]` |
| `POST /v1/communities/{id}/invites` | `[max_uses(uint), ttl_seconds(uint), grants_admin(uint)]` | `201 [invite_id, code(tstr), url(tstr), expires(uint)]` |
| `GET /v1/communities/{id}/invites` | — | `[[invite_id, community_id\|null, created_by\|null, grants_admin, max_uses, used_count, created, expires_at, revoked_at\|null]]` |
| `GET /v1/dms` | — | `[[channel_id, kind, [user_id]]]` |
| `GET /v1/channels/{id}/messages?from=&limit=` | — | `[[seq, sender, envelope(bstr), franking_tag(bstr32), created, edited\|null, deleted\|null]]` |
| `PATCH /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}` | `[envelope(bstr)]` | `204` |
| `DELETE /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}` | — | `204` |
| `GET /v1/channels/{id}/search?q=&limit=&before=` | — | `[[channel_id, seq, sender, snippet(tstr), score_micros(uint), created]]` — `score` travels as `round(score × 1e6)` because §2.1's deterministic-CBOR subset carries no floats |
| `PUT /v1/channels/{id}/read-state` | `[last_read_seq(uint)]` | `204` |
| `DELETE /v1/admin/blobs/{blob_id}` | `[reason(tstr)]` | `204` |
| `GET /v1/admin/audit?since=&limit=` | — | `[[actor\|null, action(tstr), target(tstr), detail(tstr), at]]` |
| `POST /v1/admin/users/{id}/disable` | `[disabled(uint)]` | `204` |
| `POST /v1/channels/{id}/calls` | `[]` | `201 [call_id, livekit_room(tstr), token(tstr), url(tstr)]` |
| `DELETE /v1/calls/{call_id}` | — | `204` |

**The canonical spelling of an edit and a delete on a readable channel** is the seq-scoped verb:
`PATCH /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}` with `[envelope(bstr)]`, and
`DELETE /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}`. A type-1 or type-2 envelope posted to
`POST /v1/channels/{id}/messages` is accepted as an **alias** — the client that already builds
envelopes need not learn a second shape — and is routed to the same `applyEdit`/`applyDelete`,
resolving its target through `reply_to` (P2-D15) instead of through the path. Both spellings answer
`204`. Task 8 registers all three routes; task 9 writes the handlers.

### Symbols this plan consumes from Plan 1

These are **not** deviations — the names are the contract's (§5.1 task 6, §6.3 task 12, §6.0). The
signatures below are the ones **Plan 1a actually writes**; every Plan 2 code block in this file is
written against them. The one exception is `auth.SessionFrom`/`auth.WithSession`, which Plan 2 adds
to `internal/auth/session.go` in task 1 (**P2-D26**). Carried again under "Needs verification" as
P2-1.

```go
// internal/server — Plan 1a task 6
type Mux struct{ ... }
func NewMux() *Mux
func (m *Mux) Handle(pattern string, h http.Handler)
func (m *Mux) HandleFunc(pattern string, h http.HandlerFunc)
func (m *Mux) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request)
type Code struct{ ... }                                       // §2.1's closed vocabulary; carries its own status
type Error struct{ ... }
func (e *Error) Error() string
func (e *Error) Status() int
func Errorf(code Code, format string, args ...any) *Error      // NOT (status int, code string, …)
func WriteError(w http.ResponseWriter, err error)              // TWO arguments, no *http.Request
func DecodeBody(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, max int64, v any) error  // FOUR arguments
func EncodeBody(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, v any) error
func PathID(r *http.Request, name string) (id.ID, error)       // 32 lowercase hex
func PathDigest(r *http.Request, name string) ([]byte, error)  // 64 lowercase hex — P2-D4
type RateLimiter struct{ ... }
func NewRateLimiter(c config.Rate, clk clock.Clock) *RateLimiter
func (l *RateLimiter) Allow(class Class, key string) (bool, time.Duration)  // reads the WALL clock
// internal/auth — Plan 1a task 12
type Session struct{ UserID, DeviceID id.ID; Scope Scope; TokenHash []byte; Expires, IdleExpires int64 }
func FromContext(ctx context.Context) (Session, bool)          // set by (*Sessions).Middleware
func TokenHash(token string) []byte                            // SHA-256; see P2-10
// internal/store/sqlite — Plan 1a task 3 and §4.6
func New(write, read *sql.DB, clk clock.Clock) store.Repository
func Migrate(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB) error            // goose Up over the embedded FS
func MigrationVersion(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB) (int64, error)
// internal/store/postgres — the same three, over *sql.DB opened with sql.Open("pgx", dsn)
// internal/store — Plan 1a task 3
func VacuumInto(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB, path string) error
func DumpPostgres(ctx context.Context, dsn string, w io.Writer) error   // takes a DSN — see P2-D33
```

**Every code block in this file uses those spellings.** In particular: route registration is
`mux.HandleFunc(pattern, handler)`; an error is `server.Errorf(server.CodeForbidden, "…")` where the
`Code` value carries the status, so no HTTP status is passed at the call site; `server.WriteError(w,
err)` takes two arguments; and a request body is decoded with `server.DecodeBody(w, r, max, &req)`,
where `max` is the route's own cap from `interfaces.md` §5.3, passed as a number: **64 KiB
(`64 * 1024`) on every CBOR route**, `maxEnvelopeBody = 96 * 1024` on
`POST /v1/channels/{id}/messages` and `PATCH /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}` (P2-D28), and
`max_ciphertext_bytes + 4096` on `POST /v1/groups/{id}/message` (Plan 1's route, not this plan's).
**There is no `server.MaxBody` constant**: Plan 1 task 6 produces the four-argument `DecodeBody`
helper and nothing else, and Plan 1's own handlers carry the cap as a `MaxBody int64` struct field
fed from `config.Limits.MaxBodyBytes`. Where a block below still
shows a status argument or a three-argument `WriteError`, the Plan 1a spelling wins and the worker
adjusts the call site — that is the whole content of P2-1, and it never changes behaviour.

**What Plan 1a does and does not ship.** Plan 1a task 3 declares `store.Structure`, `store.Readable`
and `store.Blobs` in `internal/store/store.go` and creates `internal/store/rows.go`. It writes **no
stub methods**: per Plan 1a's own deviation **ID1**, `store.Repository` simply does not embed
`Structure; Readable; Blobs`, and per its note in task 3 step 3 the row types for tables 1a does not
create are "declared by the task that ships their table". So every Plan 2 task that implements one
of those interfaces **adds** the methods, **adds** the row types to `internal/store/rows.go`
(**P2-D24**), and **adds** the interface to `Repository`'s embed list (**P2-D23**) — it does not
replace a stub, and the expected failure at each task's first run is a **build error**, not
`errors.ErrUnsupported`.

### Migration file numbers

§4.6 fixes `NNNNN_snake_case.sql` with a zero-padded five-digit sequence. **One sequence spans both
plans, and it is settled** (controller ruling 2026-09-24, on Plan 1's deviation **B19**).

**Plan 1 lands three migration files, not six:** Plan 1a task 3 concatenates schema files `001`–`003`
and `009` into a single `00001_init.sql`, Plan 1b task 19 lands `00002_mls.sql` and its task 23
lands `00003_messages.sql`. The listing Plan 2 starts from is therefore
`00001_init.sql  00002_mls.sql  00003_messages.sql` — three files, no gaps — and **Plan 2 allocates
from `00004` upward, in the order its tasks run**:

| file | task | depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `00004_structure.sql` | 1 | `users` (`00001_init.sql`) |
| `00005_channels.sql` | 2 | `communities` |
| `00006_overwrites.sql` | 3 | `channels` |
| `00007_bans.sql` | 4 | `communities`, `users` |
| `00008_channel_members.sql` | 6 | `channels`, `users` |
| `00009_readable.sql` | 8 | `channels` |
| `00010_blobs.sql` | 10 | `channels`, `users` |
| `00011_voice.sql` | 16 | `channels`, `mls_groups` (`00002_mls.sql`) |
| `00012_reports.sql` | 17 | `mls_app_messages` (`00003_messages.sql`), `readable_messages` |

Every concrete filename, `goose` invocation, `git add` line and `ls` expectation in this file uses
those numbers. They are no longer relative: an earlier draft wrote them as *N*+1 … *N*+9 against an
assumed *N* = 5 and then spelled them `00007`–`00014`, two higher than the directory will ever show.

**Task 1's step 1 still lists the directory first, and every later task still asserts that its own
predecessors are present and its own number free.** That check is now a guard against a concurrent
branch, not an allocation: if the listing does not read `00001_init.sql  00002_mls.sql
00003_messages.sql`, the executor stops and reconciles with Plan 1 rather than silently shifting a
number, because a migration renumbered after it has been applied anywhere is a migration that runs
twice.

**The order is in dependency order, and that is not cosmetic:** goose applies migrations in numeric
order, so `readable_messages`, which carries `REFERENCES channels(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, must be
numbered above the `channels` migration.

## Rulings that bind this plan

The controller's 39 rulings (`rulings.md`, 2026-09-24) settle every open design decision in this
wave. One row per ruling, with where it lands here. Rulings whose work is Plan 1's are marked so and
are listed because this plan consumes their result and must not contradict it. Where a ruling and a
verified fact disagree about an API, a version or a number, **the fact wins** and the deviation is
recorded in the tables above.

| R | What it settles | Where it lands in this plan |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Two plans on branch `sdd-dillad`, one PR each | This file is dillad-2; tasks 1–18 |
| R2 | Package layout | `internal/api/{communities,channels,roles,bans,dms,readable,blobs,reports,admin}.go`, `internal/blob/{store,gc}.go`, `internal/ops/{archive,lock,doctor}.go`, `internal/server/{tls,demux,turn}.go`, `cmd/dillad/{backup,restore,admin}.go` — see "File structure" |
| R3 | One `E_*` vocabulary and the fixed-position CBOR error body | Every handler in tasks 1–18; `E_ENVELOPE_SHAPE`, `E_ENVELOPE_TYPE`, `E_ENVELOPE_LIMIT` (400) and `E_CHANNEL_MODE` (403) are in the table and in `server.Code*` before this plan starts — **Plan 1 tasks 0 and 6** declare them (P2-D30); **task 9** and task 8 here only use them |
| R4 | The four-element gateway frame and its uint opcodes | Consumed: task 8 produces `message.plain` (op 32) payloads through `gateway.DeliverGroup`; task 13 re-frames every response with the new generation |
| R5 | Handshake `kind` and `sender` | Consumed by task 7's batched Adds and task 4's kick-while-offline scenario. Plan 1 tasks 0, 19–20 |
| R6 | 128 KiB ciphertext cap and the tightened `protocol/04` limits | Enforced receiver-side by **task 9**'s envelope validator and by task 8's 96 KiB body cap (P2-D28); the numbers are `mime` 255 per **R32** |
| R7 | Device sessions, bearer tokens, the 8-session cap | Consumed by every route; **task 14**'s `admin user disable` revokes them through `auth.TokenHash` (P2-10) |
| R8 | Heal endpoints and `GET /v1/instance`'s `generation` | **Task 13** drives them: after a restore every group is epoch-unknown until a member heals it, and the happy and unhappy paths are both scenarios |
| R9 | `group_id` is device-chosen; `binding.target_id` is the channel/DM id; call groups are keyed by `voice_sessions.call_id` | **Tasks 2, 6, 7** (the channel/DM ids a binding names) and **task 16** (`voice_sessions` and the call group) |
| R10 | "Online" = an authenticated gateway session with a heartbeat < 90 s old | Consumed by **task 4**'s "kick while offline freezes the group" scenario and by task 16's call presence |
| R11 | The four additive wasi ABI v2 exports | Consumed, not extended. Plan 1 tasks 14–16 |
| R12 | SQL is the record; the `PublicGroup` blob is persisted per accepted handshake | Consumed. **Task 13** restores it and marks every group epoch-unknown |
| R13 | Sequencing exactly as invariants 1–11, each with a Go test **and** a testkit scenario | **Tasks 4 and 7** add the two scenarios this plan owns (kick-while-offline, the 256-batched join storm); the invariants themselves are Plan 1 tasks 20–27 |
| R14 | Testkit remote mode and the Go harness | **Tasks 4, 7, 13** write scenario files into the harness Plan 1 task 29 built |
| R15 | Argon2id, TOTP, passkeys, OIDC, login throttling | Consumed. **Task 1** stores `require_mod_2fa` in the community policy and **task 14** enforces it for the admin CLI |
| R16 | ASCII handles, free-Unicode display names | Consumed by **task 1**'s member nicknames, which take the display-name rules, not the handle rules |
| R17 | Join gates: `min_account_age_seconds` (default 0), invite-required registration, a stored-not-enforced screening flag, `users.age_bracket` stored and unused | **Task 1** stores the policy; **task 5** enforces the gate on `POST /v1/communities/{id}/join` |
| R18 | Two sqlc packages behind one `store.Repository`, two schema directories, goose, the pools | Every schema task: **1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 16, 17** |
| R19 | stdlib mux, coder/websocket, certmagic with appended `NextProtos` and `DisableHTTPChallenge`, `behind_proxy` as dillad's own mode, the peeking TURN demux | **Task 16** in full (`internal/server/tls.go`, `demux.go`, `turn.go`) |
| R20 | Strict TOML, `slog` with redaction, systemd `Type=notify` without `MemoryDenyWriteExecute`, the distroless image from a `$BUILDPLATFORM` builder, `prometheus/client_golang` v1.24.1 | **Task 15** (`dillad doctor`), **task 18** (Dockerfile, `deploy/dilla.service`, Compose, the Proxmox helper) |
| R21 | The CI gates, pinned tool versions, `if-no-files-found: error` | **Task 18** extends `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with the image build; the gates themselves are Plan 1 task 30 |
| R22 | Commit conventions, fail-closed CI, one PR per plan | "Global Constraints" above and the commit step of every task |
| R23 | `protocol/07-versioning.md`'s governance step | Consumed; **tasks 9 and 17** each add a change-table row under it |
| R24 | Every protocol-visible id is 16 CSPRNG bytes | Every schema task. The one exception is **P2-D31**: a 1:1 DM's `channel_id` is the first 16 bytes of `SHA-256("dilla dm v1" \|\| lo \|\| hi)`, because both clients must agree on `binding.target_id` without a round trip (**task 6**) |
| R25 | Resync is exempt from the freeze, with two guards | Consumed. Plan 1 task 25 |
| R26 | The W5-freeze parameters, `freeze_max` 30 days | Consumed. Plan 1 task 21 |
| R27 | `abi_version = 2` | Consumed. Plan 1 tasks 14–16 |
| R28 | Delivery retention vs archival retention (community policy, default indefinite) | **Task 1** stores the community policy; **task 11** expires unreferenced blobs by it; readable-message retention rides the same policy |
| R29 | Delete-for-everyone is uploader-only at v1; moderator deletion needs a signed moderation event | **Tasks 9 and 11** (`E_NOT_UPLOADER`); the moderation event is follow-up card 2 |
| R30 | `message.ct` fans out to the uploader too; the upload response returns `seq` | Consumed; **task 8** does the same for `message.plain` on readable channels |
| R31 | `commit_needed`'s deadline stays relative | Consumed. Plan 1 tasks 17 and 22 |
| R32 | 131072-byte cap; `mime` 255 bytes | **Task 9**'s validator and **task 10**'s attachment metadata |
| R33 | Invalid path id → `400 E_INVALID_REQUEST`; unknown → `404 E_NOT_FOUND` | Every handler, through `server.PathID` and `server.PathDigest` (P2-D4) |
| R34 | Attachment deletion is the uploader's own `DELETE /v1/blobs/{blob_id}`; blob rows carry `uploader_device` and `channel_id`; unreferenced blobs expire by the retention policy | **Tasks 10 and 11** in full |
| R35 | `dillad init` creates and migrates; `serve` migrates after the pre-migration backup | Consumed; **task 13**'s restore takes the same pre-migration backup path |
| R36 | A deleted account keeps its handle reserved | Consumed; **task 14**'s `admin user disable`/delete honours the tombstone |
| R37 | goose keeps `goose_db_version` | Every migration task; each one lists the directory first and checks its own fixed number from "Migration file numbers" (`00004`–`00012`, P2-D33) is free |
| R38 | "backups hold no end-to-end-encrypted plaintext: they contain ciphertext, server-readable channel content, revealed report envelopes, TLS material and the instance keys" | **Tasks 12, 13 and 18** — the archive's own README, the restore output and the operator documentation all use this wording |
| R39 | The exact Postgres CI image and `POSTGRES_INITDB_ARGS`; TURN off by default and only on a separate operator-configured TCP port; the SQLite DSN keeps `url.PathEscape` | **Task 16** (the TURN listener and `behind_proxy`), **task 18** (the image), consumed elsewhere |

## File structure

Every path this wave creates, with the plan and part that owns it. Paths marked *(exists)* are in
the tree at `a0a63fc`; paths marked "1a"/"1b" are Plan 1's and are extended here only where a task
below says so.

Module `github.com/jonasthim/dilla`, `go 1.27.0`. Existing packages `internal/cborx`,
`internal/mlswasi`, `internal/sfu`, `internal/store/sqlite`, `internal/deps` are **reused**, not
replaced. Every path below is new unless marked *(exists)*.

**Never**: no Go MLS code, no cgo in the release binary, no router library, no ORM, no `tool`

facts-http-gateway §4.1).

## Tasks

Eighteen tasks, numbered 1–18, in dependency order: the community and channel structure first
(1–2), then the permission resolver every later route calls (3), moderation (4), joining (5), the
three channel shapes that need the delivery service (6–7), the server-readable path and its search
(8–9), attachments (10–11), the operational half (12–15), the network edge (16), reports (17) and
packaging (18).

Each task ends with an independently testable deliverable and one or a few green commits. Every
command below is runnable as written on the dev box. Every schema task's first step lists
`internal/store/sqlite/migrations/` and checks that its predecessors are there; the numbers
themselves are fixed, `00004` upward, by the table in this file's header.

### Task 1: Communities, members and community policy

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/store/sqlite/schema/006_structure.sql`
- Create: `internal/store/postgres/schema/006_structure.sql`
- Create: `internal/store/sqlite/migrations/00004_structure.sql`
- Create: `internal/store/postgres/migrations/00004_structure.sql`
- Create: `internal/store/sqlite/queries/structure.sql`
- Create: `internal/store/postgres/queries/structure.sql`
- Modify: `internal/store/store.go` (add `Structure` to `Repository`'s embed list — P2-D23; add `ListMemberRoles`, `GetMember`, `UpdateCommunityMeta`, `SoftDeleteCommunity` to `Structure`)
- Modify: `internal/store/rows.go` (declare `CommunityRow`, `MemberOfCommunityRow`, `RoleRow` — P2-D24)
- Modify: `internal/store/sqlite/repo.go` (implement the `Structure` community methods)
- Modify: `internal/store/postgres/repo.go` (implement the `Structure` community methods)
- Modify: `internal/auth/session.go` (add the exported `SessionFrom` and `WithSession` — P2-D26)
- Create: `internal/api/communities.go`
- Create: `internal/api/perm.go` (the 24-bit permission vocabulary; task 3 adds the resolver)
- Create: `internal/api/roles.go` (`requireSecondFactor` and the role-grant route only)
- Create: `internal/api/apitest_test.go` (the shared `internal/api` test harness)
- Test: `internal/api/communities_test.go`
- Test: `internal/store/structure_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `store.Repository` and its `Structure` sub-interface as §4.1 declares it, plus the four
  methods **P2-D7** and **P2-D7b** add. The row types `store.CommunityRow`,
  `store.MemberOfCommunityRow` and `store.RoleRow` are **written by this task** into
  `internal/store/rows.go` (**P2-D24**), and `Structure` is added to `Repository`'s embed list
  (**P2-D23**);
  `store.ErrNotFound`, `store.ErrConflict`; `sqlite.New`, `sqlite.Migrate`, `sqlite.OpenWrite`,
  `sqlite.OpenRead`; `server.Mux`, `server.DecodeBody`, `server.EncodeBody`, `server.Errorf`,
  `server.WriteError`, `server.PathID` **in the Plan 1a spellings listed in this file's header**;
  `auth.FromContext`, `auth.Session`; `id.ID`; `clock.Clock`.
- Produces:
  ```go
  // internal/api
  type Communities struct{ ... }
  func NewCommunities(repo store.Repository, clk clock.Clock, log *slog.Logger) *Communities
  func (c *Communities) Register(mux *server.Mux)
  // the six §5.2 community routes and the CBOR bodies of the table at the top of this file
  ```
  ```go
  type Roles struct{ ... }
  func NewRoles(repo store.Repository, clk clock.Clock, rpID string, log *slog.Logger) *Roles
  func (h *Roles) Register(mux *server.Mux)   // only PUT .../members/{user_id}/roles/{role_id} here
  type Bits uint64
  const ( PermViewChannel Bits = 1 << 0; … PermManageNicknames Bits = 1 << 23 )
  // internal/auth
  func SessionFrom(ctx context.Context) (Session, bool)
  func WithSession(ctx context.Context, s Session) context.Context
  ```
  the `communities`, `members`, `roles` and `member_roles` tables on both engines; `policy_version`,
  `min_account_age_seconds` and `require_mod_2fa`; the `@everyone` role, whose **id** — not its
  position — task 3's resolver reads as the permission base.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing store test**

Create `internal/store/structure_test.go`:

```go
package store_test

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"testing"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/id"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/store"
)

// newRepos returns one repository per engine that is available. SQLite is always
// present; Postgres is present only when DILLA_TEST_PG names a DSN. Plan 1a task 3
// defines this helper in internal/store/repo_test.go; the map key is the engine name.
func TestCommunityRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	for engine, repo := range newRepos(t) {
		t.Run(engine, func(t *testing.T) {
			ctx := context.Background()
			owner := id.New()
			if err := repo.CreateUser(ctx, newTestUser(owner, "owner")); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
			}

			cid := id.New()
			in := store.CommunityRow{
				ID:                   cid,
				Owner:                owner,
				Name:                 "Kryptering",
				IconBlob:             nil,
				PolicyJSON:           []byte(`{"retention_days":0}`),
				PolicyVersion:        1,
				MinAccountAgeSeconds: 0,
				RequireMod2FA:        0,
				Created:              1000,
			}
			if err := repo.CreateCommunity(ctx, in); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("CreateCommunity: %v", err)
			}

			got, err := repo.GetCommunity(ctx, cid)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("GetCommunity: %v", err)
			}
			if got.Name != "Kryptering" || got.Owner != owner || got.PolicyVersion != 1 {
				t.Fatalf("GetCommunity = %+v", got)
			}
			if string(got.PolicyJSON) != `{"retention_days":0}` {
				t.Fatalf("PolicyJSON = %q", got.PolicyJSON)
			}

			if err := repo.UpdateCommunityPolicy(ctx, cid, []byte(`{"retention_days":30}`), 2); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("UpdateCommunityPolicy: %v", err)
			}
			got, err = repo.GetCommunity(ctx, cid)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("GetCommunity after update: %v", err)
			}
			if got.PolicyVersion != 2 || string(got.PolicyJSON) != `{"retention_days":30}` {
				t.Fatalf("after update = %+v", got)
			}

			if _, err := repo.GetCommunity(ctx, id.New()); !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
				t.Fatalf("GetCommunity(unknown) = %v, want ErrNotFound", err)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestCommunityMembers(t *testing.T) {
	for engine, repo := range newRepos(t) {
		t.Run(engine, func(t *testing.T) {
			ctx := context.Background()
			cid, owner := id.New(), id.New()
			if err := repo.CreateUser(ctx, newTestUser(owner, "owner")); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
			}
			if err := repo.CreateCommunity(ctx, store.CommunityRow{
				ID: cid, Owner: owner, Name: "c", PolicyJSON: []byte(`{}`),
				PolicyVersion: 1, Created: 1,
			}); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("CreateCommunity: %v", err)
			}

			users := make([]id.ID, 3)
			for i := range users {
				users[i] = id.New()
				if err := repo.CreateUser(ctx, newTestUser(users[i], "u"+string(rune('a'+i)))); err != nil {
					t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
				}
				if err := repo.PutMember(ctx, store.MemberOfCommunityRow{
					CommunityID: cid, UserID: users[i], Joined: int64(100 + i), Nick: "",
				}); err != nil {
					t.Fatalf("PutMember: %v", err)
				}
			}

			// PutMember is an upsert: a second call updates the nick and adds no row.
			if err := repo.PutMember(ctx, store.MemberOfCommunityRow{
				CommunityID: cid, UserID: users[0], Joined: 100, Nick: "nick",
			}); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("PutMember upsert: %v", err)
			}

			var all []store.MemberOfCommunityRow
			var after id.ID
			for {
				page, err := repo.ListMembersOfCommunity(ctx, cid, after, 2)
				if err != nil {
					t.Fatalf("ListMembersOfCommunity: %v", err)
				}
				if len(page) == 0 {
					break
				}
				all = append(all, page...)
				after = page[len(page)-1].UserID
			}
			if len(all) != 3 {
				t.Fatalf("members = %d, want 3", len(all))
			}
			for i := 1; i < len(all); i++ {
				if string(all[i-1].UserID[:]) >= string(all[i].UserID[:]) {
					t.Fatalf("members not ordered by user_id: %x then %x", all[i-1].UserID, all[i].UserID)
				}
			}

			if err := repo.DeleteMember(ctx, cid, users[1]); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("DeleteMember: %v", err)
			}
			rest, err := repo.ListMembersOfCommunity(ctx, cid, id.ID{}, 10)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("ListMembersOfCommunity: %v", err)
			}
			if len(rest) != 2 {
				t.Fatalf("after delete = %d, want 2", len(rest))
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestRolesPerCommunity(t *testing.T) {
	for engine, repo := range newRepos(t) {
		t.Run(engine, func(t *testing.T) {
			ctx := context.Background()
			cid, owner := id.New(), id.New()
			if err := repo.CreateUser(ctx, newTestUser(owner, "owner")); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
			}
			if err := repo.CreateCommunity(ctx, store.CommunityRow{
				ID: cid, Owner: owner, Name: "c", PolicyJSON: []byte(`{}`),
				PolicyVersion: 1, Created: 1,
			}); err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("CreateCommunity: %v", err)
			}
			everyone := store.RoleRow{
				ID: id.New(), CommunityID: cid, Name: "@everyone",
				Position: 0, Allow: 0x401c3, Deny: 0, Created: 1,
			}
			mod := store.RoleRow{
				ID: id.New(), CommunityID: cid, Name: "mod",
				Position: 10, Allow: 0x4, Deny: 0, Hoist: 1, Created: 2,
			}
			for _, r := range []store.RoleRow{mod, everyone} {
				if err := repo.PutRole(ctx, r); err != nil {
					t.Fatalf("PutRole: %v", err)
				}
			}
			got, err := repo.ListRoles(ctx, cid)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("ListRoles: %v", err)
			}
			if len(got) != 2 {
				t.Fatalf("ListRoles = %d rows, want 2", len(got))
			}
			if got[0].Position != 0 || got[1].Position != 10 {
				t.Fatalf("ListRoles is not ordered by position: %+v", got)
			}
			if got[0].Allow != 0x401c3 {
				t.Fatalf("@everyone allow = %#x", got[0].Allow)
			}
		})
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the SQLite schema**

Create `internal/store/sqlite/schema/006_structure.sql`:

```sql
-- 006_structure.sql — communities, members, roles, channels, overwrites, bans, voice.
-- Shorthand expanded per interfaces.md §4.3: ID -> BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(x) = 16),
-- TS/U -> INTEGER NOT NULL, every table STRICT (gap-66).
CREATE TABLE communities (
  id                      BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(id) = 16) PRIMARY KEY,
  owner                   BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(owner) = 16),
  name                    TEXT    NOT NULL,
  icon_blob               BLOB,
  policy_json             TEXT    NOT NULL,
  policy_version          INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
  min_account_age_seconds INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  require_mod_2fa         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (require_mod_2fa IN (0, 1)),
  created                 INTEGER NOT NULL,
  deleted_at              INTEGER
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE members (
  community_id BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(community_id) = 16)
                       REFERENCES communities(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  user_id      BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(user_id) = 16)
                       REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  joined       INTEGER NOT NULL,
  nick         TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  PRIMARY KEY (community_id, user_id)
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE roles (
  id           BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(id) = 16) PRIMARY KEY,
  community_id BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(community_id) = 16)
                       REFERENCES communities(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  name         TEXT    NOT NULL,
  color        INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  position     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  allow        INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  deny         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  hoist        INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (hoist IN (0, 1)),
  mentionable  INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (mentionable IN (0, 1)),
  created      INTEGER NOT NULL
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX roles_by_community ON roles(community_id, position);

CREATE TABLE member_roles (
  community_id BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(community_id) = 16),
  user_id      BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(user_id) = 16),
  role_id      BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(role_id) = 16)
                    REFERENCES roles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  PRIMARY KEY (community_id, user_id, role_id),
  FOREIGN KEY (community_id, user_id) REFERENCES members(community_id, user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE
) STRICT;
```

Tasks 2, 3, 4 and 6 append `channels`, `channel_overwrites`, `channel_members`, `bans` and
`voice_sessions` to this same file and to the same migration's successor files; each is a separate
migration so that a half-landed plan still migrates cleanly.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the Postgres schema**

Create `internal/store/postgres/schema/006_structure.sql`:

```sql
-- 006_structure.sql — the SQLite file with interfaces.md §4.2's substitutions:
-- BLOB -> BYTEA with octet_length, INTEGER -> BIGINT, enums -> SMALLINT, JSON policy -> JSONB,
-- no STRICT suffix.
CREATE TABLE communities (
  id                      BYTEA    NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(id) = 16) PRIMARY KEY,
  owner                   BYTEA    NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(owner) = 16),
  name                    TEXT     NOT NULL,
  icon_blob               BYTEA,
  policy_json             JSONB    NOT NULL,
  policy_version          BIGINT   NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
  min_account_age_seconds BIGINT   NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  require_mod_2fa         SMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (require_mod_2fa IN (0, 1)),
  created                 BIGINT   NOT NULL,
  deleted_at              BIGINT
);

CREATE TABLE members (
  community_id BYTEA  NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(community_id) = 16)
                      REFERENCES communities(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  user_id      BYTEA  NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(user_id) = 16)
                      REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  joined       BIGINT NOT NULL,
  nick         TEXT   NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  PRIMARY KEY (community_id, user_id)
);

CREATE TABLE roles (
  id           BYTEA    NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(id) = 16) PRIMARY KEY,
  community_id BYTEA    NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(community_id) = 16)
                        REFERENCES communities(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  name         TEXT     NOT NULL,
  color        BIGINT   NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  position     BIGINT   NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  allow        BIGINT   NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  deny         BIGINT   NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  hoist        SMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (hoist IN (0, 1)),
  mentionable  SMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (mentionable IN (0, 1)),
  created      BIGINT   NOT NULL
);
CREATE INDEX roles_by_community ON roles(community_id, position);

CREATE TABLE member_roles (
  community_id BYTEA NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(community_id) = 16),
  user_id      BYTEA NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(user_id) = 16),
  role_id      BYTEA NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(role_id) = 16)
                     REFERENCES roles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  PRIMARY KEY (community_id, user_id, role_id),
  FOREIGN KEY (community_id, user_id) REFERENCES members(community_id, user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);
```

> `allow` and `deny` are `BIGINT`, i.e. **signed** 64-bit on Postgres, while the Go field is
> `uint64`. Task 3's bit vocabulary therefore stops at bit 62 and the plan asserts it
> (`TestPermissionBitsFitSigned64`): a 64th bit would round-trip as a negative number.

- [ ] **Step 7: Write the sqlc query files**

Create `internal/store/sqlite/queries/structure.sql`:

```sql
-- name: CreateCommunity :exec
INSERT INTO communities (id, owner, name, icon_blob, policy_json, policy_version,
                         min_account_age_seconds, require_mod_2fa, created, deleted_at)
VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?);

-- name: GetCommunity :one
SELECT id, owner, name, icon_blob, policy_json, policy_version,
       min_account_age_seconds, require_mod_2fa, created, deleted_at
FROM communities
WHERE id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL;

-- name: UpdateCommunityPolicy :execrows
UPDATE communities SET policy_json = ?, policy_version = ?
WHERE id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL;

-- name: UpdateCommunityMeta :execrows
UPDATE communities
SET name = ?, min_account_age_seconds = ?, require_mod_2fa = ?
WHERE id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL;

-- name: SoftDeleteCommunity :execrows
UPDATE communities SET deleted_at = ? WHERE id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL;

-- name: PutMember :exec
INSERT INTO members (community_id, user_id, joined, nick)
VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)
ON CONFLICT (community_id, user_id) DO UPDATE SET nick = excluded.nick;

-- name: DeleteMember :execrows
DELETE FROM members WHERE community_id = ? AND user_id = ?;

-- name: GetMember :one
SELECT community_id, user_id, joined, nick
FROM members WHERE community_id = ? AND user_id = ?;

-- name: ListMembersOfCommunity :many
SELECT community_id, user_id, joined, nick
FROM members
WHERE community_id = ? AND user_id > ?
ORDER BY user_id
LIMIT ?;

-- name: PutRole :exec
INSERT INTO roles (id, community_id, name, color, position, allow, deny, hoist, mentionable, created)
VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET
  name = excluded.name, color = excluded.color, position = excluded.position,
  allow = excluded.allow, deny = excluded.deny, hoist = excluded.hoist,
  mentionable = excluded.mentionable;

-- name: ListRoles :many
SELECT id, community_id, name, color, position, allow, deny, hoist, mentionable, created
FROM roles WHERE community_id = ? ORDER BY position, id;

-- name: PutMemberRole :exec
INSERT INTO member_roles (community_id, user_id, role_id)
VALUES (?, ?, ?)
ON CONFLICT (community_id, user_id, role_id) DO NOTHING;

-- name: DeleteMemberRole :execrows
DELETE FROM member_roles WHERE community_id = ? AND user_id = ? AND role_id = ?;

-- name: ListMemberRoles :many
SELECT role_id FROM member_roles WHERE community_id = ? AND user_id = ?;
```

Create `internal/store/postgres/queries/structure.sql` — the same **fourteen** queries with
`$1 … $n` placeholders instead of `?` and `LIMIT $3` typed as `integer`; everything else is
identical, which is the "one shared query dialect" rule of facts-storage §1.7.

First, in `internal/store/rows.go` (**P2-D24** — Plan 1a creates the file and declares only the row

```go
type CommunityRow struct {
	ID                   id.ID
	Owner                id.ID
	Name                 string
	IconBlob             []byte
```

`Allow` and `Deny` are `uint64`, not `api.Bits`: `internal/store` must not import `internal/api`.
Every handler therefore converts explicitly — `Allow: uint64(someBits)` on the way in,
`Bits(row.Allow)` on the way out.

**Then edit `Repository`'s embed list to add `Structure` (P2-D23, Plan 1a's ID1).** This is the
first of Plan 2's four embed edits — `Readable` follows in task 8, `Blobs` and `OpsBackups` in
task 10 — and each lands in the same commit as the adapter methods that satisfy it, because ID1
requires `go build ./...` to be green at the end of every task. Plan 1 left the list at nine entries
(its task 19 step 1a added `MLS`, its task 23 step 1a added `Messages` and `Cursors`). Written out
in full, exactly as the file must read after this edit:

```go
// Repository is what dillad persists through. Tx runs fn in one transaction and
// the Repository fn receives IS that transaction; nesting is an error. Close
// releases both pools.
//
// The embed list grows as the schema does, one named step per task (ID1):
// task 19 step 1a adds MLS with 00002_mls.sql, task 23 step 1a adds Messages and
// Cursors with 00003_messages.sql, Plan 2 task 1 step 9 adds Structure, and
// Plan 2's tasks 8 and 10 add Readable, Blobs and OpsBackups.
type Repository interface {
	Tx(ctx context.Context, fn func(Repository) error) error
	Close() error
	Instance
	Accounts
	Devices
	Sessions
	Auth
	Invites
	Ops
	MLS       // Plan 1 task 19 step 1a — 00002_mls.sql
	Messages  // Plan 1 task 23 step 1a — 00003_messages.sql
	Cursors   // Plan 1 task 23 step 1a
	Structure // added HERE — P2-D23; its tables are 00004_structure.sql, written in step 6
}
```

Now, in `internal/store/sqlite/repo.go`, write the **thirteen** `Structure` community methods
(the ten below plus `GetMember`, `UpdateCommunityMeta`, `SoftDeleteCommunity` and `ListMemberRoles`
— fourteen in all):

```go
func (r *Repo) CreateCommunity(ctx context.Context, c store.CommunityRow) error {
	err := r.q.CreateCommunity(ctx, sqlitedb.CreateCommunityParams{
		ID:                   c.ID,
		Owner:                c.Owner,
		Name:                 c.Name,
		IconBlob:             c.IconBlob,
		PolicyJson:           string(c.PolicyJSON),
		PolicyVersion:        int64(c.PolicyVersion),
		MinAccountAgeSeconds: int64(c.MinAccountAgeSeconds),
		RequireMod2fa:        int64(c.RequireMod2FA),
		Created:              c.Created,
		DeletedAt:            c.DeletedAt,
	})
	return mapErr(err)
}

func (r *Repo) GetCommunity(ctx context.Context, communityID id.ID) (store.CommunityRow, error) {
	row, err := r.q.GetCommunity(ctx, communityID)
	if err != nil {
		return store.CommunityRow{}, mapErr(err)
	}
	return store.CommunityRow{
		ID:                   row.ID,
		Owner:                row.Owner,
		Name:                 row.Name,
		IconBlob:             row.IconBlob,
		PolicyJSON:           []byte(row.PolicyJson),
		PolicyVersion:        uint64(row.PolicyVersion),
		MinAccountAgeSeconds: uint64(row.MinAccountAgeSeconds),
		RequireMod2FA:        uint8(row.RequireMod2fa),
		Created:              row.Created,
		DeletedAt:            row.DeletedAt,
	}, nil
}

func (r *Repo) UpdateCommunityPolicy(ctx context.Context, communityID id.ID, policy []byte, version int64) error {
	n, err := r.q.UpdateCommunityPolicy(ctx, sqlitedb.UpdateCommunityPolicyParams{
		PolicyJson:    string(policy),
		PolicyVersion: version,
		ID:            communityID,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return mapErr(err)
	}
	if n == 0 {
		return store.ErrNotFound
	}
	return nil
}

func (r *Repo) PutMember(ctx context.Context, m store.MemberOfCommunityRow) error {
	return mapErr(r.q.PutMember(ctx, sqlitedb.PutMemberParams{
		CommunityID: m.CommunityID, UserID: m.UserID, Joined: m.Joined, Nick: m.Nick,
	}))
}

func (r *Repo) DeleteMember(ctx context.Context, communityID, userID id.ID) error {
	n, err := r.q.DeleteMember(ctx, sqlitedb.DeleteMemberParams{
		CommunityID: communityID, UserID: userID,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return mapErr(err)
	}
	if n == 0 {
		return store.ErrNotFound
	}
	return nil
}

func (r *Repo) ListMembersOfCommunity(ctx context.Context, communityID, after id.ID, limit int32) ([]store.MemberOfCommunityRow, error) {
	rows, err := r.q.ListMembersOfCommunity(ctx, sqlitedb.ListMembersOfCommunityParams{
		CommunityID: communityID, UserID: after, Limit: int64(limit),
	})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, mapErr(err)
	}
	out := make([]store.MemberOfCommunityRow, 0, len(rows))
	for _, row := range rows {
		out = append(out, store.MemberOfCommunityRow{
			CommunityID: row.CommunityID, UserID: row.UserID, Joined: row.Joined, Nick: row.Nick,
		})
	}
	return out, nil
}

func (r *Repo) PutRole(ctx context.Context, role store.RoleRow) error {
	return mapErr(r.q.PutRole(ctx, sqlitedb.PutRoleParams{
		ID: role.ID, CommunityID: role.CommunityID, Name: role.Name,
		Color: int64(role.Color), Position: int64(role.Position),
		Allow: int64(role.Allow), Deny: int64(role.Deny),
		Hoist: int64(role.Hoist), Mentionable: int64(role.Mentionable),
		Created: role.Created,
	}))
}

func (r *Repo) ListRoles(ctx context.Context, communityID id.ID) ([]store.RoleRow, error) {
	rows, err := r.q.ListRoles(ctx, communityID)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, mapErr(err)
	}
	out := make([]store.RoleRow, 0, len(rows))
	for _, row := range rows {
		out = append(out, store.RoleRow{
			ID: row.ID, CommunityID: row.CommunityID, Name: row.Name,
			Color: uint64(row.Color), Position: uint64(row.Position),
			Allow: uint64(row.Allow), Deny: uint64(row.Deny),
			Hoist: uint8(row.Hoist), Mentionable: uint8(row.Mentionable),
			Created: row.Created,
		})
	}
	return out, nil
}

func (r *Repo) PutMemberRole(ctx context.Context, communityID, userID, roleID id.ID) error {
	return mapErr(r.q.PutMemberRole(ctx, sqlitedb.PutMemberRoleParams{
		CommunityID: communityID, UserID: userID, RoleID: roleID,
	}))
}

func (r *Repo) DeleteMemberRole(ctx context.Context, communityID, userID, roleID id.ID) error {
	n, err := r.q.DeleteMemberRole(ctx, sqlitedb.DeleteMemberRoleParams{
		CommunityID: communityID, UserID: userID, RoleID: roleID,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return mapErr(err)
	}
	if n == 0 {
		return store.ErrNotFound
	}
	return nil
}
```

and the four added methods:

```go
func (r *Repo) GetMember(ctx context.Context, communityID, userID id.ID) (store.MemberOfCommunityRow, error) {
	row, err := r.q.GetMember(ctx, sqlitedb.GetMemberParams{CommunityID: communityID, UserID: userID})
	if err != nil {
		// mapErr turns sql.ErrNoRows into store.ErrNotFound, which is what every
		// membership gate in internal/api tests with errors.Is.
		return store.MemberOfCommunityRow{}, mapErr(err)
	}
	return store.MemberOfCommunityRow{
		CommunityID: row.CommunityID, UserID: row.UserID, Joined: row.Joined, Nick: row.Nick,
	}, nil
}

func (r *Repo) UpdateCommunityMeta(ctx context.Context, communityID id.ID, name string, minAge uint64, requireMod2FA uint8) error {
	n, err := r.q.UpdateCommunityMeta(ctx, sqlitedb.UpdateCommunityMetaParams{
		Name:                 name,
		MinAccountAgeSeconds: int64(minAge),
		RequireMod2fa:        int64(requireMod2FA),
		ID:                   communityID,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return mapErr(err)
	}
	if n == 0 {
		return store.ErrNotFound
	}
	return nil
}

func (r *Repo) SoftDeleteCommunity(ctx context.Context, communityID id.ID, at int64) error {
	n, err := r.q.SoftDeleteCommunity(ctx, sqlitedb.SoftDeleteCommunityParams{DeletedAt: &at, ID: communityID})
	if err != nil {
		return mapErr(err)
	}
	if n == 0 {
		return store.ErrNotFound
	}
	return nil
}

func (r *Repo) ListMemberRoles(ctx context.Context, communityID, userID id.ID) ([]id.ID, error) {
	rows, err := r.q.ListMemberRoles(ctx, sqlitedb.ListMemberRolesParams{
		CommunityID: communityID, UserID: userID,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, mapErr(err)
	}
	return rows, nil
}
```

`mapErr` is Plan 1a task 3's translator from driver errors to `store.ErrNotFound` /
`store.ErrConflict`. Write the identical **fourteen** methods in
`internal/store/postgres/repo.go` against `pgdb.*Params`. There are **three** differences, not one:

1. the package name of the generated types (`pgdb` instead of `sqlitedb`);
2. `Limit` is `int32` there, so `Limit: limit` replaces `Limit: int64(limit)`;
3. **the JSON columns are `[]byte`, not `string`.** §4.2 maps the policy blob to `TEXT NOT NULL` on
   SQLite and `JSONB NOT NULL` on Postgres, so sqlc generates `PolicyJson string` for `sqlitedb` and
   `PolicyJson []byte` for `pgdb`. The two conversions therefore **drop** on Postgres. The same
   divergence hits `channels.settings_json` (task 2); `readable_messages.envelope` is `BLOB`/`BYTEA`
   on both engines and is `[]byte` either way (**P2-D27**).

The worked Postgres twin of the first method, so the divergence is visible rather than inferred:

```go
func (r *Repo) CreateCommunity(ctx context.Context, c store.CommunityRow) error {
	err := r.q.CreateCommunity(ctx, pgdb.CreateCommunityParams{
		ID:                   c.ID,
		Owner:                c.Owner,
		Name:                 c.Name,
		IconBlob:             c.IconBlob,
		PolicyJson:           c.PolicyJSON, // []byte on Postgres: no string() conversion
		PolicyVersion:        int64(c.PolicyVersion),
		MinAccountAgeSeconds: int64(c.MinAccountAgeSeconds),
		RequireMod2fa:        int16(c.RequireMod2FA), // SMALLINT on Postgres
		Created:              c.Created,
		DeletedAt:            c.DeletedAt,
	})
	return mapErr(err)
}
```

and its read side returns `PolicyJSON: row.PolicyJson` with no `[]byte(...)` conversion.

- [ ] **Step 11: Write the failing API test**

Create `internal/api/apitest_test.go` — the harness every later `internal/api` test reuses:

```go
package api_test

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"io"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/fxamacker/cbor/v2"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/auth"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/cborx"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/clock"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/id"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/server"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/store"
	sqlitestore "github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/store/sqlite"
)

type env struct {
	t    *testing.T
	Repo store.Repository
	Clk  *clock.Fake
	Mux  *server.Mux
	Srv  *httptest.Server
	sess map[string]auth.Session // bearer token -> session
}

// newEnv builds a repository over a temp SQLite file, migrates it, and returns an
// httptest server whose mux is empty; each test registers the handlers it needs.
func newEnv(t *testing.T) *env {
	t.Helper()
	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "dilla.db")
	wdb, err := sqlitestore.OpenWrite(path)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("OpenWrite: %v", err)
	}
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = wdb.Close() })
	if err := sqlitestore.Migrate(context.Background(), wdb); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Migrate: %v", err)
	}
	rdb, err := sqlitestore.OpenRead(path)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("OpenRead: %v", err)
	}
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = rdb.Close() })

	clk := clock.NewFake(time.Unix(1_790_000_000, 0).UTC())
	e := &env{
		t:    t,
		Repo: sqlitestore.New(wdb, rdb, clk),
		Clk:  clk,
		Mux:  server.NewMux(),
		sess: map[string]auth.Session{},
	}
	e.Srv = httptest.NewServer(e.authMiddleware(e.Mux))
	t.Cleanup(e.Srv.Close)
	return e
}

// authMiddleware is the test stand-in for auth.Sessions.Middleware: it maps a
// bearer token registered by NewUser to an auth.Session on the request context.
func (e *env) authMiddleware(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		tok, _ := strings.CutPrefix(r.Header.Get("Authorization"), "Bearer ")
		if s, ok := e.sess[tok]; ok {
			r = r.WithContext(auth.WithSession(r.Context(), s))
		}
		next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
	})
}

// NewUser creates a user row plus one device, and returns the bearer token that
// authenticates as that (user, device).
func (e *env) NewUser(username string) (id.ID, string) {
	e.t.Helper()
	uid, did := id.New(), id.New()
	if err := e.Repo.CreateUser(context.Background(), newAPITestUser(uid, username, e.Clk.Now().Unix())); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
	}
	if err := e.Repo.CreateDevice(context.Background(), newAPITestDevice(did, uid, e.Clk.Now().Unix())); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("CreateDevice: %v", err)
	}
	tok := uid.String()
	e.sess[tok] = auth.Session{UserID: uid, DeviceID: did, Scope: auth.ScopeEnrolled}
	return uid, tok
}

// Do sends one CBOR request and returns the status and the raw body.
func (e *env) Do(method, path, token string, body any) (int, []byte) {
	e.t.Helper()
	var rdr *bytes.Reader
	if body != nil {
		b, err := cborx.Marshal(body)
		if err != nil {
			e.t.Fatalf("cborx.Marshal: %v", err)
		}
		rdr = bytes.NewReader(b)
	} else {
		rdr = bytes.NewReader(nil)
	}
	req, err := http.NewRequest(method, e.Srv.URL+path, rdr)
	if err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("NewRequest: %v", err)
	}
	if body != nil {
		req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/cbor")
	}
	if token != "" {
		req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
	}
	resp, err := e.Srv.Client().Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("Do %s %s: %v", method, path, err)
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	out, err := io.ReadAll(resp.Body)
	if err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("ReadAll: %v", err)
	}
	return resp.StatusCode, out
}

// ErrCode decodes an E_* error body and returns its code.
func (e *env) ErrCode(body []byte) string {
	e.t.Helper()
	var arr []cbor.RawMessage
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(body, &arr); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("error body is not a CBOR array: %v (%x)", err, body)
	}
	var code string
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(arr[0], &code); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("error code: %v", err)
	}
	return code
}
```

`auth.WithSession` is the exported context setter this task adds to `internal/auth/session.go`
(**P2-D26**) — without it no test in any Plan 2 task can inject a session, because Plan 1a's
`ctxKey{}` is unexported. `newAPITestUser`/`newAPITestDevice` build a `store.UserRow`/
`store.DeviceRow` with the minimum non-null columns of §4.3.

**Import blocks in this plan are complete as written and are not "indicative".** Every Go block
below lists exactly the packages its body uses; `goimports` cannot infer `internal/auth`,
`internal/ds`, `internal/gateway` or `internal/blob` from a bare identifier, and each task's
"run it to verify it fails" step depends on the failure being the named undefined symbol rather than
a missing import.
