- [ ] **Step 3: Write the parser and the limits**

Create `internal/api/envelope.go`:

```go
package api

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net/http"
	"regexp"
	"unicode/utf8"

	"github.com/fxamacker/cbor/v2"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/cborx"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/id"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/server"
)

// Envelope types, protocol/04 "Envelope".
const (
	EnvMessage        uint8 = 0
	EnvEdit           uint8 = 1
	EnvDelete         uint8 = 2
	EnvReactionAdd    uint8 = 3
	EnvReactionRemove uint8 = 4
	EnvPin            uint8 = 5
	EnvUnpin          uint8 = 6
)

// The limits of protocol/04 as amended by interfaces.md §2.8. Every one of them
// has a reject vector.
const (
	maxBodyMessage     = 4000
	maxBodyReaction    = 32
	maxAttachments     = 4
	maxPreviews        = 2
	maxMimeBytes       = 255
	maxThumbBytes      = 8192
	maxPreviewURL      = 2048
	maxPreviewTitle    = 256
	maxPreviewDesc     = 1024
	maxPreviewImage    = 16384
	envelopeElements   = 9
	frankingKeyBytes   = 32
	attachmentElements = 8
	previewElements    = 4
)

// Attachment is protocol/04's 8-element fixed-position array, in element order:
// blob_id, key, nonce, size, mime, w, h, thumb.
//
// The `cbor:",toarray"` marker is load-bearing, not decoration. fxamacker/cbor
// decodes a CBOR array into a struct ONLY when the struct carries it; without it
// the decoder expects a map keyed by field name, and internal/cborx forbids maps
// outright (ErrMapForbidden, decode.go:19). Every attachment- and preview-bearing
// envelope would be refused at run time. internal/cborx's own package doc states
// the rule — "Structs must carry the `cbor:\",toarray\"` tag on a leading
// `_ struct{}` field" — and internal/cborx/decode_test.go's envelopeV1 fixture is
// the in-repo example.
type Attachment struct {
	_      struct{} `cbor:",toarray"`
	BlobID []byte
	Key    []byte
	Nonce  []byte
	Size   uint64
	Mime   string
	W, H   *uint64
	Thumb  []byte
}

// Preview is protocol/04's 4-element array: url, title, description, image.
type Preview struct {
	_                       struct{} `cbor:",toarray"`
	URL, Title, Description string
	Image                   []byte
}

type Envelope struct {
	V            uint64
	MsgID        id.ID
	Type         uint8
	ThreadID     *id.ID
	ReplyTo      *id.ID
	Body         string
	Attachments  []Attachment
	Previews     []Preview
	KF           []byte
	MentionCount int
}

// envErr builds a refusal carrying one of protocol/04's three receiver codes.
// §2.1's /v1 status table is a closed vocabulary and Plan 1 task 0 owns it: the
// three rows and the three server.Code constants land there, not here (P2-D30).
// A server-readable channel's instance IS a receiver in protocol/04's sense, it
// refuses exactly what a client refuses, and a client switching on §2.1's codes
// must not meet an unknown one.
//
// The status comes from the Code, as it does at every other call site in this
// plan; all three of these are 400.
//
//	400 E_ENVELOPE_SHAPE  the envelope is not a 9-element deterministic CBOR array,
//	                      or a field has the wrong type or length
//	400 E_ENVELOPE_TYPE   the type byte names no envelope type this version knows
//	400 E_ENVELOPE_LIMIT  a field or list exceeds the §2.8 bound
func envErr(code server.Code, format string, a ...any) error {
	return server.Errorf(code, format, a...)
}

// ParseEnvelope decodes and validates one envelope. It is the server side of
// protocol/04's receiver rules: a readable channel's instance is a receiver, so
// it refuses exactly what a client refuses and stores nothing partial.
func ParseEnvelope(b []byte) (Envelope, error) {
	var raw []cbor.RawMessage
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(b, &raw); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "envelope is not deterministic CBOR: %v", err)
	}
	if len(raw) != envelopeElements {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "envelope has %d elements, want %d", len(raw), envelopeElements)
	}
	var e Envelope
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[0], &e.V); err != nil || e.V != 1 {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "v must be 1")
	}
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[1], &e.MsgID); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "msg_id must be 16 bytes")
	}
	var typ uint64
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[2], &typ); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "type must be a uint")
	}
	if typ > uint64(EnvUnpin) {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeType, "unknown type %d", typ)
	}
	e.Type = uint8(typ)
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[3], &e.ThreadID); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "thread_id must be 16 bytes or null")
	}
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[4], &e.ReplyTo); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "reply_to must be 16 bytes or null")
	}
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[5], &e.Body); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "body must be a text string")
	}
	if !utf8.ValidString(e.Body) {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "body is not valid UTF-8")
	}
	switch e.Type {
	case EnvMessage, EnvEdit:
		if len(e.Body) > maxBodyMessage {
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "body is %d bytes, at most %d", len(e.Body), maxBodyMessage)
		}
	case EnvReactionAdd, EnvReactionRemove:
		if len(e.Body) > maxBodyReaction {
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "a reaction body is %d bytes, at most %d", len(e.Body), maxBodyReaction)
		}
	default: // delete, pin, unpin
		if len(e.Body) != 0 {
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "type %d carries no body", e.Type)
		}
	}
	// Element counts are asserted before decoding: a struct with the toarray
	// marker decodes a SHORTER array by leaving trailing fields zero, so a
	// 6-element attachment would silently arrive with an empty thumb.
	if err := checkElementCounts(raw[6], attachmentElements, "attachment"); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, err
	}
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[6], &e.Attachments); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "attachments: %v", err)
	}
	if len(e.Attachments) > maxAttachments {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "%d attachments, at most %d", len(e.Attachments), maxAttachments)
	}
	for i, a := range e.Attachments {
		switch {
		case len(a.BlobID) != 32:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "attachment %d: blob_id must be 32 bytes", i)
		case len(a.Key) != 32:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "attachment %d: key must be 32 bytes", i)
		case len(a.Nonce) != 12:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "attachment %d: nonce must be 12 bytes", i)
		case len(a.Mime) > maxMimeBytes:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "attachment %d: mime is %d bytes, at most %d", i, len(a.Mime), maxMimeBytes)
		case len(a.Thumb) > maxThumbBytes:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "attachment %d: thumb is %d bytes, at most %d", i, len(a.Thumb), maxThumbBytes)
		}
	}
	if err := checkElementCounts(raw[7], previewElements, "preview"); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, err
	}
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[7], &e.Previews); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "previews: %v", err)
	}
	if len(e.Previews) > maxPreviews {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "%d previews, at most %d", len(e.Previews), maxPreviews)
	}
	for i, p := range e.Previews {
		switch {
		case len(p.URL) > maxPreviewURL:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "preview %d: url is %d bytes, at most %d", i, len(p.URL), maxPreviewURL)
		case len(p.Title) > maxPreviewTitle:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "preview %d: title is %d bytes, at most %d", i, len(p.Title), maxPreviewTitle)
		case len(p.Description) > maxPreviewDesc:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "preview %d: description is %d bytes, at most %d", i, len(p.Description), maxPreviewDesc)
		case len(p.Image) > maxPreviewImage:
			return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeLimit, "preview %d: image is %d bytes, at most %d", i, len(p.Image), maxPreviewImage)
		}
	}
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw[8], &e.KF); err != nil || len(e.KF) != frankingKeyBytes {
		return Envelope{}, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "k_f must be %d bytes", frankingKeyBytes)
	}
	e.MentionCount = countMentions(e.Body)
	return e, nil
}

// checkElementCounts asserts that every member of a CBOR array of arrays has
// exactly n elements, so a short or long attachment or preview is E_ENVELOPE_SHAPE
// rather than a silently zero-filled struct.
func checkElementCounts(raw cbor.RawMessage, n int, what string) error {
	var items []cbor.RawMessage
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(raw, &items); err != nil {
		return envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "%ss must be an array: %v", what, err)
	}
	for i, it := range items {
		var fields []cbor.RawMessage
		if err := cborx.Unmarshal(it, &fields); err != nil {
			return envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "%s %d must be an array: %v", what, i, err)
		}
		if len(fields) != n {
			return envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "%s %d has %d elements, want %d", what, i, len(fields), n)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// mentionRE matches the two mention spellings a readable body may carry:
// <@hexhexhex…> for a user or role, and <@everyone>. AutoMod counts them at
// write time (gap-69 §7) so the count is one dialect-neutral column rather than
// a JSON extraction that differs per engine.
var mentionRE = regexp.MustCompile(`<@(everyone|here|[0-9a-f]{32})>`)

// countMentions returns the number of DISTINCT mention targets, so pasting the
// same handle ten times is one mention, not ten.
func countMentions(body string) int {
	seen := map[string]bool{}
	for _, m := range mentionRE.FindAllStringSubmatch(body, -1) {
		seen[m[1]] = true
	}
	return len(seen)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Wire the six non-message types into the readable path**

In `internal/api/readable.go`, `post` now calls `ParseEnvelope` — which task 8 deliberately left
out so that task 8 could end green — fills `Body` and `MentionCount` on the stored row, and gains
the type switch:

```go
	env, err := ParseEnvelope(req.Envelope)
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
```

and the `PutReadableMessage` call becomes
`Body: env.Body, MentionCount: uint64(env.MentionCount)`. Then:

```go
	switch env.Type {
	case EnvMessage:
		// falls through to the ordinary append below
	case EnvEdit:
		// The ALIAS path. The canonical spelling is
		// PATCH /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq}, registered by task 8, which
		// takes its target from the path; a type-1 envelope posted here resolves
		// its target through reply_to (P2-D15) and lands in the same applyEdit.
		if env.ReplyTo == nil {
			server.WriteError(w, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "an edit names its target in reply_to"))
			return
		}
		// An edit is applied in place, not appended: readable history is the
		// server's own record, so a second row would show the old text forever.
		if err := h.applyEdit(r.Context(), ch, s, seqFromRef(*env.ReplyTo), req.Envelope); err != nil {
			server.WriteError(w, err)
			return
		}
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
		return
	case EnvDelete:
		if env.ReplyTo == nil {
			server.WriteError(w, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "a delete names its target in reply_to"))
			return
		}
		if err := h.applyDelete(r.Context(), ch, s, seqFromRef(*env.ReplyTo)); err != nil {
			server.WriteError(w, err)
			return
		}
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
		return
	case EnvPin, EnvUnpin:
		if err := h.res.Require(r.Context(), s.UserID, ch, PermPinMessages); err != nil {
			server.WriteError(w, err)
			return
		}
		// A pin is an ordinary appended envelope; clients read the channel's
		// pin set from the envelope stream, exactly as protocol/04 describes.
	case EnvReactionAdd, EnvReactionRemove:
		// Also appended; the client folds them.
	}
```

`applyEdit` loads the target by `msg_id` — which means `readable_messages` needs `msg_id`. It does
not have one: §4.3 keys a readable row by `(channel_id, seq)` and the envelope's `msg_id` lives
inside the stored `envelope` blob. **[here]** `reply_to` on the readable path therefore names the target's
**`seq`**, encoded in the envelope's 16-byte `reply_to` field as a big-endian uint64 in the last
eight bytes with the first eight zero, and `applyEdit` reads it back. That keeps `protocol/04`'s
element shape untouched while giving the server a key it actually holds. Recorded as **P2-D15** and
written into `protocol/09-http-api.md` under `## Readable channels`, with the sentence: "on a
server-readable channel, an envelope's `reply_to` and `thread_id` carry the target's channel `seq`
as a big-endian uint64 in the low eight bytes; in an end-to-end encrypted group they carry the
target's `msg_id` unchanged, because there the server has no key to resolve".

```go
// applyEdit is reached from PATCH /v1/channels/{id}/messages/{seq} (canonical)
// and from a type-1 envelope on POST (alias). Both give it the target seq.
func (h *Readable) applyEdit(ctx context.Context, ch store.ChannelRow, s auth.Session, seq uint64, envelope []byte) error {
	env, err := ParseEnvelope(envelope)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	rows, err := h.repo.ListReadableMessages(ctx, ch.ID, seq, 1)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if len(rows) == 0 || rows[0].Seq != seq {
		return server.Errorf(server.CodeNotFound, "no such message")
	}
	if rows[0].Sender != s.UserID {
		return server.Errorf(server.CodeForbidden, "only the author may edit a message")
	}
	if rows[0].Deleted != nil {
		return server.Errorf(server.CodeNotFound, "no such message")
	}
	now := h.clk.Now().Unix()
	// The franking tag is RECOMPUTED over the new bytes. Reusing rows[0].FrankingTag
	// would leave a tag that commits to text the instance no longer stores, so a
	// report against an edited message could never verify — and the tag would be
	// evidence for a message that no longer exists.
	kf, err := envelopeKF(envelope)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	c, err := Commitment(envelope, kf)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	keyID, key := h.keys.Current()
	tag := Tag(key, ch.ID, 0, seq, s.DeviceID, c, now)
	if err := h.repo.EditReadableMessage(ctx, ch.ID, seq, envelope, env.Body, tag, keyID, now); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	h.fan.DeliverChannel(ch.ID, gateway.Frame{
		Op: gateway.OpMessagePlain, Replay: true,
		Payload: mustPayload(ch.ID, seq, rows[0].Sender, envelope, tag, 1, 0),
	})
	return nil
}

// applyDelete tombstones the row: the body is emptied so it leaves the search
// index through the _au trigger, while the franking tuple survives for the
// report path of task 17.
func (h *Readable) applyDelete(ctx context.Context, ch store.ChannelRow, s auth.Session, seq uint64) error {
	rows, err := h.repo.ListReadableMessages(ctx, ch.ID, seq, 1)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if len(rows) == 0 || rows[0].Seq != seq || rows[0].Deleted != nil {
		return server.Errorf(server.CodeNotFound, "no such message")
	}
	if rows[0].Sender != s.UserID {
		if err := h.res.Require(ctx, s.UserID, ch, PermManageMessages); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	now := h.clk.Now().Unix()
	if err := h.repo.DeleteReadableMessage(ctx, ch.ID, seq, now); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	h.fan.DeliverChannel(ch.ID, gateway.Frame{
		Op: gateway.OpMessagePlain, Replay: true,
		Payload: mustPayload(ch.ID, seq, rows[0].Sender, nil, rows[0].FrankingTag, 0, 1),
	})
	return nil
}

// seqFromRef reads a readable channel's message reference: the low eight bytes
// of the 16-byte field, big endian. P2-D15. It is used only on the envelope
// alias path; the canonical PATCH and DELETE take {seq} from the path.
func seqFromRef(ref id.ID) uint64 { return binary.BigEndian.Uint64(ref[8:]) }
```

`EditReadableMessage` takes two more parameters than **P2-D13** gave it, for the reason above — the
tag and the key id move with the bytes:

```go
EditReadableMessage(ctx context.Context, channelID id.ID, seq uint64,
	envelope []byte, body string, frankingTag []byte, frankingKeyID id.ID, at int64) error
```

the stored one, which is what makes a stale tab harmless.

Create `internal/blob/store_test.go`:

```go
package blob_test

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"errors"
	"io"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/blob"
)

func newStore(t *testing.T) (*blob.Store, string) {
	t.Helper()
	dir := t.TempDir()
	s, err := blob.Open(dir, "fs")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Open: %v", err)
	}
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = s.Close() })
	return s, dir
}

func TestPutStreamsAndVerifiesTheHash(t *testing.T) {
	s, dir := newStore(t)
	payload := bytes.Repeat([]byte("ciphertext"), 1000)
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)

	n, created, err := s.Put(t.Context(), sum[:], bytes.NewReader(payload), 1<<20)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Put: %v", err)
	}
	if !created || n != int64(len(payload)) {
		t.Fatalf("Put = (%d, %v)", n, created)
	}
	// The file lands at the two-level fan-out the storage_ref names.
	hex := blob.StorageRef("fs", sum[:])
	want := filepath.Join(dir, strings.TrimPrefix(hex, "fs:"))
	if fi, err := os.Stat(want); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("stat %s: %v", want, err)
	} else if fi.Size() != int64(len(payload)) {
		t.Fatalf("size = %d", fi.Size())
	}
	// A second Put of the same bytes is not new.
	if _, created, err = s.Put(t.Context(), sum[:], bytes.NewReader(payload), 1<<20); err != nil || created {
		t.Fatalf("second Put = (%v, %v), want created=false", created, err)
	}
}

func TestPutRejectsAMismatchAndLeavesNothingBehind(t *testing.T) {
	s, dir := newStore(t)
	payload := []byte("actual bytes")
	wrong := sha256.Sum256([]byte("different bytes"))

	if _, _, err := s.Put(t.Context(), wrong[:], bytes.NewReader(payload), 1<<20); !errors.Is(err, blob.ErrHashMismatch) {
		t.Fatalf("Put = %v, want ErrHashMismatch", err)
	}
	var files []string
	if err := filepath.WalkDir(dir, func(p string, d os.DirEntry, err error) error {
		if err == nil && !d.IsDir() {
			files = append(files, p)
		}
		return err
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("walk: %v", err)
	}
	if len(files) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("a rejected upload left %v behind", files)
	}
}

func TestPutRefusesOverTheLimitWithoutBufferingIt(t *testing.T) {
	s, _ := newStore(t)
	big := bytes.Repeat([]byte("x"), 4096)
	sum := sha256.Sum256(big)
	if _, _, err := s.Put(t.Context(), sum[:], bytes.NewReader(big), 1024); !errors.Is(err, blob.ErrTooLarge) {
		t.Fatalf("Put over the limit = %v, want ErrTooLarge", err)
	}
}

func TestOsRootBlocksAnEscape(t *testing.T) {
	s, dir := newStore(t)
	outside := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(dir), "escaped")
	// StorageRef is derived from the 32-byte id, so a traversal cannot be
	// spelled through the API; assert the guarantee os.Root gives anyway, since
	// it is what makes the derivation safe rather than merely tidy.
	if err := blob.WriteRelativeForTest(s, "../escaped", []byte("x")); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("os.Root allowed a write outside the root")
	}
	if _, err := os.Stat(outside); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
		t.Fatalf("a file appeared outside the root: %v", err)
	}
}

func TestGetReturnsASeekableFile(t *testing.T) {
	s, _ := newStore(t)
	payload := []byte("0123456789")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	if _, _, err := s.Put(t.Context(), sum[:], bytes.NewReader(payload), 1<<20); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Put: %v", err)
	}
	f, size, err := s.Get(sum[:])
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Get: %v", err)
	}
	defer f.Close()
	if size != int64(len(payload)) {
		t.Fatalf("size = %d", size)
	}
	if _, err := f.Seek(4, io.SeekStart); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Seek: %v", err)
	}
	rest, err := io.ReadAll(f)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ReadAll: %v", err)
	}
	if string(rest) != "456789" {
		t.Fatalf("rest = %q", rest)
	}
}

func TestSweepTempRemovesACrashedUpload(t *testing.T) {
	s, dir := newStore(t)
	shard := filepath.Join(dir, "att", "aa", "bb")
	if err := os.MkdirAll(shard, 0o700); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("MkdirAll: %v", err)
	}
	leftover := filepath.Join(shard, "aabb00.tmp-deadbeef")
	if err := os.WriteFile(leftover, []byte("half"), 0o600); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("WriteFile: %v", err)
	}
	n, err := s.SweepTemp()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SweepTemp: %v", err)
	}
	if n != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("SweepTemp removed %d files, want 1", n)
	}
	if _, err := os.Stat(leftover); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
		t.Fatalf("the temp file survived: %v", err)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the blob store**

Create `internal/blob/store.go`:

```go
// Package blob is dillad's content-addressed store for attachment ciphertext.
// The name of an object is the SHA-256 of its bytes, so the store never has to
// trust a client's claim about what it uploaded: it hashes what it receives.
package blob

import (
	"context"
	"crypto/hmac"
	"crypto/rand"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/hex"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"io/fs"
	"os"
	"path"
	"strings"
)

var (
	ErrHashMismatch = errors.New("blob: body does not hash to the requested id")
	ErrTooLarge     = errors.New("blob: over the instance limit")
	ErrNotFound     = errors.New("blob: no such object")
)

// Store writes under one *os.Root, so a path can never leave the blob
// directory: "Methods on Root can only access files and directories beneath a
// root directory" (go doc os.Root, Go 1.27).
type Store struct {
	root    *os.Root
	backend string
}

func New(root *os.Root, backend string) *Store { return &Store{root: root, backend: backend} }

func Open(dir, backend string) (*Store, error) {
	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o700); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	root, err := os.OpenRoot(dir)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return New(root, backend), nil
}

func (s *Store) Close() error { return s.root.Close() }

// StorageRef is the value of blobs.storage_ref: a backend tag plus a derived
// locator, never a stored path. gap-47 §9: a stored path can drift from the
// hash, is attacker-influenced, and would have to be rewritten row by row when
// the S3 backend lands.
func StorageRef(backend string, blobID []byte) string {
	h := hex.EncodeToString(blobID)
	return backend + ":" + path.Join("att", h[0:2], h[2:4], h)
}

// rel is the path inside the root.
func rel(blobID []byte) string {
	h := hex.EncodeToString(blobID)
	return path.Join("att", h[0:2], h[2:4], h)
}

// Put streams r through SHA-256 while writing it, and keeps the bytes only when
// the digest equals want. The sequence is temp file (0600) -> Sync -> Rename ->
// directory Sync, so a crash leaves either nothing or a complete object.
func (s *Store) Put(ctx context.Context, want []byte, r io.Reader, max int64) (int64, bool, error) {
	if len(want) != sha256.Size {
		return 0, false, fmt.Errorf("blob: id is %d bytes, want %d", len(want), sha256.Size)
	}
	final := rel(want)
	if fi, err := s.root.Stat(final); err == nil {
		return fi.Size(), false, nil
	} else if !errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
		return 0, false, err
	}
	dir := path.Dir(final)
	if err := s.root.MkdirAll(dir, 0o700); err != nil {
		return 0, false, err
	}
	// os.Root has no CreateTemp, so the name is built by hand and O_EXCL makes
	// the create the lock.
	var suffix [8]byte
	if _, err := rand.Read(suffix[:]); err != nil {
		return 0, false, err
	}
	tmp := final + ".tmp-" + hex.EncodeToString(suffix[:])
	f, err := s.root.OpenFile(tmp, os.O_RDWR|os.O_CREATE|os.O_EXCL, 0o600)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, false, err
	}
	cleanup := func() {
		_ = f.Close()
		_ = s.root.Remove(tmp)
	}
	h := sha256.New()
	n, err := io.Copy(f, io.TeeReader(io.LimitReader(r, max+1), h))
	if err != nil {
		cleanup()
		return 0, false, err
	}
	if n > max {
		cleanup()
		return 0, false, ErrTooLarge
	}
	if !hmac.Equal(h.Sum(nil), want) {
		cleanup()
		return 0, false, ErrHashMismatch
	}
	if err := f.Sync(); err != nil {
		cleanup()
		return 0, false, err
	}
	if err := f.Close(); err != nil {
		_ = s.root.Remove(tmp)
		return 0, false, err
	}
	if err := s.root.Rename(tmp, final); err != nil {
		_ = s.root.Remove(tmp)
		return 0, false, err
	}
	// Sync the directory so the rename itself is durable, not only the bytes.
	if d, err := s.root.Open(dir); err == nil {
		_ = d.Sync()
		_ = d.Close()
	}
	return n, true, nil
}

// Get returns the object as a *os.File, which is documented to satisfy
// io.ReadSeeker, so http.ServeContent can answer Range requests. Do not reach
// for ServeFileFS over root.FS(): Root.FS() promises only StatFS/ReadFileFS/
// ReadDirFS/ReadLinkFS, and ServeFileFS requires io.Seeker.
func (s *Store) Get(blobID []byte) (*os.File, int64, error) {
	f, err := s.root.Open(rel(blobID))
	if err != nil {
		if errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
			return nil, 0, ErrNotFound
		}
		return nil, 0, err
	}
	fi, err := f.Stat()
	if err != nil {
		_ = f.Close()
		return nil, 0, err
	}
	return f, fi.Size(), nil
}

func (s *Store) Stat(blobID []byte) (int64, error) {
	fi, err := s.root.Stat(rel(blobID))
	if errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
		return 0, ErrNotFound
	}
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	return fi.Size(), nil
}

func (s *Store) Delete(blobID []byte) error {
	err := s.root.Remove(rel(blobID))
	if errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
		return nil
	}
	return err
}

// SweepTemp removes the .tmp-* files a crash between create and rename leaves.
// dillad serve calls it once at start.
func (s *Store) SweepTemp() (int, error) {
	var n int
	err := fs.WalkDir(s.root.FS(), "att", func(p string, d fs.DirEntry, err error) error {
		if err != nil {
			if errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
				return nil
			}
			return err
		}
		if d.IsDir() || !strings.Contains(d.Name(), ".tmp-") {
			return nil
		}
		if err := s.root.Remove(p); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		n++
		return nil
	})
	return n, err
}

// writeRelative is unexported and is reached from tests through
// internal/blob/export_test.go. It exists only so a test can assert that os.Root
// refuses a traversal; an exported production method that exists only for a test
// is what the §9.3 linter's unused-export rule is for.
func (s *Store) writeRelative(p string, b []byte) error {
	f, err := s.root.OpenFile(p, os.O_RDWR|os.O_CREATE|os.O_EXCL, 0o600)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	defer f.Close()
	_, err = f.Write(b)
	return err
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the schema, migration and queries**

Create `internal/store/sqlite/schema/008_blobs.sql` (Postgres twin with §4.2's substitutions;
migration `00010_blobs.sql` wraps the same DDL):

```sql
-- 008_blobs.sql — the content-addressed store's bookkeeping.
-- The reference table is blob_refs; interfaces.md §4.3's `attachments` twin is
-- not created (P2-D2), because no store.Blobs method reads or writes it.
CREATE TABLE blobs (
  blob_id     BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(blob_id) = 32) PRIMARY KEY,
  size        INTEGER NOT NULL,          -- CIPHERTEXT bytes on disk
  storage_ref TEXT    NOT NULL,          -- derived: "<backend>:att/aa/bb/<hex>"
  created     INTEGER NOT NULL,
  unref_since INTEGER                    -- NULL while referenced
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX blobs_unref ON blobs(unref_since) WHERE unref_since IS NOT NULL;

CREATE TABLE blob_refs (
  blob_id         BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(blob_id) = 32)
                          REFERENCES blobs(blob_id) ON DELETE RESTRICT,
  channel_id      BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(channel_id) = 16)
                          REFERENCES channels(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  uploader_device BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(uploader_device) = 16),
  mime            TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  created         INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (blob_id, channel_id)
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX blob_refs_by_channel ON blob_refs(channel_id, created);

CREATE TABLE blob_tombstones (
  blob_id BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(blob_id) = 32) PRIMARY KEY,
  reason  TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  by_user BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(by_user) = 16),
  created INTEGER NOT NULL
) STRICT;

-- P2-D32: device_id is NOT NULL with the ALL-ZERO id meaning "not device
-- scoped", rather than §4.3's nullable column. A nullable column inside a
-- PRIMARY KEY behaves differently on the two engines and is wrong on both:
-- PostgreSQL implicitly forces every PK column NOT NULL, so a kind = 0 root
-- backup could not be inserted at all there, while SQLite permits the NULL in a
-- rowid table's PK and therefore does NOT enforce uniqueness, accepting duplicate
-- root rows. The sentinel keeps the composite key and makes both engines agree.
-- §4.5's `backups.device_id` pointer: true override is dropped in the same commit.
CREATE TABLE backups (
  user_id      BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(user_id) = 16)
                       REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  kind         INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN (0, 1, 2)),
  device_id    BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(device_id) = 16),
  chunk_seq    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  blob_id      BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(blob_id) = 32),
  manifest_sig BLOB,
  created      INTEGER NOT NULL,
  -- kind 0 (root) and kind 1 (state) are not device scoped and carry the
  -- all-zero id; kind 2 (chunk) carries a real device id.
  CHECK (kind = 2 OR device_id = x'00000000000000000000000000000000'),
  PRIMARY KEY (user_id, kind, device_id, chunk_seq)
) STRICT;
```

`ON DELETE RESTRICT` on `blobs` is the database-level guarantee that a `blobs` row is never deleted
while a reference stands; SQLite's RESTRICT is immediate, not deferred. It only bites when
`foreign_keys` is on, which §4.7's DSN sets on both pools and which the migration connection must
also set.

The queries (both engines): `PutBlob`, `GetBlob`, `PutBlobRef`, `DeleteBlobRef`, `CountBlobRefs`,
`MarkBlobUnreferenced`, `ClearBlobUnreferenced`, `ListCollectableBlobs`, `DeleteBlob`,
`PutBlobTombstone`, `GetBlobTombstone`, `UserBlobBytes`, `PutBackup`, `ListBackups`. Two that are
not one-liners:

```sql
-- name: MarkBlobUnreferenced :exec
UPDATE blobs SET unref_since = ?
WHERE blob_id = ?
  AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM blob_refs WHERE blob_refs.blob_id = blobs.blob_id);

-- name: UserBlobBytes :one
-- The quota counts each distinct blob a user uploaded once, however many
-- channels they published it into.
SELECT COALESCE(SUM(b.size), 0) FROM blobs b
WHERE b.blob_id IN (
  SELECT DISTINCT r.blob_id FROM blob_refs r
  JOIN devices d ON d.id = r.uploader_device
  WHERE d.user_id = ?
);
```

Expected: no output; `blobs.blob_id` is `[]byte` because §4.5's first override excludes
`*.blob_id` from the id wildcards.

Implement all fourteen in both `repo.go` files. `ClearBlobUnreferenced` is **P2-D16** — §4.1 has
`MarkBlobUnreferenced` but no way to clear it, and gap-47 §8.3 requires that every `PUT` creating a
reference clears `unref_since` in the same transaction.

```go
func TestRangeAndHeadCarryTheRightHeaders(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok := blobEnv(t)
	payload := []byte("0123456789abcdef")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	url := "/v1/channels/" + ch.String() + "/blobs/" + hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
	if status, _ := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut, url, tok, "application/octet-stream", payload); status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatal("PUT failed")
	}

	head := e.Request(t, http.MethodHead, url, tok, nil, nil)
	defer head.Body.Close()
	if head.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("HEAD = %d", head.StatusCode)
	}
	for k, want := range map[string]string{
		"Content-Type":                "application/octet-stream",
		"Content-Length":              "16",
		"Accept-Ranges":               "bytes",
		"ETag":                        `"` + hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]) + `"`,
		"Content-Disposition":         "attachment",
		"X-Content-Type-Options":      "nosniff",
		"Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy": "same-origin",
		"Cache-Control":               "private, max-age=31536000, immutable",
	} {
		if got := head.Header.Get(k); got != want {
			t.Errorf("HEAD %s = %q, want %q", k, got, want)
		}
	}
	if head.Header.Get("Last-Modified") != "" {
		t.Error("Last-Modified must not be sent: the mtime is meaningless after a restore")
	}

	get := e.Request(t, http.MethodGet, url, tok, map[string]string{"Range": "bytes=4-7"}, nil)
	defer get.Body.Close()
	if get.StatusCode != http.StatusPartialContent {
		t.Fatalf("ranged GET = %d", get.StatusCode)
	}
	b, _ := io.ReadAll(get.Body)
	if string(b) != "4567" {
		t.Fatalf("range body = %q", b)
	}
	if got := get.Header.Get("Content-Range"); got != "bytes 4-7/16" {
		t.Fatalf("Content-Range = %q", got)
	}
}

func TestAFetchWithNoReferenceInThisChannelIs404(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok := blobEnv(t)
	other := secondChannel(t, e, ch, tok)
	payload := []byte("shared")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	if status, _ := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut,
		"/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok,
		"application/octet-stream", payload); status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatal("PUT failed")
	}
	// The bytes exist, but not in `other`. The answer must be 404, never 403:
	// the response must not tell a caller that a blob they cannot reach exists.
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodGet,
		"/v1/channels/"+other.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok, nil)
	if status != http.StatusNotFound {
		t.Fatalf("cross-channel GET = %d, want 404", status)
	}
	if code := e.ErrCode(body); code != "E_NOT_FOUND" {
		t.Fatalf("code = %s", code)
	}
}
```

`e.DoRaw` and `e.Request` join `apitest_test.go`: `DoRaw` sends an arbitrary content type and body
and returns `(status, body)`; `Request` returns the `*http.Response` so headers can be asserted.

- [ ] **Step 8: Write the handlers**

Create `internal/api/blobs.go`. The route table first — `Register` is declared in the Produces block
above and is written out here, because these four routes are implemented by no other task:

```go
func (b *Blobs) Register(mux *server.Mux) {
	mux.HandleFunc("PUT /v1/channels/{id}/blobs/{blob_id}", b.put)
	// ServeMux routes HEAD to the GET handler, so one registration answers both;
	// http.ServeContent writes no body for a HEAD and keeps the headers.
	mux.HandleFunc("GET /v1/channels/{id}/blobs/{blob_id}", b.get)
	mux.HandleFunc("DELETE /v1/channels/{id}/blobs/{blob_id}", b.delete) // task 11
}
```

`{id}` is the channel, parsed with `server.PathID`; `{blob_id}` is the 64-hex digest, parsed with
`server.PathDigest` **before** the database or the filesystem is touched, which is §5.0's rule.

The `PUT` path, which carries every rule:

```go
func (b *Blobs) put(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	// SetReadDeadline must be the FIRST statement: "Setting the read deadline
	// after it has been exceeded will not extend it" (go doc
	// http.ResponseController.SetReadDeadline, Go 1.27), and a 100 MiB upload on
	// a homelab uplink outruns any whole-request ReadTimeout.
	if err := http.NewResponseController(w).SetReadDeadline(b.clk.Now().Add(b.cfg.UploadTimeout)); err != nil {
		b.log.Warn("set read deadline", "err", err)
	}
	s, ch, err := b.channel(r, PermAttachFiles)
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	// NV9: an interaction group has no channel_id, so it has no blob surface.
	if ch.Kind == ChannelCategory {
		server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodeForbidden,
			"a category holds no attachments"))
		return
	}
	blobID, err := server.PathDigest(r, "blob_id")
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	if tomb, err := b.repo.GetBlobTombstone(r.Context(), blobID); err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	} else if tomb {
		// Without this, content addressing undoes an admin purge: anyone holding
		// the ciphertext re-PUTs it and gets the same name back.
		server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodePruned,
			"these bytes were removed by the server operator"))
		return
	}
	used, err := b.repo.UserBlobBytes(r.Context(), s.UserID)
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	if b.cfg.QuotaBytesPerUser > 0 && used >= int64(b.cfg.QuotaBytesPerUser) {
		server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodeStorageFull,
			"your attachment quota is exhausted"))
		return
	}
	body := http.MaxBytesReader(w, r.Body, int64(b.cfg.MaxBlobBytes))
	n, created, err := b.store.Put(r.Context(), blobID, body, int64(b.cfg.MaxBlobBytes))
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, blob.ErrTooLarge):
		server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodeTooLarge,
			"at most %d bytes", b.cfg.MaxBlobBytes))
		return
	case errors.Is(err, blob.ErrHashMismatch):
		server.WriteError(w, server.WithStatus(http.StatusUnprocessableEntity,
			server.Errorf(server.CodeInvalidRequest, "the body does not hash to the requested blob_id")))
		return
	case err != nil:
		var mbe *http.MaxBytesError
		if errors.As(err, &mbe) {
			server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodeTooLarge,
				"at most %d bytes", b.cfg.MaxBlobBytes))
			return
		}
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	if b.cfg.QuotaBytesPerUser > 0 && used+n > int64(b.cfg.QuotaBytesPerUser) {
		if created {
			_ = b.store.Delete(blobID)
		}
		server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodeStorageFull,
			"your attachment quota is exhausted"))
		return
	}
	now := b.clk.Now().Unix()
	if err := b.repo.Tx(r.Context(), func(tx store.Repository) error {
		if created {
			if err := tx.PutBlob(r.Context(), store.BlobRow{
				BlobID: blobID, Size: uint64(n),
				StorageRef: blob.StorageRef(b.cfg.Backend, blobID), Created: now,
			}); err != nil {
				return err
			}
		} else if err := tx.ClearBlobUnreferenced(r.Context(), blobID); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		return tx.PutBlobRef(r.Context(), blobID, ch.ID, s.DeviceID, "", now)
	}); err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	status := http.StatusOK
	if created {
		status = http.StatusCreated
	}
	if err := server.EncodeBody(w, status, []any{blobID, uint64(n)}); err != nil {
		b.log.Error("encode blob put", "err", err)
	}
}
```

`get` (which also answers `HEAD`, because `ServeMux` routes `HEAD` to the `GET` handler) reads the
reference first and answers `404 E_NOT_FOUND` when there is none in *this* channel, then sets the
fixed header set and calls `http.ServeContent(w, r, "", time.Time{}, f)` — `Content-Type` set
**before** the call so `DetectContentType` never sniffs ciphertext, `name` empty so nothing is
inferred from an extension, and the zero `modtime` so no `Last-Modified` is emitted. That one call
buys `206`, `416`, `If-Range` and `304`.

The blob body is the one exception to §5.0's CBOR rule: raw octets,
`Content-Type: application/octet-stream` on the way in and out.

### Task 11: Blob deletion, reference counting, GC and admin purge

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/blob/gc.go`
- Modify: `internal/store/rows.go` (declare `store.BlobRefRow` — P2-D17, P2-D24)
- Create: `internal/api/admin.go`
- Modify: `internal/api/blobs.go` (the uploader-only `DELETE`)
- Modify: `cmd/dillad/serve.go` (start the sweeper)
- Test: `internal/blob/gc_test.go`
- Test: `internal/api/admin_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: task 10; `clock.Clock`; `config.Blobs.GCGrace`, `.GCInterval`, `.PendingTTL`.
- Produces:
  ```go
  // internal/blob
  type Sweeper struct{ ... }
  func NewSweeper(repo store.Repository, s *Store, clk clock.Clock, grace, interval time.Duration, log *slog.Logger) *Sweeper
  func (s *Sweeper) Run(ctx context.Context)          // ticks every interval until ctx is done
  func (s *Sweeper) SweepOnce(ctx context.Context) (deleted int, err error)
  // internal/api
  type Admin struct{ ... }
  func NewAdmin(repo store.Repository, bs *blob.Store, clk clock.Clock, log *slog.Logger) *Admin
  func (a *Admin) Register(mux *server.Mux)
  ```
  the uploader-only `DELETE /v1/channels/{cid}/blobs/{blob_id}`, `blob_tombstones`, and
  `DELETE /v1/admin/blobs/{blob_id}`.

**The rule this task exists to hold (gap-47 §0).** A channel never deletes a blob; it deletes a
**reference**. The file is unlinked by the sweeper, only when no reference remains anywhere and
`unref_since` is older than `gc_grace`. The grace window is what makes the check-then-unlink race
harmless: a forward that creates a reference between the `SELECT` and the `unlink` would otherwise
lose its bytes, and with a 24 h window that race needs a 24 h stall inside one sweeper pass.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/blob/gc_test.go`:

```go
package blob_test

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/blob"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/id"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/store"
)

func TestTheFileSurvivesWhileAnyReferenceStands(t *testing.T) {
	h := newGCHarness(t) // repo + blob.Store + clock.Fake + Sweeper with a 24h grace
	payload := bytes.Repeat([]byte("a"), 100)
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	chA, chB := h.NewChannel(), h.NewChannel()
	h.Upload(sum[:], payload, chA)
	h.Upload(sum[:], payload, chB)

	h.DeleteRef(sum[:], chA)
	h.Clock.Advance(48 * time.Hour)
	if n, err := h.Sweeper.SweepOnce(t.Context()); err != nil || n != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("SweepOnce deleted %d blobs while a reference stood (%v)", n, err)
	}
	if _, err := h.Store.Stat(sum[:]); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("the file was unlinked while referenced: %v", err)
	}

	h.DeleteRef(sum[:], chB)
	// Still inside the grace window.
	if n, _ := h.Sweeper.SweepOnce(t.Context()); n != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("SweepOnce deleted %d blobs inside the grace window", n)
	}
	h.Clock.Advance(25 * time.Hour)
	if n, err := h.Sweeper.SweepOnce(t.Context()); err != nil || n != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("SweepOnce = (%d, %v), want (1, nil)", n, err)
	}
	if _, err := h.Store.Stat(sum[:]); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("the unreferenced file survived the sweep")
	}
	if _, err := h.Repo.GetBlob(t.Context(), sum[:]); !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("the blobs row survived: %v", err)
	}
}

func TestAReferenceCreatedDuringTheGraceWindowSavesTheBlob(t *testing.T) {
	h := newGCHarness(t)
	payload := []byte("forwarded")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	chA, chB := h.NewChannel(), h.NewChannel()
	h.Upload(sum[:], payload, chA)
	h.DeleteRef(sum[:], chA)
	h.Clock.Advance(25 * time.Hour)
	// A forward lands after the grace window has passed but before the sweep.
	h.Upload(sum[:], payload, chB)
	if n, err := h.Sweeper.SweepOnce(t.Context()); err != nil || n != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("SweepOnce = (%d, %v); a re-referenced blob must not be collected", n, err)
	}
	if _, err := h.Store.Stat(sum[:]); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("the forwarded blob was unlinked: %v", err)
	}
}

func TestTheSweeperIsIdempotentAndBounded(t *testing.T) {
	h := newGCHarness(t)
	for i := range 300 {
		payload := []byte{byte(i), byte(i >> 8)}
		sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
		ch := h.NewChannel()
		h.Upload(sum[:], payload, ch)
		h.DeleteRef(sum[:], ch)
	}
	h.Clock.Advance(25 * time.Hour)
	first, err := h.Sweeper.SweepOnce(t.Context())
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SweepOnce: %v", err)
	}
	if first > 256 {
		t.Fatalf("SweepOnce collected %d blobs in one pass; the batch is capped at 256", first)
	}
	total := first
	for range 4 {
		n, err := h.Sweeper.SweepOnce(t.Context())
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("SweepOnce: %v", err)
		}
		total += n
	}
	if total != 300 {
		t.Fatalf("collected %d of 300", total)
	}
	if n, _ := h.Sweeper.SweepOnce(t.Context()); n != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("a sweep with nothing to do collected %d", n)
	}
}
```

```go
func TestDeleteRemovesOnlyThisChannelsReference(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok := blobEnv(t)
	other := secondChannel(t, e, ch, tok)
	payload := []byte("shared")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	for _, c := range []id.ID{ch, other} {
		if status, _ := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut,
			"/v1/channels/"+c.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok,
			"application/octet-stream", payload); status >= 300 {
			t.Fatalf("PUT into %x = %d", c, status)
		}
	}
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodDelete,
		"/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok, nil); status != http.StatusNoContent {
		t.Fatal("DELETE failed")
	}
	n, err := e.Repo.CountBlobRefs(t.Context(), sum[:])
	if err != nil || n != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("references = %d (%v), want 1", n, err)
	}
	// The other channel can still fetch it.
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodGet,
		"/v1/channels/"+other.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok, nil); status != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatal("the surviving reference could not fetch the blob")
	}
	// DELETE is idempotent.
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodDelete,
		"/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok, nil); status != http.StatusNoContent {
		t.Fatal("a repeated DELETE was not 204")
	}
}

// A moderator is a non-uploader too. Without this case the rule above is
// untested, because the non-uploader below holds only @everyone.
func TestDeletionByAModeratorIsAlsoRefused(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok := blobEnv(t)
	payload := []byte("mine")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	if status, _ := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut,
		"/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok,
		"application/octet-stream", payload); status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatal("PUT failed")
	}
	modTok := newModerator(t, e, ch) // holds PermManageMessages in this channel
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodDelete,
		"/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), modTok, nil)
	if status != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("DELETE by a moderator = %d, want 403", status)
	}
	if code := e.ErrCode(body); code != "E_NOT_UPLOADER" {
		t.Fatalf("code = %s, want E_NOT_UPLOADER", code)
	}
	if n, _ := e.Repo.CountBlobRefs(t.Context(), sum[:]); n != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("the reference count moved to %d", n)
	}
}

func TestDeletionByANonUploaderIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok := blobEnv(t)
	payload := []byte("mine")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	if status, _ := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut,
		"/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), tok,
		"application/octet-stream", payload); status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatal("PUT failed")
	}
	_, otherTok := e.NewUser("other")
	joinChannel(t, e, ch, otherTok)
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodDelete,
		"/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), otherTok, nil)
	if status != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("DELETE by a non-uploader = %d", status)
	}
	if code := e.ErrCode(body); code != "E_NOT_UPLOADER" {
		t.Fatalf("code = %s, want E_NOT_UPLOADER", code)
	}
}

func TestATombstonedBlobCannotBeReuploaded(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok := blobEnv(t)
	adminTok := e.NewInstanceAdmin("root")
	payload := []byte("takedown")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	hexID := hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
	if status, _ := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hexID, tok,
		"application/octet-stream", payload); status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatal("PUT failed")
	}
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodDelete, "/v1/admin/blobs/"+hexID, adminTok,
		[]any{"court order 42"}); status != http.StatusNoContent {
		t.Fatal("admin purge failed")
	}
	if n, _ := e.Repo.CountBlobRefs(t.Context(), sum[:]); n != 0 {
		t.Fatal("the purge left a reference")
	}
	if _, err := e.Blobs.Stat(sum[:]); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("the purge did not unlink the file")
	}
	status, body := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hexID, tok,
		"application/octet-stream", payload)
	if status != http.StatusGone {
		t.Fatalf("re-PUT of purged bytes = %d, want 410", status)
	}
	if code := e.ErrCode(body); code != "E_PRUNED" {
		t.Fatalf("code = %s", code)
	}
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodGet, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hexID, tok, nil); status != http.StatusGone {
		t.Fatal("GET of purged bytes was not 410")
	}
	// The purge is audited, naming the admin and the reason.
	rows, _ := e.Repo.ListAudit(t.Context(), 0, 50)
	var found bool
	for _, r := range rows {
		if r.Action == "blob.purge" && r.Target == hexID && r.Detail == "court order 42" {
			found = true
		}
	}
	if !found {
		t.Fatalf("no audit row for the purge: %+v", rows)
	}
}

func TestAPurgeByANonAdminIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok := blobEnv(t)
	payload := []byte("not yours")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	hexID := hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
	if status, _ := e.DoRaw(http.MethodPut, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/blobs/"+hexID, tok,
		"application/octet-stream", payload); status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatal("PUT failed")
	}
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodDelete, "/v1/admin/blobs/"+hexID, tok, []any{"because"})
	if status != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("purge by a non-admin = %d", status)
	}
	if code := e.ErrCode(body); code != "E_FORBIDDEN" {
		t.Fatalf("code = %s", code)
	}
}
```

`e.NewInstanceAdmin` creates a user whose `users.flags` has bit 0 set (NV8's layout, fixed by Plan
1a task 8) and returns its bearer token; `e.Blobs` exposes the harness's `*blob.Store`;
`joinChannel` adds a `channel_members` row.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the sweeper**

Create `internal/blob/gc.go`:

```go
package blob

import (
	"context"
	"log/slog"
	"time"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/clock"
	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/store"
)

// sweepBatch bounds one pass so the sweeper never holds the single SQLite write
// connection for an unbounded delete.
const sweepBatch = 256

type Sweeper struct {
	repo     store.Repository
	store    *Store
	clk      clock.Clock
	grace    time.Duration
	interval time.Duration
	log      *slog.Logger
}

func NewSweeper(repo store.Repository, s *Store, clk clock.Clock, grace, interval time.Duration, log *slog.Logger) *Sweeper {
	return &Sweeper{repo: repo, store: s, clk: clk, grace: grace, interval: interval, log: log}
}

// Run sweeps every interval until ctx is done. dillad serve starts it once.
func (s *Sweeper) Run(ctx context.Context) {
	t := s.clk.NewTimer(s.interval)
	defer t.Stop()
	for {
		select {
		case <-ctx.Done():
			return
		case <-t.C:
			if n, err := s.SweepOnce(ctx); err != nil {
				s.log.Error("blob sweep", "err", err)
			} else if n > 0 {
				s.log.Info("blob sweep", "deleted", n)
			}
			t.Reset(s.interval)
		}
	}
}

// SweepOnce unlinks at most sweepBatch blobs that have no reference and whose
// unref_since is older than the grace window. The row is deleted first, inside a
// transaction that re-checks for references; the file is unlinked after the
// commit, so a crash between the two leaves an orphan file (harmless, and
// dillad doctor reports it) rather than a row pointing at nothing.
func (s *Sweeper) SweepOnce(ctx context.Context) (int, error) {
	cutoff := s.clk.Now().Add(-s.grace).Unix()
	rows, err := s.repo.ListCollectableBlobs(ctx, cutoff, sweepBatch)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	var deleted int
	for _, row := range rows {
		var collect bool
		if err := s.repo.Tx(ctx, func(tx store.Repository) error {
			n, err := tx.CountBlobRefs(ctx, row.BlobID)
			if err != nil {
				return err
			}
			if n > 0 {
				// A reference appeared between the listing and now: clear the
				// mark and leave the bytes alone. This is the forward case.
				return tx.ClearBlobUnreferenced(ctx, row.BlobID)
			}
			collect = true
			return tx.DeleteBlob(ctx, row.BlobID)
		}); err != nil {
			return deleted, err
		}
		if !collect {
			continue
		}
		if err := s.store.Delete(row.BlobID); err != nil {
			return deleted, err
		}
		deleted++
	}
	return deleted, nil
}
```

`ListCollectableBlobs(ctx, before int64, limit int32)` is §4.1's; its query is

```sql
-- name: ListCollectableBlobs :many
SELECT blob_id, size, storage_ref, created, unref_since FROM blobs
WHERE unref_since IS NOT NULL AND unref_since < ?
  AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM blob_refs WHERE blob_refs.blob_id = blobs.blob_id)
ORDER BY unref_since
LIMIT ?;
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the uploader-only DELETE and the admin purge**

In `internal/api/blobs.go`:

```go
func (b *Blobs) delete(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	s, ch, err := b.channel(r, PermViewChannel)
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	blobID, err := server.PathDigest(r, "blob_id")
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	ref, err := b.repo.GetBlobRef(r.Context(), blobID, ch.ID)
	if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
		// Idempotent: deleting something that is already gone is a success.
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
		return
	}
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	// R29 and R34: at v1 only the uploading user may delete, from any of their
	// devices. There is NO moderator fallback — a channel's manage-messages holder
	// is refused here like anyone else. Moderator deletion of an attachment needs a
	// signed moderation event, which is follow-up card 2; letting PermManageMessages
	// drop another user's reference would ship exactly the capability R29 defers,
	// with no signed event and no audit row.
	dev, err := b.repo.GetDevice(r.Context(), ref.UploaderDevice)
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	if dev.UserID != s.UserID {
		server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodeNotUploader,
			"only the uploading user may delete this object"))
		return
	}
	now := b.clk.Now().Unix()
	if err := b.repo.Tx(r.Context(), func(tx store.Repository) error {
		if err := tx.DeleteBlobRef(r.Context(), blobID, ch.ID); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		// Sets unref_since only when no reference is left; the statement's own
		// NOT EXISTS makes it safe to call unconditionally.
		return tx.MarkBlobUnreferenced(r.Context(), blobID, now)
	}); err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
}
```

`GetBlobRef(ctx, blobID []byte, channelID id.ID) (BlobRefRow, error)` is **P2-D17**: §4.1 has
`PutBlobRef`, `DeleteBlobRef` and `CountBlobRefs` but no read, and both the uploader check and the
`GET`'s 404 rule need one.

Create `internal/api/admin.go`:

```go
package api

// requireInstanceAdmin is the gate on every /v1/admin route: users.flags bit 0.
func requireInstanceAdmin(ctx context.Context, repo store.Repository, userID id.ID) error {
	u, err := repo.GetUser(ctx, userID)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if u.Flags&flagInstanceAdmin == 0 {
		return server.Errorf(server.CodeForbidden, "instance admin only")
	}
	return nil
}

const (
	flagInstanceAdmin uint64 = 1 << 0 // NV8, fixed by Plan 1a task 8
	flagBotOperator   uint64 = 1 << 1
)

func (a *Admin) Register(mux *server.Mux) {
	mux.HandleFunc("DELETE /v1/admin/blobs/{blob_id}", a.purgeBlob)
	mux.HandleFunc("GET /v1/admin/audit", a.audit)
	mux.HandleFunc("POST /v1/admin/users/{id}/disable", a.disableUser)
	mux.HandleFunc("GET /v1/admin/diagnostics", a.diagnostics)
}

// purgeBlob removes every reference, unlinks immediately and writes a
// tombstone. The tombstone is not optional: without it, anyone still holding the
// ciphertext re-PUTs it and content addressing hands back the same name.
//
// And the caveat the host guide must carry in writing: every attachment is
// encrypted under a random per-attachment key, so the same file sent by someone
// else has a different blob_id. A purge removes BYTES, not CONTENT.
func (a *Admin) purgeBlob(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	s, ok := auth.SessionFrom(r.Context())
	if !ok {
		server.WriteError(w, errUnauthenticated())
		return
	}
	if err := requireInstanceAdmin(r.Context(), a.repo, s.UserID); err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	blobID, err := server.PathDigest(r, "blob_id")
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	var req struct {
		_      struct{} `cbor:",toarray"`
		Reason string
	}
	if err := server.DecodeBody(w, r, 64*1024, &req); err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	now := a.clk.Now().Unix()
	if err := a.repo.Tx(r.Context(), func(tx store.Repository) error {
		if err := tx.PutBlobTombstone(r.Context(), blobID, req.Reason, s.UserID, now); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		// DeleteAllBlobRefs returns (int64, error) — P2-D18 — so the count is
		// discarded explicitly; a single-value call does not compile.
		if _, err := tx.DeleteAllBlobRefs(r.Context(), blobID); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if err := tx.DeleteBlob(r.Context(), blobID); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
			return err
		}
		return tx.Audit(r.Context(), store.AuditRow{
			Actor: &s.UserID, Action: "blob.purge",
			Target: hex.EncodeToString(blobID), Detail: req.Reason, At: now,
		})
	}); err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	if err := a.store.Delete(blobID); err != nil {
		a.log.Error("unlink purged blob", "err", err)
	}
	w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
}
```

`DeleteAllBlobRefs(ctx, blobID []byte) (int64, error)` is **P2-D18**, needed only by the purge.
`GET` and `PUT` both consult `GetBlobTombstone` and answer `410 E_PRUNED`; task 10's `put` already
does, and `get` gains the same two lines.

`audit` requires instance admin and returns `ListAudit(since, limit)`; `disableUser` sets
`users.disabled_at` and, in the same transaction, `DeleteSessionsByUser` — protocol §2.2 point 6:
"Setting `users.disabled_at` does the same for every device of that user". `diagnostics` returns
the same report `dillad doctor` prints (task 15) as a CBOR array.

In `cmd/dillad/serve.go`, after the store and the blob store are open and before the listener:

```go
	sweeper := blob.NewSweeper(repo, blobStore, clk, cfg.Blobs.GCGraceDuration(),
		cfg.Blobs.GCIntervalDuration(), log)
	if n, err := blobStore.SweepTemp(); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("sweep temp uploads: %w", err)

		log.Info("removed interrupted uploads", "count", n)
	}
	go sweeper.Run(ctx)
```

`GCGraceDuration` and `GCIntervalDuration` are the `time.ParseDuration` accessors §6.4 requires,
because the TOML fields are strings — a `time.Duration` field would decode a TOML integer as
nanoseconds.

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `store.VacuumInto(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB, path string) error` (Plan 1a task 3)
  and `store.DumpPostgres`, whose signature **this task changes** — see P2-D33 below;
  `config.Config`; `store.Repository.SchemaVersion`; `blob.StorageRef`;
  `ops.AcquireSharedLock` (task 13).

> **P2-D33.** Plan 1a task 3 produces
> `func DumpPostgres(ctx context.Context, dsn string, w io.Writer) error`. It takes a **DSN**, so it
> opens its own connection and the caller cannot wrap it in a transaction — which makes step 3's
> stated consistency guarantee ("one `sql.TxOptions{Isolation: sql.LevelRepeatableRead, ReadOnly:
> true}` transaction, so the per-table `COPY TO`s see one snapshot") unimplementable. The signature
> becomes
> `func DumpPostgres(ctx context.Context, tx *sql.Tx, w io.Writer) error`,
> and `Backup` opens the repeatable-read read-only transaction, reads `generation` inside it and
> passes it in. Record it against §1's `internal/store/dump.go` row; Plan 1a's own test changes with
> it, and the three call sites are this task and Plan 1a's `dump_test.go`.
- Produces:
  ```go
  // internal/ops
  type Manifest struct {
      FormatVersion int            `json:"format_version"`
      DillaVersion  string         `json:"dilla_version"`
      SchemaVersion int64          `json:"schema_version"`
      Engine        string         `json:"engine"`
      Generation    uint64         `json:"generation"`
      CreatedAt     string         `json:"created_at"`
      BlobCount     int            `json:"blob_count"`
      TotalBytes    int64          `json:"total_bytes"`
      Entries       []Entry        `json:"entries"`
  }
  type Entry struct { Path string `json:"path"`; Size int64 `json:"size"`; SHA256 string `json:"sha256"` }
  type BackupOptions struct { Out io.Writer; IncludeBlobs bool; Label string; AllowGaps bool }
  func Backup(ctx context.Context, cfg *config.Config, repo store.Repository, o BackupOptions) (Manifest, error)
  func Verify(ctx context.Context, r io.Reader) (Manifest, error)
  const FormatVersion = 1
  ```
  `dillad backup [--out=PATH|-] [--include-blobs] [--label=TEXT] [--allow-gaps]` and
  `dillad backup verify --from=PATH`.

**The archive, in its fixed order.** The order is part of the format, not an implementation detail:
it is what makes `restore` streamable and two backups of an unchanged instance byte-identical.

```
dilla-backup/
  MANIFEST.json                 # first entry, always; carries every other entry's sha256
  db/dilla.sqlite               # VACUUM INTO output — or db/dump/<table>.copy for Postgres,
                                # tables in lexicographic order
  config/dilla.toml
  keys/instance.json            # external-sender key history, franking key id, ACME account key
  blobs/<aa>/<bb>/<blob_id>     # lexicographic by the full relative path
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/ops/archive_test.go`:

```go
package ops_test

import (
	"archive/tar"
	"bytes"
	"compress/gzip"
	"io"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/ops"
)

func TestSQLiteBackupIsConsistentWithAReaderOpen(t *testing.T) {
	h := newOpsHarness(t) // a migrated SQLite instance with two blobs and a reader pool open
	h.SeedRows(100)
	var buf bytes.Buffer
	man, err := ops.Backup(t.Context(), h.Cfg, h.Repo, ops.BackupOptions{Out: &buf, IncludeBlobs: true})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Backup: %v", err)
	}
	if man.Engine != "sqlite" {
		t.Fatalf("engine = %q", man.Engine)
	}
	if man.SchemaVersion != h.SchemaVersion(t) {
		t.Fatalf("manifest schema_version = %d, want %d", man.SchemaVersion, h.SchemaVersion(t))
	}
	names := tarNames(t, buf.Bytes())
	want := []string{"dilla-backup/MANIFEST.json", "dilla-backup/db/dilla.sqlite",
		"dilla-backup/config/dilla.toml", "dilla-backup/keys/instance.json"}
	for i, w := range want {
		if names[i] != w {
			t.Fatalf("entry %d = %q, want %q", i, names[i], w)
		}
	}
	// The restored database opens and holds the rows.
	if n := h.RowsInArchivedDB(t, buf.Bytes()); n != 100 {
		t.Fatalf("archived database holds %d rows, want 100", n)
	}
}

// Renamed from TestTheArchiveIsByteIdenticalForAnUnchangedInstance, which
// promised more than it checked: MANIFEST.json carries created_at, which this
// test advances by an hour, so the streams cannot be byte-identical. What the
// format actually guarantees is that no HOST state reaches the archive, and that
// is what is asserted here. TestTwoBackupsWithTheClockHeldAreIdentical below
// carries the byte-for-byte half.
func TestTheArchiveCarriesNoHostState(t *testing.T) {
	h := newOpsHarness(t)
	h.SeedRows(10)
	var a, b bytes.Buffer
	if _, err := ops.Backup(t.Context(), h.Cfg, h.Repo, ops.BackupOptions{Out: &a, IncludeBlobs: true}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first Backup: %v", err)
	}
	h.Clock.Advance(time.Hour) // only the manifest's created_at moves
	if _, err := ops.Backup(t.Context(), h.Cfg, h.Repo, ops.BackupOptions{Out: &b, IncludeBlobs: true}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second Backup: %v", err)
	}
	na, nb := tarNames(t, a.Bytes()), tarNames(t, b.Bytes())
	if !slices.Equal(na, nb) {
		t.Fatalf("entry order moved:\n%v\n%v", na, nb)
	}
	ha, hb := tarHeaders(t, a.Bytes()), tarHeaders(t, b.Bytes())
	for i := range ha {
		if ha[i].ModTime != hb[i].ModTime || ha[i].Uid != hb[i].Uid || ha[i].Gid != hb[i].Gid ||
			ha[i].Uname != hb[i].Uname || ha[i].Gname != hb[i].Gname || ha[i].Format != tar.FormatPAX {
			t.Fatalf("entry %q carries host state: %+v", ha[i].Name, ha[i])
		}
	}
}

func TestTwoBackupsWithTheClockHeldAreIdentical(t *testing.T) {
	h := newOpsHarness(t)
	h.SeedRows(10)
	var a, b bytes.Buffer
	// The clock does NOT move, so created_at is the same and the gzip streams must
	// match byte for byte. This is the property the fixed entry order, the zeroed
	// tar headers and the zeroed gzip header exist for.
	if _, err := ops.Backup(t.Context(), h.Cfg, h.Repo, ops.BackupOptions{Out: &a, IncludeBlobs: true}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first Backup: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := ops.Backup(t.Context(), h.Cfg, h.Repo, ops.BackupOptions{Out: &b, IncludeBlobs: true}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second Backup: %v", err)
	}
	if !bytes.Equal(a.Bytes(), b.Bytes()) {
		t.Fatalf("two backups of an unchanged instance differ (%d vs %d bytes)", a.Len(), b.Len())
	}
}

func TestVerifyDetectsATruncatedMemberAWrongDigestAndASchemaMismatch(t *testing.T) {
	h := newOpsHarness(t)
	h.SeedRows(5)
	var good bytes.Buffer
	if _, err := ops.Backup(t.Context(), h.Cfg, h.Repo, ops.BackupOptions{Out: &good, IncludeBlobs: true}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Backup: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := ops.Verify(t.Context(), bytes.NewReader(good.Bytes())); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Verify of a good archive: %v", err)
	}
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name string
		mut  func([]byte) []byte
		want string
	}{
		{"truncated member", truncateLastMember, "short"},
		{"wrong digest", flipOneByteOfTheDatabase, "sha256"},
		{"schema newer than the binary", bumpManifestSchema, "schema"},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			_, err := ops.Verify(t.Context(), bytes.NewReader(tc.mut(slices.Clone(good.Bytes()))))
			if err == nil {
				t.Fatal("Verify accepted a damaged archive")
			}
			if !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tc.want) {
				t.Fatalf("error %q does not name %q", err, tc.want)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestTheArchiveHoldsNoEndToEndPlaintext(t *testing.T) {
	h := newOpsHarness(t)
	// A marker that would only be present if an envelope were stored decrypted.
	const marker = "PLAINTEXT-CANARY-8f31"
	h.SeedE2EEMessageWithPlaintext(marker) // stores the marker as MLS ciphertext only
	var buf bytes.Buffer
	if _, err := ops.Backup(t.Context(), h.Cfg, h.Repo, ops.BackupOptions{Out: &buf, IncludeBlobs: true}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Backup: %v", err)
	}
	gz, err := gzip.NewReader(bytes.NewReader(buf.Bytes()))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("gzip: %v", err)
	}
	all, err := io.ReadAll(gz)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read: %v", err)
	}
	if bytes.Contains(all, []byte(marker)) {
		t.Fatal("the archive contains end-to-end-encrypted plaintext")
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the archive writer**

Create `internal/ops/archive.go`. The determinism rules are the whole point of the file, so they
are stated where they are enforced:

```go
// Package ops holds dillad's operator-facing archive and diagnostics. The
// archive format is fixed: entry order is part of it, because that is what makes
// restore streamable and two backups of an unchanged instance comparable.
package ops

import (
	"archive/tar"
	"compress/gzip"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/hex"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"os"
	"path"
	"slices"
	"time"
)

const FormatVersion = 1

// epoch is the ModTime every header carries. FileInfoHeader would copy the
// host's mtime, uid and gid into the archive; none of that is instance state.
var epoch = time.Unix(0, 0).UTC()

// writeEntry appends one member with a fully deterministic header. Format is set
// explicitly to PAX: left unspecified, archive/tar picks the first of USTAR, PAX,
// GNU that can encode the header, so the format changes silently the first time
// a path exceeds 100 bytes.
func writeEntry(tw *tar.Writer, name string, mode int64, body []byte) (Entry, error) {
	if err := tw.WriteHeader(&tar.Header{
		Typeflag: tar.TypeReg,
		Name:     name,
		Size:     int64(len(body)),
		Mode:     mode,
		ModTime:  epoch,
		Uid:      0,
		Gid:      0,
		Uname:    "",
		Gname:    "",
		Format:   tar.FormatPAX,
	}); err != nil {
		return Entry{}, err
	}
	if _, err := tw.Write(body); err != nil {
		return Entry{}, err
	}
	sum := sha256.Sum256(body)
	return Entry{Path: name, Size: int64(len(body)), SHA256: hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])}, nil
}
```

`Backup` runs in this order, and the order is load-bearing:

1. Read `SchemaVersion` and refuse with `exit.Config` if it exceeds the highest embedded migration —
   the mirror of `serve`'s "refuse to start on a newer schema".
2. Take the **database snapshot first** and read `generation` inside the same read transaction.
   SQLite: `store.VacuumInto` into a temp file (`VACUUM INTO` takes a read lock and is consistent
   with WAL on). Postgres: `Backup` opens the transaction itself —
   `db.BeginTx(ctx, &sql.TxOptions{Isolation: sql.LevelRepeatableRead, ReadOnly: true})` — reads
   `generation` through it, and passes the `*sql.Tx` to `store.DumpPostgres` (**P2-D33**: the
   signature takes a `*sql.Tx`, not a DSN, precisely so the caller can do this), so the per-table
   `COPY TO`s and the `generation` read all see one snapshot; otherwise `mls_handshakes` and
   `mls_groups` can disagree about the epoch and the archive is inconsistent with itself.
3. Write `MANIFEST.json` **first into the tar**, although it carries every other entry's `sha256`.
   That is a **two-pass** design, and it is the only one: a self-hosted instance's database plus its
   blob tree does not fit in memory, so nothing is buffered. Pass one walks the members in the fixed
   order and computes each one's size and SHA-256 (both are needed for the manifest, and `size` is
   needed for the tar header, which must be written before the body); pass two writes the manifest
   as member 0 and then copies each member's bytes through. The database snapshot is already on disk
   in a temp file, so both passes read it from there. The streaming twin of `writeEntry`:

   ```go
   // writeEntryFrom writes one member by copying size bytes from r, tee-ing them
   // through SHA-256 as they go. The digest it returns is of the bytes actually
   // written, so a file that changed between the two passes is caught by Verify
   // rather than silently archived under a stale digest.
   func writeEntryFrom(tw *tar.Writer, name string, mode, size int64, r io.Reader) (Entry, error) {
   	if err := tw.WriteHeader(&tar.Header{
   		Typeflag: tar.TypeReg, Name: name, Size: size, Mode: mode,
   		ModTime: epoch, Uid: 0, Gid: 0, Uname: "", Gname: "", Format: tar.FormatPAX,
   	}); err != nil {
   		return Entry{}, err
   	}
   	h := sha256.New()
   	n, err := io.Copy(tw, io.TeeReader(io.LimitReader(r, size), h))
   	if err != nil {
   		return Entry{}, err
   	}
   	if n != size {
   		return Entry{}, fmt.Errorf("ops: %s: wrote %d bytes, header says %d", name, n, size)
   	}
   	return Entry{Path: name, Size: size, SHA256: hex.EncodeToString(h.Sum(nil))}, nil
   }
   ```

   `writeEntry` stays for the three small members whose bytes the process already holds — the
   manifest, `config/dilla.toml` and `keys/instance.json` — and `writeEntryFrom` carries the
   database snapshot and every blob.
4. Walk `blobs` rows ordered by `blob_id`, skipping and **counting** any blob created after the
   snapshot. A blob the snapshot does not reference is dead weight; a referenced blob that appeared
   afterwards is a real gap the manifest names. Exit non-zero if `gaps > 0` unless `--allow-gaps`.
5. gzip with `gz.Name = ""`, `gz.ModTime = time.Time{}`, `gz.Comment = ""`, `gz.Extra = nil` and
   `gzip.BestSpeed` — the payload is ciphertext and barely compresses, so the CPU is better spent
   elsewhere. All four non-`OS` header fields are written into the stream and must be zeroed for a
   byte-reproducible archive.

`Verify` re-reads the archive, checks that `MANIFEST.json` is member 0, recomputes every entry's
`sha256`, refuses a `schema_version` newer than the binary, and returns a named error for each of
the three failures the test drives.

The sweeper interaction is a real bug if ignored: a concurrent orphan sweep can unlink a blob the
snapshot still references, and the walk then reports a false gap. Two guards, both **implemented**,
not merely stated:

1. **`Backup` takes a SHARED lock**, `ops.AcquireSharedLock` (`LOCK_SH|LOCK_NB`, task 13), not the
   exclusive one. `serve` and `restore` take `LOCK_EX`, so a backup can run against a live instance
   — which is the whole point of a nightly backup — while a restore cannot start underneath one.
   An earlier draft had `Backup` take the same exclusive lock, which would have made backing up a
   running server impossible.
2. **The sweeper honours `last_backup_started`.** `Backup` writes
   `PutSetting(ctx, "last_backup_started", strconv.FormatInt(now, 10))` **before** taking the
   database snapshot, and task 11's `Sweeper.SweepOnce` reads it:

   ```go
   func (s *Sweeper) SweepOnce(ctx context.Context) (int, error) {
   	cutoff := s.clk.Now().Add(-s.grace).Unix()
   	// A backup in flight pins the cutoff to its start: a blob that lost its last
   	// reference after the snapshot was taken is still referenced BY the snapshot,
   	// and collecting it would put a gap in an archive that claims to be complete.
   	if raw, err := s.repo.GetSetting(ctx, "last_backup_started"); err == nil {
   		if started, perr := strconv.ParseInt(raw, 10, 64); perr == nil && started < cutoff {
   			cutoff = started
   		}
   	} else if !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
   		return 0, err
   	}
   	rows, err := s.repo.ListCollectableBlobs(ctx, cutoff, sweepBatch)
   	// … unchanged …
   ```

   with `TestASweepDuringABackupCollectsNothing` in `internal/blob/gc_test.go`: set
   `last_backup_started` to now, unreference a blob, advance the clock past the grace window, sweep,
   and assert `0` collected and the file still present. Task 11's step 3 gains the two lines and the
   test; this task writes the setting.

```go
package ops_test

import (
	"errors"
	"os/exec"
	"testing"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/ops"
)

func TestTheLockIsExclusiveWithinOneProcess(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	l, err := ops.AcquireLock(dir)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("AcquireLock: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := ops.AcquireLock(dir); !errors.Is(err, ops.ErrLocked) {
		t.Fatalf("second AcquireLock = %v, want ErrLocked", err)
	}
	if err := l.Release(); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Release: %v", err)
	}
	l2, err := ops.AcquireLock(dir)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("AcquireLock after Release: %v", err)
	}
	_ = l2.Release()
}

// flock is per open file description, so the cross-process case is the one that
// matters and it needs a second process.
func TestTheLockIsExclusiveAcrossProcesses(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	l, err := ops.AcquireLock(dir)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("AcquireLock: %v", err)
	}
	defer l.Release()
	cmd := exec.Command(os.Args[0], "-test.run=TestLockHelperProcess")
	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "DILLA_LOCK_HELPER="+dir)
	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
	if err == nil {
		t.Fatalf("the helper acquired the lock: %s", out)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(string(out), "ErrLocked") {
		t.Fatalf("helper output = %s", out)
	}
}

func TestLockHelperProcess(t *testing.T) {
	dir := os.Getenv("DILLA_LOCK_HELPER")
	if dir == "" {
		t.Skip("not the helper")
	}
	if _, err := ops.AcquireLock(dir); errors.Is(err, ops.ErrLocked) {
		fmt.Println("ErrLocked")
		os.Exit(1)
	}
	os.Exit(0)
}
```

```go
func TestRestoreBumpsTheGenerationAndArmsTheHeal(t *testing.T) {

	code, stderr := h.Run(t, "restore", "--config="+h.ConfigPath, "--from="+archive)
	if code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("restore exit = %d: %s", code, stderr)
	}
	if after := h.Generation(t); after != before+1 {
		t.Fatalf("generation = %d, want %d", after, before+1)
	}
	for _, g := range h.Groups(t) {
		if g.EpochUnknown != 1 {
			t.Fatalf("group %x is not epoch-unknown after restore", g.GroupID)
		}
		if g.HealDeadline == nil || *g.HealDeadline != h.Now()+24*3600 {
			t.Fatalf("group %x heal deadline = %v, want now+24h", g.GroupID, g.HealDeadline)
		}
	}
	kept, purged := h.CountKeyPackages(t)
	if purged != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("%d non-last-resort KeyPackages survived the restore", purged)
	}
	if kept == 0 {
		t.Fatal("the last-resort KeyPackages were purged too")
	}
	if h.LiveCalls(t) != 0 {
		t.Fatal("a live call survived the restore")
	}
	// The operator is told exactly what happens next.
	if !strings.Contains(stderr, "24h") || !strings.Contains(stderr, "live calls ended") {
		t.Fatalf("restore did not print the operator summary: %q", stderr)
	}
}

// The migration set is INJECTED, not embedded: §4.6 embeds the migrations
// directory with //go:embed *.sql at compile time, so a test cannot add a file to
// it at run time. sqlite.MigrateFS(ctx, db, fsys) is added by this task and takes
// the fs.FS; sqlite.Migrate keeps its signature and calls MigrateFS with the
// embedded FS, so serve and every other caller are unchanged. Record the added
// signature as a deviation against §4.6 beside P2-D19.
func TestAFailedMigrationAtServeRestoresThePreMigrationBackup(t *testing.T) {
	h := newCLIHarness(t)
	h.SeedRows(t, 20)

	h.UseMigrations(t, brokenMigrationFS(t))
	code, stderr := h.Run(t, "serve", "--config="+h.ConfigPath, "--listen=127.0.0.1:0")
	if code == 0 {

}
```
