- [ ] **Step 3: Write the lock**

Create `internal/ops/lock.go`:

```go
package ops

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"syscall"
)

// ErrLocked is returned when another dillad holds the data directory.
var ErrLocked = errors.New("ops: the data directory is in use by another dillad")

// Lock is an exclusive advisory lock on <dir>/dilla.lock. syscall.Flock is
// stdlib, so no dependency is added; the lock is per open file description and
// is released by Release or by the process exiting.
type Lock struct{ f *os.File }

func AcquireLock(dir string) (*Lock, error) {
	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o700); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	f, err := os.OpenFile(filepath.Join(dir, "dilla.lock"), os.O_RDWR|os.O_CREATE, 0o600)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if err := syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX|syscall.LOCK_NB); err != nil {
		_ = f.Close()
		if errors.Is(err, syscall.EWOULDBLOCK) {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", ErrLocked, dir)
		}
		return nil, err
	}
	return &Lock{f: f}, nil
}

func (l *Lock) Release() error {
	if l == nil || l.f == nil {
		return nil
	}
	err := syscall.Flock(int(l.f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_UN)
	if cerr := l.f.Close(); err == nil {
		err = cerr
	}
	l.f = nil
	return err
}
```

`serve` and `restore` take the **exclusive** lock; `backup` takes a **shared** one, so a nightly
backup of a live instance is possible — which it would not be if `backup` waited on the lock `serve`
holds for the life of the process. The blob sweeper runs inside `serve` and is covered by its lock.
`doctor` takes none, because it must be runnable against a live instance.

```go
// AcquireSharedLock takes LOCK_SH|LOCK_NB. Several shared holders coexist; an
// exclusive holder excludes them all and is excluded by any of them.
func AcquireSharedLock(dir string) (*Lock, error) { /* … LOCK_SH in place of LOCK_EX … */ }
```

`internal/ops/lock_test.go` gains `TestBackupAndServeHoldTheDirectoryTogether`: take `LOCK_EX`,
assert a shared acquire fails; release; take `LOCK_SH` twice, assert both succeed; assert an
exclusive acquire then fails, which is the `restore`-during-`backup` refusal.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the restore**

Add `Restore` to `internal/ops/archive.go`. The order is the contract:

1. `AcquireLock(cfg.Instance.DataDir)` — fail with `exit.TempFail` and the message "the data
   directory is in use; stop dillad first" when `serve` holds it.
2. `Verify` the whole archive **before writing anything**: manifest first, every `sha256` checked.
3. Refuse when `MANIFEST.schema_version` is newer than the binary's highest embedded migration,
   with `exit.Config` and both numbers in the message — the mirror of `serve`'s refusal.
4. `--dry-run` prints the plan (per-table row counts from the archived database, blobs to restore,
   generation before and after) and returns without writing.
5. Write into a sibling temp directory `<data_dir>.restore-<8 hex>`, then **swap in three steps**,
   because `os.Rename` fails with `ENOTEMPTY` when the destination is an existing non-empty
   directory — which a live data directory always is:

   ```go
   old := dataDir + ".old-" + hex8()
   if err := os.Rename(dataDir, old); err != nil {           // 1. move the live one aside
   	return err
   }
   if err := os.Rename(tmpDir, dataDir); err != nil {        // 2. move the restored one in
   	_ = os.Rename(old, dataDir)                            //    best-effort undo
   	return err
   }
   // 3. The exclusive lock from point 1 is held on <old>/dilla.lock, whose
   // directory is no longer the data directory: without re-acquiring, a second
   // dillad could start against the restored directory immediately. Release the
   // stale lock and take a fresh one on the new inode before doing anything else.
   _ = lock.Release()
   lock, err = AcquireLock(dataDir)
   if err != nil {
   	return err
   }
   ```

   `<old>` is left on disk and its path is printed, so an operator can inspect or remove it; a
   `--remove-old` flag deletes it. `cmd/dillad/restore_test.go` gains
   `TestASecondDilladStillCannotStartAfterARestore`, which runs a restore and then asserts that
   `ops.AcquireLock(dataDir)` returns `ErrLocked` — the assertion that catches a lock left on an
   orphaned inode.
6. Open the restored database and, **in one transaction**: `BumpGeneration`, set every
   `mls_groups` row `epoch_unknown = 1` and `heal_deadline = now + 24h`, `PurgeKeyPackages(true)`
   (keeping last resort), clear `mls_pending_proposals`, and end every live `voice_sessions` row.
7. Call `ds.OnRestore(ctx, generation)` when a delivery service is attached — `dillad restore` runs
   without one, so the CLI path performs steps 6's SQL itself through `store.Repository` and
   `serve` calls `OnRestore` on its next start, which is what makes the two paths converge.
8. Print exactly what the operator will see: live calls ended, clients will resync, groups with no
   member-signed GroupInfo within **24 h** are closed and re-created by the channel owner's device.

Two store methods this needs and §4.1 does not have — **P2-D19**:
`MarkAllGroupsEpochUnknown(ctx context.Context, healDeadline int64) (int64, error)` and
`EndAllVoiceSessions(ctx context.Context, at int64) (int64, error)`. §4.3 declares
`mls_groups.epoch_unknown`, `mls_groups.heal_deadline` and `voice_sessions.ended`, and invariant 11
requires all of them to move at once; doing it row by row from the CLI would need a full group
listing for no benefit.

- [ ] **Step 5: Roll back a failed migration in `serve`**

In `cmd/dillad/serve.go`, around the migrate-at-start step:

```go
	backupPath := filepath.Join(cfg.Instance.DataDir, "pre-migration.db")
	if cfg.DB.PreMigrationBackup {
		if err := store.VacuumInto(ctx, writeDB, backupPath); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("pre-migration backup: %w", err)
		}
	}
	if err := sqlitestore.Migrate(ctx, writeDB); err != nil {
		if cfg.DB.PreMigrationBackup {
			if rerr := ops.RestoreFile(backupPath, cfg.DB.Path); rerr != nil {
				return fmt.Errorf("migration failed (%v) and the rollback failed too: %w", err, rerr)
			}
			log.Error("migration failed; restored the pre-migration backup; the group heal protocol is pending",
				"err", err)
		}
		return exit.Data
	}
```

`ops.RestoreFile` closes the pool, renames the backup over the database file and re-opens — the
"migration failure restores the pre-migration backup and exits non-zero with the heal protocol
pending" rule the spec's operational section states.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the two scenarios**

Create `testkit/scenarios/restore_heal_from_tail.dsl`:

```
# Invariant 11, the happy path: after a restore the instance holds a state blob,
# a member uploads its GroupInfo and its handshake tail, and the group heals.
ds
instance i1
client alice
client bob
group g1 alice
join g1 bob
sync
send alice g1 "before the restore"
expect_decrypts bob g1 "before the restore"
snapshot s1
restore_snapshot s1
# Every response now carries the new generation and the group is epoch-unknown.
expect_frame hello generation=2
heal g1 alice
sync
send bob g1 "after the heal"
expect_decrypts alice g1 "after the heal"
```

Create `testkit/scenarios/restore_force_recreate.dsl`:

```
# Invariant 11, the unhappy path: no heal arrives within 24 hours, so the group
# is closed and re-created by the channel owner's device.
ds
instance i1
client alice
client bob
group g1 alice
join g1 bob
sync
snapshot s1
restore_snapshot s1
go_offline alice
go_offline bob
advance_clock 25h
go_online alice
expect_reject alice g1 status=404 code=E_NOT_FOUND
group g2 alice
join g2 bob
sync
send alice g2 "re-created"
expect_decrypts bob g2 "re-created"
```

`heal <group> <client>` is the one verb beyond §7.2's list that these two need; it is sugar over
`GET`+`POST /v1/groups/{id}/heal` and is **P2-D20**, recorded with `join_storm` in `protocol/02`'s
testkit note.

### Task 14: The admin CLI

**Files:**
- Create: `cmd/dillad/admin.go`
- Modify: `cmd/dillad/main.go` (`admin` stops being a reserved verb)
- Modify: `internal/gateway/heartbeat.go` (close a connection whose session row is gone, code 4401)
- Test: `cmd/dillad/admin_test.go`
- Test: `internal/gateway/heartbeat_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `store.Repository` in full; `auth.NewInviteCode`, `auth.Sessions.RevokeUser`,
  `auth.Sessions.RevokeDevice`; `exit.Code`; tasks 10–11's `blob.Store`.
- Produces: `admin user list|show|disable|enable|delete`, `admin invite create|list|revoke`,
  `admin community list|show`, `admin device revoke`, `admin blob purge`, `admin audit`.

**Shape.** `dillad admin <noun> <verb> [flags]`. Every noun has its own `flag.FlagSet` carrying
`--config`; an unknown noun or verb prints the usage block to **stderr** and exits **2**. `--help`
goes to **stdout** and exits **0**. `flag.ContinueOnError` already prints the message and the usage
block, so the handler must not print them a second time.

```go
package main

import (
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/exit"
)

func TestEveryNounVerbPairDispatches(t *testing.T) {
	h := newCLIHarness(t)
	uid := h.SeedUser(t, "alice")
	cid := h.SeedCommunity(t, uid)
	did := h.SeedDevice(t, uid)

	cases := []struct {
		args []string
		want exit.Code
		in   string // a substring that must appear in stdout
	}{
		{[]string{"admin", "user", "list"}, exit.OK, "alice"},
		{[]string{"admin", "user", "show", "--id=" + uid.String()}, exit.OK, uid.String()},
		{[]string{"admin", "user", "disable", "--id=" + uid.String()}, exit.OK, "disabled"},
		{[]string{"admin", "user", "enable", "--id=" + uid.String()}, exit.OK, "enabled"},
		{[]string{"admin", "invite", "create", "--max-uses=3", "--ttl=24h"}, exit.OK, "https://"},
		{[]string{"admin", "invite", "list"}, exit.OK, "max_uses"},
		{[]string{"admin", "community", "list"}, exit.OK, cid.String()},
		{[]string{"admin", "community", "show", "--id=" + cid.String()}, exit.OK, "policy_version"},
		{[]string{"admin", "device", "revoke", "--id=" + did.String()}, exit.OK, "revoked"},
		{[]string{"admin", "audit"}, exit.OK, "action"},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(strings.Join(tc.args, " "), func(t *testing.T) {
			code, stdout := h.Run(t, append(tc.args, "--config="+h.ConfigPath)...)
			if exit.Code(code) != tc.want {
				t.Fatalf("exit = %d, want %d: %s", code, tc.want, stdout)
			}
			if !strings.Contains(stdout, tc.in) {
				t.Fatalf("stdout does not contain %q: %s", tc.in, stdout)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestAnUnknownNounOrVerbExits2WithUsage(t *testing.T) {
	h := newCLIHarness(t)
	for _, args := range [][]string{
		{"admin"},
		{"admin", "nosuchnoun", "list"},
		{"admin", "user", "nosuchverb"},
	} {
		code, _, stderr := h.Run3(t, append(args, "--config="+h.ConfigPath)...)
		if code != int(exit.Usage) {
			t.Fatalf("%v: exit = %d, want 2", args, code)
		}
		if !strings.Contains(stderr, "usage: dillad admin") {
			t.Fatalf("%v: stderr = %q", args, stderr)
		}
	}
	// --help is not an error.
	code, stdout, stderr := h.Run3(t, "admin", "--help")
	if code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("--help exit = %d", code)
	}
	if stdout == "" || stderr != "" {
		t.Fatalf("--help wrote stdout=%q stderr=%q; it must write only stdout", stdout, stderr)
	}
}

func TestInviteCreatePrintsTheLinkOnceAndStoresOnlyTheHash(t *testing.T) {
	h := newCLIHarness(t)
	code, stdout := h.Run(t, "admin", "invite", "create", "--max-uses=1", "--ttl=24h", "--config="+h.ConfigPath)
	if code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("exit = %d", code)
	}
	links := strings.Count(stdout, "/i/")
	if links != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("the link was printed %d times, want once", links)
	}
	// A DISTINCT name: `code` is the int exit status returned by h.Run, and
	// assigning a string to it does not compile. SplitN(…, 2)[1] is everything
	// after the first "/i/"; the first line of that is the code, whatever the CLI
	// prints afterwards.
	inviteCode := strings.TrimSpace(strings.SplitN(strings.SplitN(stdout, "/i/", 2)[1], "\n", 2)[0])
	if inviteCode == "" {
		t.Fatalf("no invite code in stdout: %q", stdout)
	}
	rows := h.Invites(t)
	if len(rows) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("invites = %d", len(rows))
	}
	if bytes.Contains(h.RawDB(t), []byte(inviteCode)) {
		t.Fatal("the plaintext invite code reached the database")
	}
	if !bytes.Equal(rows[0].CodeHash, auth.InviteHash(inviteCode)) {
		t.Fatal("the stored hash is not SHA-256 of the printed code")
	}
	// It is not in the log either.
	if strings.Contains(h.LogOutput(t), inviteCode) {
		t.Fatal("the plaintext invite code reached the log")
	}
}

// dillad admin is a SEPARATE PROCESS from dillad serve: it holds no handle on the
// running server's gateway registry and cannot close a socket, let alone "in the
// same transaction". What it can do is write the rows, and what serve does is
// notice. The mechanism, stated so the test can assert it rather than assert a
// harness artefact:
//
//   - the CLI sets users.disabled_at and deletes every session of that user, in
//     one transaction;
//   - the gateway re-checks the session's liveness on every heartbeat (§6.1's
//     heartbeat interval, default 30 s) by reading the session row, and closes the
//     connection with close code 4401 when it is gone.
//
// So a live socket survives for at most one heartbeat, which the test asserts by
// driving the heartbeat rather than by waiting.
func TestUserDisableDeletesSessionsAndTheGatewayDropsTheSocket(t *testing.T) {
	h := newCLIHarness(t)
	uid := h.SeedUser(t, "alice")
	tok := h.SeedSession(t, uid)
	conn := h.OpenGatewayConn(t, uid) // a real gateway session against a real serve
	if code, _ := h.Run(t, "admin", "user", "disable", "--id="+uid.String(), "--config="+h.ConfigPath); code != 0 {
		t.Fatal("disable failed")
	}
	if _, err := h.Repo(t).GetSessionByHash(t.Context(), auth.TokenHash(tok), h.Now()); !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
		t.Fatalf("the session survived: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := h.Repo(t).GetUser(t.Context(), uid); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("GetUser: %v", err)
	} else if u, _ := h.Repo(t).GetUser(t.Context(), uid); u.DisabledAt == nil {
		t.Fatal("disabled_at was not set")
	}
	// One heartbeat later the socket is gone, with the revocation close code.
	h.Clock.Advance(h.HeartbeatInterval + time.Second)
	code, err := conn.WaitClosed(t, 5*time.Second)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("the gateway connection was still open a heartbeat after the disable: %v", err)
	}
	if code != 4401 {
		t.Fatalf("close code = %d, want 4401", code)
	}
}

func TestEveryMutatingVerbWritesAnAuditRow(t *testing.T) {
	h := newCLIHarness(t)
	uid := h.SeedUser(t, "alice")
	did := h.SeedDevice(t, uid)
	for _, args := range [][]string{
		{"admin", "user", "disable", "--id=" + uid.String()},
		{"admin", "user", "enable", "--id=" + uid.String()},
		{"admin", "device", "revoke", "--id=" + did.String()},
		{"admin", "invite", "create", "--max-uses=1", "--ttl=1h"},
	} {
		if code, _ := h.Run(t, append(args, "--config="+h.ConfigPath)...); code != 0 {
			t.Fatalf("%v failed", args)
		}
	}
	rows, err := h.Repo(t).ListAudit(t.Context(), 0, 100)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ListAudit: %v", err)
	}
	want := []string{"user.disable", "user.enable", "device.revoke", "invite.create"}
	var got []string
	for _, r := range rows {
		got = append(got, r.Action)
		if r.Actor != nil {
			t.Fatalf("a CLI audit row names a user actor (%x); the CLI acts as the operator and writes actor NULL", *r.Actor)
		}
		if r.Detail == "" && r.Action == "invite.create" {
			t.Fatal("invite.create wrote no reference")
		}
	}
	for _, w := range want {
		if !slices.Contains(got, w) {
			t.Fatalf("no audit row for %s: %v", w, got)
		}
	}
	// A read-only verb writes none.
	before := len(rows)
	if code, _ := h.Run(t, "admin", "user", "list", "--config="+h.ConfigPath); code != 0 {
		t.Fatal("list failed")
	}
	after, _ := h.Repo(t).ListAudit(t.Context(), 0, 100)
	if len(after) != before {
		t.Fatalf("a read-only verb wrote %d audit rows", len(after)-before)
	}
}

func TestTheCLIRefusesANewerSchema(t *testing.T) {
	h := newCLIHarness(t)
	h.SetGooseVersion(t, 1_000_000)
	code, _, stderr := h.Run3(t, "admin", "user", "list", "--config="+h.ConfigPath)
	if code != int(exit.Config) {
		t.Fatalf("exit = %d, want %d", code, exit.Config)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(stderr, "1000000") {
		t.Fatalf("stderr = %q", stderr)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the verb group**

Create `cmd/dillad/admin.go`. The dispatcher, written out because the exit codes are the contract:

```go
// adminUsage is printed to stderr on a usage error and to stdout on --help.
const adminUsage = `usage: dillad admin <noun> <verb> [flags]

nouns and verbs:
  user      list | show | disable | enable | delete
  invite    create | list | revoke
  community list | show
  device    revoke
  blob      purge
  audit

every verb accepts --config=PATH; run "dillad admin <noun> --help" for its flags
`

func runAdmin(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) exit.Code {
	if len(args) > 0 && (args[0] == "--help" || args[0] == "-h") {
		fmt.Fprint(stdout, adminUsage)
		return exit.OK
	}
	if len(args) < 2 {
		fmt.Fprint(stderr, adminUsage)
		return exit.Usage
	}
	noun, verb, rest := args[0], args[1], args[2:]
	switch noun {
	case "user":
		return adminUser(verb, rest, stdout, stderr)
	case "invite":
		return adminInvite(verb, rest, stdout, stderr)
	case "community":
		return adminCommunity(verb, rest, stdout, stderr)
	case "device":
		return adminDevice(verb, rest, stdout, stderr)
	case "blob":
		return adminBlob(verb, rest, stdout, stderr)
	case "audit":
		return adminAudit(append([]string{verb}, rest...), stdout, stderr)
	default:
		fmt.Fprintf(stderr, "dillad admin: unknown noun %q\n\n", noun)
		fmt.Fprint(stderr, adminUsage)
		return exit.Usage
	}
}
```

Each `admin<Noun>` builds a `flag.NewFlagSet(noun, flag.ContinueOnError)` with `SetOutput(stderr)`,
registers `--config` plus its own flags, and on an unknown verb prints the usage block to stderr and
returns `exit.Usage`. Every verb opens the store through the same helper `serve` uses, and that
helper refuses a `goose_db_version` higher than the binary's highest embedded migration with
`exit.Config` — so the CLI can never write through a schema it does not understand.

Every **mutating** verb writes an `audit_log` row with `Actor: nil`: the CLI acts as the operator,
who is not a `users` row, and inventing one would make the audit trail lie. Every **read-only**
verb writes none.

`auth.TokenHash(token string) []byte` is Plan 1a's session-token hashing primitive (§2.2 point 3
stores tokens as `SHA-256(token)`); it is listed under "Symbols this plan consumes from Plan 1" and
carried as **P2-10** under "Needs verification", because §6.3 names neither its spelling nor its
argument type. Confirm it before writing this test.

`admin invite create` mints the code with `auth.NewInviteCode()`, prints
`https://<domain>/i/<code>` exactly once to stdout, stores only `auth.InviteHash(code)`, and logs
the 8-hex reference — never the code.

`admin user disable` sets `users.disabled_at` and calls `DeleteSessionsByUser` **in one
transaction**, and that is all it can do: it is a different process from `serve` and has no handle
on the running gateway. Closing the sockets is `serve`'s job, and this task adds the one check that
makes it happen — the gateway's heartbeat re-reads the session row and closes the connection with
code 4401 when it is gone (`internal/gateway/heartbeat.go`, one query per heartbeat per connection,
already on the hot path for the idle-timeout check). That is protocol §2.2 point 6's
"Setting `users.disabled_at` does the same for every device of that user", with the bound stated:
**at most one heartbeat interval**, not instantly.

`admin blob purge --id=<64 hex> --reason=TEXT` is the CLI twin of task 11's admin route and shares
its implementation.

  ```go
  // internal/ops
  type Leg struct {
      Name   string
      Status Status   // Green, Yellow, Red
      Detail string
      Fix    string   // the exact command an operator runs, or ""
  }
  type Status uint8
  const (Green Status = iota; Yellow; Red)
  type Report struct { Legs []Leg }
  func (r Report) Text() string     // stable, diffable, one line per leg
  func (r Report) Worst() Status
  func ClockSkew(ctx context.Context, c *http.Client, url string) (time.Duration, error)
  func ClockLeg(ctx context.Context, c *http.Client, peers []string, max time.Duration) Leg
  func CertificateLeg(ctx context.Context, domain string, tlsCfg *tls.Config) Leg
  func TURNLeg(ctx context.Context, listen, realm, secret string) Leg
  func UDPLeg(cfg config.Config) Leg
  ```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/ops/doctor_test.go`:

```go
package ops_test

import (
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/jonasthim/dilla/internal/ops"
)

func TestClockSkewParsesTheDateHeader(t *testing.T) {
	// RFC 9110 §6.6.1: an origin server MUST generate Date in all responses
	// except 1xx, 204 and 304. So any HTTPS origin works, not only the ACME one.
	skewed := time.Now().Add(-10 * time.Minute)
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		w.Header().Set("Date", skewed.UTC().Format(http.TimeFormat))
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
	}))
	defer srv.Close()

	got, err := ops.ClockSkew(t.Context(), srv.Client(), srv.URL)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ClockSkew: %v", err)
	}
	// HTTP-date has one-second granularity, so the floor is 1s + rtt/2.
	if got < 9*time.Minute || got > 11*time.Minute {
		t.Fatalf("skew = %v, want about 10m", got)
	}
}

func TestClockLegFailsPast60Seconds(t *testing.T) {
	ok1, ok2, ok3 := mk60(t, 0), mk60(t, 0), mk60(t, 0)
	leg := ops.ClockLeg(t.Context(), ok1.Client(), []string{ok1.URL, ok2.URL, ok3.URL}, 60*time.Second)
	if leg.Status != ops.Green {
		t.Fatalf("a synchronised clock = %v (%s)", leg.Status, leg.Detail)
	}

	bad1, bad2, bad3 := mk60(t, -5*time.Minute), mk60(t, -5*time.Minute), mk60(t, -5*time.Minute)
	leg = ops.ClockLeg(t.Context(), bad1.Client(), []string{bad1.URL, bad2.URL, bad3.URL}, 60*time.Second)
	if leg.Status != ops.Red {
		t.Fatalf("a 5-minute skew = %v", leg.Status)
	}
	for _, want := range []string{"ahead", "ACME", "KeyPackage"} {
		if !strings.Contains(leg.Detail, want) {
			t.Fatalf("detail %q does not say what breaks (%q)", leg.Detail, want)
		}
	}
	if leg.Fix == "" {
		t.Fatal("a red clock leg must name the fix")
	}
}

// mk60 is the same helper as mk in TestClockLegFailsPast60Seconds, hoisted to
// package scope so both tests use one definition. It returns an httptest server
// whose Date header is offset by off, registered for cleanup with t.Cleanup.
func mk60(t *testing.T, off time.Duration) *httptest.Server {
	t.Helper()
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		w.Header().Set("Date", time.Now().Add(off).UTC().Format(http.TimeFormat))
	}))
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	return srv
}

func TestOneMisconfiguredPeerDoesNotFailTheLeg(t *testing.T) {
	good1, good2 := mk60(t, 0), mk60(t, 0)
	bad := mk60(t, -30*time.Minute)
	leg := ops.ClockLeg(t.Context(), good1.Client(), []string{good1.URL, good2.URL, bad.URL}, 60*time.Second)
	if leg.Status != ops.Green {
		t.Fatalf("the median of three should be green, got %v (%s)", leg.Status, leg.Detail)
	}
}

func TestTheReportIsStableDiffableText(t *testing.T) {
	r := ops.Report{Legs: []ops.Leg{
		{Name: "config", Status: ops.Green, Detail: "parsed, 128 keys"},
		{Name: "database", Status: ops.Green, Detail: "goose 9, target 9"},
		{Name: "certificate", Status: ops.Yellow, Detail: "expires in 12d, issuer Let's Encrypt"},
		{Name: "turn", Status: ops.Red, Detail: "no allocation", Fix: "open tcp/5349 to dillad"},
	}}
	want := strings.Join([]string{
		"OK    config       parsed, 128 keys",
		"OK    database     goose 9, target 9",
		"WARN  certificate  expires in 12d, issuer Let's Encrypt",
		"FAIL  turn         no allocation",
		"      fix: open tcp/5349 to dillad",
		"",
	}, "\n")
	if got := r.Text(); got != want {
		t.Fatalf("Text() =\n%s\nwant\n%s", got, want)
	}
	if r.Worst() != ops.Red {
		t.Fatalf("Worst() = %v", r.Worst())
	}
	// Two runs of the same report are byte-identical: a runbook diffs them.
	if r.Text() != r.Text() {
		t.Fatal("Text() is not stable")
	}
}

func TestTheUDPLegPrintsAnInstructionAndContactsNothing(t *testing.T) {
	var contacted int
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) { contacted++ }))
	defer srv.Close()
	leg := ops.UDPLeg(configWithUDPProbe(srv.URL))
	if contacted != 0 {
		t.Fatal("the UDP leg contacted a network service")
	}
	if leg.Status != ops.Yellow {
		t.Fatalf("status = %v, want Yellow (unknown until the operator checks)", leg.Status)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(leg.Fix, "7882/udp") {
		t.Fatalf("the leg does not tell the operator what to open: %q", leg.Fix)
	}
}

func TestBlobConsistencyReportsBothDirections(t *testing.T) {
	h := newGCHarness(t)
	payload := []byte("present")
	sum := sha256.Sum256(payload)
	ch := h.NewChannel()
	h.Upload(sum[:], payload, ch)
	// A row with no file, and a file with no row.
	h.DeleteFileBehindTheStore(sum[:])
	h.WriteStrayFile([]byte("stray"))
	leg := ops.BlobConsistencyLeg(t.Context(), h.Repo, h.Store)
	if leg.Status != ops.Red {
		t.Fatalf("status = %v", leg.Status)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(leg.Detail, "1 referenced blob missing") ||
		!strings.Contains(leg.Detail, "1 file with no row") {
		t.Fatalf("detail = %q", leg.Detail)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the legs**

Create `internal/ops/doctor.go`. The clock leg, which is the one with measured rules:

```go
// ClockSkew issues a HEAD to url and returns the estimated local-clock offset;
// positive means the local clock is ahead.
//
// HEAD, not GET: there is no body and RFC 9110's MUST still applies. The server
// generated Date somewhere in [t0, t1], so the midpoint is the best single
// estimate, with residual error at most rtt/2 plus Date's one-second
// granularity. Never report a skew below 1s + rtt/2 as non-zero; the spec's 60 s
// threshold has about 60x headroom, which is why it is a sound threshold and a
// tighter one would not be.
func ClockSkew(ctx context.Context, c *http.Client, url string) (time.Duration, error) {
	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodHead, url, nil)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	req.Header.Set("Cache-Control", "no-cache")
	t0 := time.Now()
	resp, err := c.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	t1 := time.Now()
	hdr := resp.Header.Get("Date")
	if hdr == "" {
		return 0, fmt.Errorf("no Date header from %s", url)
	}
	remote, err := http.ParseTime(hdr) // TimeFormat, RFC850 and ANSIC
	if err != nil {
		return 0, fmt.Errorf("unparseable Date %q from %s: %w", hdr, url, err)
	}
	mid := t0.Add(t1.Sub(t0) / 2)
	return mid.Sub(remote), nil
}

// ClockLeg samples every peer, discards samples whose round trip exceeded 2 s,
// and takes the MEDIAN, so one misconfigured origin cannot fail doctor.
func ClockLeg(ctx context.Context, c *http.Client, peers []string, max time.Duration) Leg {
	var samples []time.Duration
	var failures []string
	for _, p := range peers {
		d, err := ClockSkew(ctx, c, p)
		if err != nil {
			failures = append(failures, fmt.Sprintf("%s: %v", p, err))
			continue
		}
		samples = append(samples, d)
	}
	if len(samples) == 0 {
		return Leg{Name: "clock", Status: Yellow,
			Detail: "no HTTPS origin answered: " + strings.Join(failures, "; "),
			Fix:    "check outbound HTTPS, or pass --clock-peer=https://<a host you can reach>"}
	}
	slices.Sort(samples)
	med := samples[len(samples)/2]
	if med.Abs() <= max {
		return Leg{Name: "clock", Status: Green,
			Detail: fmt.Sprintf("within %s of %d HTTPS origins (median %s)", max, len(samples), med.Round(time.Second))}
	}
	dir := "ahead of"
	if med < 0 {
		dir = "behind"
	}
	return Leg{Name: "clock", Status: Red,
		Detail: fmt.Sprintf("local clock is %s %s %d HTTPS origins (median); ACME order validation and MLS KeyPackage lifetime checks will fail",
			med.Abs().Round(time.Second), dir, len(samples)),
		Fix: "enable systemd-timesyncd or chrony, then re-run dillad doctor"}
}
```

`serve` never gates on this leg: a booting LXC with no network yet must still start. The skew is
additionally exported as the gauge `dilla_clock_skew_seconds`, refreshed hourly.

`CertificateLeg` reads the certificate certmagic holds for the configured domain and reports the
issuer and `NotAfter`: Green outside the renewal window, **Yellow inside it or after a failed
renewal**, and Red once expired. The spec's operational table requires "ACME failure keeps the last
certificate and alerts", and nothing else in this plan covered it, so the leg takes a third input:

```go
func CertificateLeg(ctx context.Context, domain string, tlsCfg *tls.Config, lastRenewalError string) Leg
```

`lastRenewalError` is the empty string when the most recent issuance or renewal succeeded. It is
fed by the certmagic `OnEvent` hook task 16 installs:

```go
// In internal/server/tls.go. certmagic keeps serving the existing certificate
// when a renewal fails — that part is certmagic's own behaviour and needs no
// code — but it says nothing, so the operator finds out when the certificate
// expires. These three lines are the alert.
cfg.OnEvent = func(ctx context.Context, event string, data map[string]any) error {
	switch event {
	case "cert_failed":
		t.setRenewalError(fmt.Sprint(data["error"]))
		obs.CertRenewalFailures.Inc()
		t.health.Degrade("certificate", "ACME renewal failed; serving the existing certificate")
	case "cert_obtained":
		t.setRenewalError("")
		t.health.Clear("certificate")
	}
	return nil
}
```

with `dilla_cert_renewal_failures_total` in `internal/obs/metrics.go` and a test in
`internal/server/tls_test.go` that points certmagic at an ACME directory URL that answers 500,
drives one renewal, and asserts that the previously issued certificate is still returned by
`GetCertificate` and that `CertificateLeg` reports Yellow with the error in `Detail` and a non-empty
`Fix`. `/readyz` stays green: a degraded certificate is not a reason to take the instance out of
rotation while the existing certificate is still valid.
`TURNLeg` allocates against the configured `turn.listen` with a credential minted from the shared
secret and reports the relayed address; it is Yellow, not Red, when `turn.enabled` is false, because
that is the founder's own topology. `UDPLeg` contacts **nothing** — there is no project-run
reflector and there will not be one — and prints the operator instruction: "publish
`<public_ip>:7882/udp` to this host and verify it from outside; in `behind_proxy` that is a raw UDP
forward, not an HTTP route". `BlobConsistencyLeg` walks both directions, which a restore can break
either way: a database snapshot older than the blob tree leaves files with no row (harmless), and a
blob tree older than the database leaves referenced-but-missing files (visible as 404s, and
recoverable, because any client holding the plaintext and the key re-uploads the identical bytes).

`Report.Text()` is the stable format the test pins, and the pinned golden output is the contract:
the status is left-padded to **6** columns (`"OK    "`, `"WARN  "`, `"FAIL  "`), the name to **13**
(`"config       "`, `"certificate  "`), then the detail, then an indented `fix:` line — six spaces
— only when `Fix` is non-empty, and a trailing newline after the last line. Written as a format
string: `fmt.Fprintf(w, "%-6s%-13s%s\n", status, leg.Name, leg.Detail)`.

```go
func TestLooksLikeSTUN(t *testing.T) {
	// RFC 8489 §5: the top two bits MUST be zero "enabling differentiation from
	// other protocols during multiplexing", and the magic cookie 0x2112A442 sits
	// at bytes [4:8].
	stun := []byte{0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x21, 0x12, 0xA4, 0x42}
	if !server.LooksLikeSTUN(stun) {
		t.Fatal("a STUN Binding header was not recognised")
	}
	for _, b := range [][]byte{
		[]byte("GET /v1/i"),
		[]byte("PRI * HTTP/2.0\r\n\r\nSM\r\n\r\n")[:8],
		{0xC0, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x21, 0x12, 0xA4, 0x42}, // top bits set
		{0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x21, 0x12, 0xA4, 0x43}, // wrong cookie
		{0x00, 0x01, 0x00},                                // too short
	} {
		if server.LooksLikeSTUN(b) {
			t.Fatalf("%x was taken for STUN", b)
		}
	}
}

func TestTheDemuxReplaysThePeekedBytesToHTTP(t *testing.T) {
	raw, tlsCfg := selfSignedListener(t)
	d := server.NewDemux(raw, tlsCfg, 10*time.Second)
	go d.Serve()
	defer d.Close()

	srv := &http.Server{Handler: http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		fmt.Fprintf(w, "path=%s proto=%s tls=%v", r.URL.Path, r.Proto, r.TLS != nil)
	})}
	go srv.Serve(d.HTTP())
	defer srv.Close()

	// selfSignedListener returns the CA pool for the certificate it generated, so
	// the test verifies the chain rather than switching verification off.
	c := &http.Client{Transport: &http.Transport{TLSClientConfig: &tls.Config{
		RootCAs: testCertPool(t), ServerName: "localhost",
	}}}
	resp, err := c.Get("https://" + raw.Addr().String() + "/v1/instance")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("GET: %v", err)
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	b, _ := io.ReadAll(resp.Body)
	// The first eight bytes of "GET /v1/instance" were consumed by the peek and
	// must reach net/http anyway, and r.TLS must be populated — Go 1.27 reads
	// ConnectionState() off a user-provided net.Conn.
	if string(b) != "path=/v1/instance proto=HTTP/1.1 tls=true" {
		t.Fatalf("body = %q", b)
	}
}

func TestTheDemuxRoutesSTUNToTheTURNBranch(t *testing.T) {
	raw, tlsCfg := selfSignedListener(t)
	d := server.NewDemux(raw, tlsCfg, 10*time.Second)
	go d.Serve()
	defer d.Close()

	got := make(chan []byte, 1)
	go func() {
		c, err := d.TURN().Accept()
		if err != nil {
			return
		}
		defer c.Close()
		buf := make([]byte, 20)
		n, _ := io.ReadFull(c, buf)
		got <- buf[:n]
	}()

	conn, err := tls.Dial("tcp", raw.Addr().String(), &tls.Config{
		RootCAs: testCertPool(t), ServerName: "localhost",
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Dial: %v", err)
	}
	defer conn.Close()
	header := append([]byte{0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x21, 0x12, 0xA4, 0x42}, bytes.Repeat([]byte{7}, 12)...)
	if _, err := conn.Write(header); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Write: %v", err)
	}
	select {
	case b := <-got:
		// The peeked eight bytes MUST be replayed: pion reads the STUN header
		// itself through turn.NewSTUNConn.
		if !bytes.Equal(b, header) {
			t.Fatalf("the TURN branch saw %x, want %x", b, header)
		}
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("the STUN stream never reached the TURN listener")
	}
}

func TestBuildTLSKeepsACMETLS1FirstAndAppendsH2(t *testing.T) {
	// BuildTLS constructs; it does not issue. Manage is what would contact
	// Let's Encrypt, and this test never calls it.
	_, cfg, err := server.BuildTLS(t.Context(), config.TLS{
		Mode: "acme_alpn", Email: "ops@example.test", Agreed: true, StorageDir: t.TempDir(),
	}, []string{"chat.example.test"})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("BuildTLS: %v", err)
	}
	// certmagic's TLSConfig() sets NextProtos to exactly ["acme-tls/1"]; dillad
	// must append and must keep acme-tls/1 first, or TLS-ALPN-01 renewals break.
	if len(cfg.NextProtos) != 3 || cfg.NextProtos[0] != "acme-tls/1" ||
		cfg.NextProtos[1] != "h2" || cfg.NextProtos[2] != "http/1.1" {
		t.Fatalf("NextProtos = %v", cfg.NextProtos)
	}
	if cfg.MinVersion != tls.VersionTLS12 {
		t.Fatalf("MinVersion = %x; TURN/TLS clients and pion use a TLS 1.2 floor", cfg.MinVersion)
	}
}

func TestTheACMEIPModeForcesShortlivedAndHalfTheWindow(t *testing.T) {
	got := server.ACMEIPSettingsForTest()
	if got.Profile != "shortlived" {
		t.Fatalf("profile = %q, want shortlived", got.Profile)
	}
	// certmagic's default renewal window ratio is 1/3, which for a 160-hour
	// certificate renews with 53.3 h left. 0.5 renews at 80 h, which a homelab
	// box that sleeps can actually meet.
	if got.RenewalWindowRatio != 0.5 {
		t.Fatalf("renewal window ratio = %v, want 0.5", got.RenewalWindowRatio)
	}
	if !got.DisableHTTPChallenge {
		t.Fatal("DisableHTTPChallenge must be set: acmez shuffles challenge order otherwise")
	}
}

func TestTURNCredentialIsHMACOverExpiryColonDevice(t *testing.T) {
	dev := id.New()
	now := time.Unix(1_790_000_000, 0)
	user, pass := server.TURNCredential("s3cret", dev, time.Hour, now)
	wantUser := fmt.Sprintf("%d:%s", now.Add(time.Hour).Unix(), dev.String())
	if user != wantUser {
		t.Fatalf("username = %q, want %q", user, wantUser)
	}
	mac := hmac.New(sha1.New, []byte("s3cret"))
	mac.Write([]byte(wantUser))
	if want := base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil)); pass != want {
		t.Fatalf("password = %q, want %q", pass, want)
	}
	// pion's LongTermTURNRESTAuthHandler splits on ':' and rejects a past expiry.
	if _, _, ok := server.TURNAuthForTest("s3cret")(&turn.RequestAttributes{Username: user}); !ok {
		t.Fatal("pion refused a credential dillad minted")
	}
	old, _ := server.TURNCredential("s3cret", dev, -time.Hour, now)
	if _, _, ok := server.TURNAuthForTest("s3cret")(&turn.RequestAttributes{Username: old}); ok {
		t.Fatal("pion accepted an expired credential")
	}
}

func TestAtMostTwoAllocationsPerDevice(t *testing.T) {
	q := server.NewAllocationQuota(2)
	dev := id.New().String()
	for i := range 2 {
		if !q.Allow(dev) {
			t.Fatalf("allocation %d was refused", i)
		}
	}
	if q.Allow(dev) {
		t.Fatal("a third allocation was allowed")
	}
	q.Release(dev)
	if !q.Allow(dev) {
		t.Fatal("a slot did not free after Release")
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the demux**

Create `internal/server/demux.go`:

```go
package server

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"crypto/tls"
	"encoding/binary"
	"io"
	"net"
	"time"
)

// stunMagicCookie is RFC 8489 §5's fixed value, in network byte order.
const stunMagicCookie uint32 = 0x2112A442

// LooksLikeSTUN implements RFC 8489 §5: the leading two bits "MUST be zeroes",
// explicitly "enabling differentiation from other protocols during
// multiplexing", and the magic cookie occupies bytes [4:8]. Eight bytes is the
// right peek: fewer cannot check the cookie, more risks blocking on a client
// that writes its first request in small pieces. Every HTTP/1.1 method token and
// the HTTP/2 preface are at least eight bytes and begin with an uppercase ASCII
// letter (>= 0x41), so the top-bits test already separates them and the cookie
// is the belt to that braces.
func LooksLikeSTUN(b []byte) bool {
	return len(b) >= 8 &&
		b[0]&0xC0 == 0 &&
		binary.BigEndian.Uint32(b[4:8]) == stunMagicCookie
}

// peekConn replays the bytes the demuxer consumed and keeps the TLS state
// visible to net/http. Go 1.27's Server reads ConnectionState() off a
// user-provided net.Conn, so r.TLS is populated and h2 still negotiates.
//
// Through the embedded *tls.Conn the type satisfies BOTH of net/http's optional
// interfaces: connectionStater and handshakeContexter. The promoted
// HandshakeContext is a no-op here because Demux.Serve already completed the
// handshake, and tls.Conn.HandshakeContext returns immediately once it has. Do
// not "fix" this by dropping the embedding: ConnectionState() would go with it,
// and r.TLS would be nil.
type peekConn struct {
	*tls.Conn
	r io.Reader
}

func (c *peekConn) Read(p []byte) (int, error) { return c.r.Read(p) }

func newPeekConn(tc *tls.Conn, peeked []byte) *peekConn {
	return &peekConn{Conn: tc, r: io.MultiReader(bytes.NewReader(peeked), tc)}
}
```

`Demux.Serve` accepts on the raw listener, does the TLS handshake with a bounded context, reads
exactly eight bytes with a read deadline, and hands a `*peekConn` to whichever `chanListener` the
test asserts.

**A read timeout with zero bytes consumed routes the connection to HTTP, and clears the deadline
before handing it over.** HTTP is the safe default: a legal HTTPS client may complete the handshake
and then wait — connection pre-warming, a keep-alive probe, a speculative browser connection — while
a STUN client always sends its Binding or Allocate immediately. Closing on the timeout would drop
exactly the connections `net/http` would have kept idle. `internal/server/demux_test.go` gains
`TestADemuxConnectionThatSendsNothingGoesToHTTP`: handshake, send nothing, and assert that
`d.HTTP().Accept()` returns the connection after the handshake timeout and that the connection is
still usable for a request afterwards. `chanListener` is a `chan net.Conn` with `Accept`, `Close` and `Addr`, where `Close`
makes `Accept` return `net.ErrClosed` so both `http.Server.Serve` and `turn.Server` unwind.

One accepted loss, written into the file as a comment so a future reviewer does not rediscover it:
`pion/turn`'s `Server.readListener` does `tlsConn, ok := conn.(*tls.Conn)`, and a `*peekConn` is
not a `*tls.Conn`, so `RequestAttributes.TLS` stays nil. dilla's auth handler is HMAC over the
username and never reads it, but a future "require a client certificate" idea would silently not
work.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the TLS modes and TURN**

`BuildTLS` switches on `config.TLS.Mode`:

**`BuildTLS` never contacts an ACME server.** It constructs the certmagic config, installs the
`OnEvent` hook of task 15, and returns the `*tls.Config` with `GetCertificate` wired to certmagic;
issuance is `(*TLS).Manage(ctx, domains)`, which `serve` calls and no test does. The split is what
keeps `TestBuildTLSKeepsACMETLS1FirstAndAppendsH2` — which asserts only `NextProtos` and
`MinVersion`, and needs no certificate at all — from making certmagic attempt a TLS-ALPN-01
challenge against `chat.example.test`, a domain that does not resolve, and hang until the ACME
client times out. **No test in this plan contacts an ACME server**, and the one test that drives a
renewal failure (task 15) points certmagic at a local `httptest` directory that answers 500.

- `acme_alpn` — `certmagic.NewDefault()`, `DisableHTTPChallenge: true` (mandatory: acmez shuffles
  challenge order otherwise), then `cfg.TLSConfig()` with `NextProtos` appended so `acme-tls/1`
  stays first and `MinVersion = tls.VersionTLS12`. `Manage` calls `ManageSync` for the domains.
- `acme_dns` — the same plus `DNS01Solver{DNSManager{DNSProvider: …, TTL, PropagationDelay,
  PropagationTimeout, Resolvers, OverrideDomain}}` from `[tls.dns]`. A non-nil `DNS01Solver` makes
  DNS-01 **exclusive**. A provider is a value satisfying two `libdns` interfaces, wired from a
  `map[string]func(map[string]string) (certmagic.DNSProvider, error)` registry; no certmagic-side
  plugin machinery exists or is needed.
- `acme_ip` — DNS-01 plus `Profile: "shortlived"` (Let's Encrypt IP certificates require it) and
  `RenewalWindowRatio = 0.5`.
- `behind_proxy` — returns an error: certmagic is **not constructed at all**. It is dilla's own
  mode, not certmagic's: certmagic v0.25.4 contains no `behind_proxy`, no `trusted_proxies` and no
  real-IP handling of any kind. `serve` binds only `server.plain_listen` and parses
  `X-Forwarded-For` against `server.trusted_proxy_cidrs`, which `Validate` requires to be non-empty
  in this mode.

`StartTURN` builds `turn.ServerConfig{Realm, AuthHandler: turn.LongTermTURNRESTAuthHandler(secret,
logger), QuotaHandler: …, EventHandler: …, ListenerConfigs: []turn.ListenerConfig{{Listener: ln,
RelayAddressGenerator: &turn.RelayAddressGeneratorStatic{RelayAddress: relayIP, Address:
relayIP.String()}}}}`. `TURNCredential` is `expiry:device_id` with
`base64(HMAC-SHA1(secret, username))`, which is exactly what `LongTermTURNRESTAuthHandler` splits
on and validates.

**Both handlers are wired, and the username is parsed.** `turn.ServerConfig` in pion/turn v5.0.13
(`server_config.go:130-157`) declares `QuotaHandler func(username, realm string, srcAddr net.Addr)
(ok bool)` — an **admission** callback only — and a separate `EventHandler` for allocation
lifecycle. Wiring only the quota handler would mean `AllocationQuota.Release` is never called in
production, and a device would be permanently capped after `allocations_per_device` allocations for
the life of the process; the quota's own test exercises `Release`, so the gap would be invisible.
The `username` pion passes is the **full REST username**, `<expiry>:<device_id>`, not the device id:

```go
cfg.QuotaHandler = func(username, realm string, srcAddr net.Addr) bool {
	// pion's own LongTermTURNRESTAuthHandler splits on ':' the same way
	// (lt_cred.go:100-105); the device id is everything after the first colon.
	_, dev, ok := strings.Cut(username, ":")
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	return q.Allow(dev)
}
cfg.EventHandler = func(e allocation.Event) {
	// Release on close, so a device that hangs up gets its slot back.
	if e.Type == allocation.EventTypeAllocationClosed || e.Type == allocation.EventTypeAllocationDeleted {
		if _, dev, ok := strings.Cut(e.Username, ":"); ok {
			q.Release(dev)
		}
	}
}
```

`TestAtMostTwoAllocationsPerDevice` keeps testing the quota directly, and
`TestTheQuotaHandlerParsesTheRESTUsername` is added beside it: pass
`fmt.Sprintf("%d:%s", expiry, dev)` to the wired handler twice, assert both are allowed, assert the
third is refused, drive the close event with the same username, and assert the next one is allowed
again.

> **Needs verification before writing this step.** `allocation.Event`, its `Type` constants and its
> `Username` field are read from
> `~/go/pkg/mod/github.com/pion/turn/v5@v5.0.13/internal/allocation/` — the exact spelling is
> confirmed against the vendored source, not assumed, because `EventHandler`'s payload type is the
> one part of this that no facts file pins.

In `behind_proxy`, TURN is **off by default** and, when explicitly enabled, listens on a separate
operator-configured TCP port — never on 443, because Pangolin's Traefik terminates TLS there and no
HTTP router rule can match a STUN Allocate. The UI's "relay unavailable" dialog is the documented
consequence.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the two voice routes**

**First, create the `voice_sessions` table — P2-D22.** §4.3 declares it and no task in either plan
ever created it, so `store.Structure.PutVoiceSession`/`EndVoiceSession` (§4.1) have no storage,
P2-D19's `EndAllVoiceSessions` has no table, task 13's "end every live `voice_sessions` row" and its
`h.LiveCalls(t) != 0` assertion cannot work, and this route has nowhere to record a call. Append to
both `006_structure.sql` files (SQLite shown; Postgres with §4.2's substitutions) and write the
migration around it as `00011_voice.sql` in both engines' directories, per the table in this file's
header:

```sql
CREATE TABLE voice_sessions (
  call_id      BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(call_id) = 16) PRIMARY KEY,
  channel_id   BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(channel_id) = 16)
                       REFERENCES channels(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  group_id     BLOB    CHECK (group_id IS NULL OR length(group_id) = 16),
  livekit_room TEXT    NOT NULL,
  started      INTEGER NOT NULL,
  ended        INTEGER
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX voice_sessions_live ON voice_sessions(channel_id) WHERE ended IS NULL;
```

with the queries `PutVoiceSession`, `GetVoiceSession`, `EndVoiceSession`, `ListLiveVoiceSessions`
and `EndAllVoiceSessions` (`UPDATE voice_sessions SET ended = ? WHERE ended IS NULL`), the three
`store.Structure` methods of §4.1 plus `GetVoiceSession` (P2-D22) implemented in both `repo.go`
files, and `store.VoiceSessionRow` declared in `internal/store/rows.go` (P2-D24).

Then create `internal/api/calls.go` with `POST /v1/channels/{id}/calls` and
`DELETE /v1/calls/{call_id}`:

```go
func (h *Calls) Register(mux *server.Mux) {
	mux.HandleFunc("POST /v1/channels/{id}/calls", h.start)
	mux.HandleFunc("DELETE /v1/calls/{call_id}", h.end)
}
```

The JWT gate is the spec's: a token is minted only for a device whose leaf is present in the call
group's **current epoch**. The mint itself is `internal/sfu`'s existing
`func (s *Server) Token(room, identity string) (string, error)` — read before writing this path, as
NV11 requires; the gate is dillad's, applied before the call:

```go
	group, err := h.callGroup(r.Context(), ch) // GroupsForTarget(ch.ID, GroupCall)
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	members, err := h.repo.ListMembers(r.Context(), group.GroupID)
	if err != nil {
		server.WriteError(w, err)
		return
	}
	var present bool
	for _, m := range members {
		if m.DeviceID == s.DeviceID && m.RemovedEpoch == nil && m.AddedEpoch <= group.Epoch {
			present = true
			break
		}
	}
	if !present {
		server.WriteError(w, server.Errorf(server.CodeLeafNotCurrent,
			"your device is not a leaf of this call group's current epoch"))
		return
	}
	token, err := h.sfu.Token(row.LivekitRoom, s.DeviceID.String())
```

**`internal/api/calls_test.go` is written out here, not merely staged.** The epoch gate is the
security-relevant half of this task — it is the spec's own rule and the resolution of NV11/P2-8 —
so it gets four cases:

```go
func TestACurrentLeafGetsALiveKitToken(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok, group := callEnv(t) // a voice channel with a call group at epoch 7
	dev := deviceOf(t, e, tok)
	seedLeaf(t, e, group, dev, 3 /* addedEpoch */, nil /* removedEpoch */)
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/calls", tok, []any{})
	if status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatalf("POST calls = %d (%x)", status, body)
	}
	var out []cbor.RawMessage
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	var callID id.ID
	var room, jwt string
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &callID)
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[1], &room)
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[2], &jwt)
	if jwt == "" || room == "" {
		t.Fatalf("empty token or room: %q %q", jwt, room)
	}
	// The call is recorded, which is what DELETE /v1/calls/{call_id} and the
	// restore path both read.
	row, err := e.Repo.GetVoiceSession(t.Context(), callID)
	if err != nil || row.Ended != nil || row.LivekitRoom != room {
		t.Fatalf("voice_sessions row = %+v (%v)", row, err)
	}
}

func TestARemovedDeviceIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok, group := callEnv(t)
	dev := deviceOf(t, e, tok)
	removed := uint64(5)
	seedLeaf(t, e, group, dev, 3, &removed)
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/calls", tok, []any{})
	if status != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("removed leaf = %d, want 403", status)
	}
	if code := e.ErrCode(body); code != "E_LEAF_NOT_CURRENT" {
		t.Fatalf("code = %s", code)
	}
}

func TestALeafAddedInAFutureEpochIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok, group := callEnv(t) // group epoch is 7
	dev := deviceOf(t, e, tok)
	seedLeaf(t, e, group, dev, 9 /* addedEpoch > epoch */, nil)
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/calls", tok, []any{})
	if status != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("future leaf = %d, want 403", status)
	}
	if code := e.ErrCode(body); code != "E_LEAF_NOT_CURRENT" {
		t.Fatalf("code = %s", code)
	}
}

func TestEndingACallMarksTheVoiceSession(t *testing.T) {
	e, ch, tok, group := callEnv(t)
	seedLeaf(t, e, group, deviceOf(t, e, tok), 3, nil)
	_, body := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/channels/"+ch.String()+"/calls", tok, []any{})
	var out []cbor.RawMessage
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	var callID id.ID
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &callID)
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodDelete, "/v1/calls/"+callID.String(), tok, nil); status != http.StatusNoContent {
		t.Fatal("DELETE call failed")
	}
	row, _ := e.Repo.GetVoiceSession(t.Context(), callID)
	if row.Ended == nil {
		t.Fatal("the voice session was not ended")
	}
}
```

`callEnv`, `seedLeaf` and `deviceOf` join `apitest_test.go`; the SFU is a stub `sfu` whose `Token`
returns a fixed string, because the JWT's contents are `internal/sfu`'s own test's business and the
gate is this test's.

### Task 17: Reports and franking verification

**Files:**
- Modify: `internal/store/sqlite/schema/009_ops.sql`, `internal/store/postgres/schema/009_ops.sql`
- Modify: `internal/store/sqlite/schema/005_messages.sql`, `internal/store/postgres/schema/005_messages.sql` (the `franking_key_id` column on `mls_app_messages`)
- Modify: `internal/store/sqlite/schema/007_readable.sql`, `internal/store/postgres/schema/007_readable.sql` (the same column on `readable_messages`)
- Modify: `internal/store/rows.go` (`store.AppMessageRow` gains `FrankingKeyID`)
- Create: `internal/store/sqlite/migrations/00012_reports.sql`, `internal/store/postgres/migrations/00012_reports.sql`
- Modify: `internal/store/sqlite/queries/ops.sql`, `internal/store/postgres/queries/ops.sql`
- Create: `internal/api/reports.go`
- Test: `internal/api/reports_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `store.Ops.PutReport`, `GetReport`, `UpdateReportStatus`, `Audit`;
  `store.Messages.GetAppMessage` (the stored `(seq, epoch, uploader_device, commitment_c,
  franking_tag, created)` tuple Plan 1b task 23 writes); `api.FrankingKeys` (task 8).
- Produces: `POST /v1/reports`, `GET /v1/reports`, `PATCH /v1/reports/{id}`, and
  `franking_key_id` selection across rotation.

**The two equations, from `protocol/04` "Franking".** A report is verified only when **both** hold:

```
C = HMAC-SHA256(k_f, "dilla frank v1" || CBOR(envelope with k_f replaced by an empty bstr))
T = HMAC-SHA256(K_frank, "dilla frank tag v1" || group_id || epoch(8, BE) || seq(8)
                || uploader_device(16) || C || recv_ts(8))
```

The reporter submits `(group_id, seq, envelope, k_f)`. The instance recomputes `C` from the
envelope and `k_f`, compares it with the `commitment_c` it stored at upload, recomputes `T` from its
own stored tuple with the **`franking_key_id` recorded on that message**, and compares it with the
stored `franking_tag`. Authorship is bound through the instance's session-to-device record, which is
an operator attestation and deniable to third parties. The report view shows exactly the submitted
envelope and nothing else.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/api/reports_test.go`:

```go
func TestAReportVerifiesOnlyWhenBothEquationsHold(t *testing.T) {
	e, group, seq, envelope, kf := seedFrankedMessage(t)
	_, reporterTok := e.NewUser("reporter")

	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok,
		[]any{group, seq, envelope, kf})
	if status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatalf("POST /v1/reports = %d (%x)", status, body)
	}
	var out []cbor.RawMessage
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	var reportID id.ID
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &reportID)
	row, err := e.Repo.GetReport(t.Context(), reportID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("GetReport: %v", err)
	}
	if row.VerificationResult != "verified" {
		t.Fatalf("verification_result = %q", row.VerificationResult)
	}

	// A forged envelope fails the commitment equation.
	forged := forgeBody(t, envelope, "something else entirely")
	status, body = e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok, []any{group, seq, forged, kf})
	if status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatalf("a forged report must still be filed, got %d", status)
	}
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &reportID)
	row, _ = e.Repo.GetReport(t.Context(), reportID)
	if row.VerificationResult != "commitment_mismatch" {
		t.Fatalf("a forged envelope was reported as %q", row.VerificationResult)
	}

	// A wrong k_f fails the same equation, and a report naming a message the
	// instance never stored is 404.
	wrongKF := make([]byte, 32)
	status, body = e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok, []any{group, seq, envelope, wrongKF})
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &reportID)
	row, _ = e.Repo.GetReport(t.Context(), reportID)
	if row.VerificationResult != "commitment_mismatch" {
		t.Fatalf("a wrong k_f was reported as %q", row.VerificationResult)
	}
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok,
		[]any{group, seq + 9999, envelope, kf}); status != http.StatusNotFound {
		t.Fatal("a report against an unknown seq was not 404")
	}
}

func TestAReportAgainstATombstonedMessageStillVerifies(t *testing.T) {
	e, group, seq, envelope, kf := seedFrankedMessage(t)
	if err := e.Repo.TombstoneAppMessage(t.Context(), group, seq, e.Clk.Now().Unix()); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("TombstoneAppMessage: %v", err)
	}
	// The ciphertext is gone; the franking tuple is not, which is the whole
	// point of storing (seq, epoch, uploader_device, C, T, recv_ts) beside it.
	_, reporterTok := e.NewUser("reporter")
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok, []any{group, seq, envelope, kf})
	if status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatalf("report against a tombstone = %d", status)
	}
	var out []cbor.RawMessage
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	var reportID id.ID
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &reportID)
	row, _ := e.Repo.GetReport(t.Context(), reportID)
	if row.VerificationResult != "verified" {
		t.Fatalf("verification_result = %q", row.VerificationResult)
	}
}

func TestARotatedFrankingKeyIsSelectedByTheStoredID(t *testing.T) {
	e, group, seq, envelope, kf := seedFrankedMessage(t) // franked under key A
	e.Keys.Rotate()                                      // key B becomes current; A is kept
	_, reporterTok := e.NewUser("reporter")
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok, []any{group, seq, envelope, kf})
	if status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatalf("report after rotation = %d", status)
	}
	var out []cbor.RawMessage
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	var reportID id.ID
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &reportID)
	row, _ := e.Repo.GetReport(t.Context(), reportID)
	if row.VerificationResult != "verified" {
		t.Fatalf("a message franked under a rotated key verified as %q", row.VerificationResult)
	}
	if row.FrankingKeyID == e.Keys.CurrentID() {
		t.Fatal("the report recorded the current key id rather than the one the message was franked with")
	}
	// A key the instance no longer holds is a distinct, honest outcome.
	e.Keys.Forget(row.FrankingKeyID)
	status, body = e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok, []any{group, seq, envelope, kf})
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &out)
	mustUnmarshal(t, out[0], &reportID)
	row, _ = e.Repo.GetReport(t.Context(), reportID)
	if row.VerificationResult != "franking_key_unavailable" {
		t.Fatalf("verification_result = %q", row.VerificationResult)
	}
}

func TestTheReportViewShowsExactlyTheSubmittedEnvelope(t *testing.T) {
	e, group, seq, envelope, kf := seedFrankedMessage(t)
	_, reporterTok := e.NewUser("reporter")
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodPost, "/v1/reports", reporterTok, []any{group, seq, envelope, kf}); status != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatal("POST /v1/reports failed")
	}
	adminTok := e.NewInstanceAdmin("root")
	status, body := e.Do(http.MethodGet, "/v1/reports", adminTok, nil)
	if status != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("GET /v1/reports = %d", status)
	}
	var rows [][]cbor.RawMessage
	mustUnmarshalBody(t, body, &rows)
	if len(rows) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("reports = %d", len(rows))
	}
	var shown []byte
	mustUnmarshal(t, rows[0][4], &shown)
	if !bytes.Equal(shown, envelope) {
		t.Fatal("the report view does not show exactly the submitted envelope")
	}
	// It shows nothing else about the group: no other message, no member list.
	if len(rows[0]) != 8 {
		t.Fatalf("a report row has %d elements; the shape is fixed at 8", len(rows[0]))
	}
	// A non-admin cannot read the queue at all.
	if status, _ := e.Do(http.MethodGet, "/v1/reports", reporterTok, nil); status != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatal("a non-admin read the report queue")
	}
}
```

`seedFrankedMessage` writes one `mls_app_messages` row exactly as Plan 1b task 23's upload path
does — computing `C` from the envelope and `k_f`, and `T` from the instance key — and returns
`(group_id, seq, envelope, k_f)`. `e.Keys` is a test `FrankingKeys` with `Rotate`, `CurrentID`,
`Forget` and `All`.

**The four tests above are behavioural, and every one of them computes its expectations with the
same formulas the handler uses.** That is fine for behaviour — "a forged envelope does not verify"
is true whatever the domain separator is — but it cannot catch a wrong separator, a wrong field
order or a wrong integer width, because both sides would be wrong together. The franking equations
are a **cross-implementation contract**: protocol/04 shares them with the client and the Rust core.
So a vector corpus pins them, exactly as task 9's reject vectors pin the envelope:

Add `protocol/vectors/franking.json`, generated by the TypeScript reference generator Plan 1a
task 0 builds, carrying at least one accept vector with every input and both outputs:

```json
{
  "commitment": [
    { "name": "message with two attachments",
      "envelope": "<hex of the 9-element CBOR array>",
      "k_f": "<hex, 32 bytes>",
      "c": "<hex, 32 bytes>" }
  ],
  "tag": [
    { "name": "epoch 7, seq 42",
      "k_frank": "<hex, 32 bytes>", "group_id": "<hex, 16>", "epoch": 7, "seq": 42,
      "uploader_device": "<hex, 16>", "c": "<hex, 32>", "recv_ts": 1790000000,
      "t": "<hex, 32 bytes>" }
  ]
}
```

and `TestTheFrankingVectorsMatch` in `internal/api/reports_test.go` replays it:

```go
func TestTheFrankingVectorsMatch(t *testing.T) {
	doc := readFrankingVectors(t) // protocol/vectors/franking.json
	if len(doc.Commitment) == 0 || len(doc.Tag) == 0 {
		t.Fatal("the franking vector corpus is empty; Plan 1a task 0's generator has not run")
	}
	for _, v := range doc.Commitment {
		t.Run("C/"+v.Name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got, err := api.Commitment(mustHex(t, v.Envelope), mustHex(t, v.KF))
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("Commitment: %v", err)
			}
			if want := mustHex(t, v.C); !bytes.Equal(got, want) {
				t.Fatalf("C = %x, want %x", got, want)
			}
		})
	}
	for _, v := range doc.Tag {
		t.Run("T/"+v.Name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got := api.Tag(mustHex(t, v.KFrank), mustID(t, v.GroupID), v.Epoch, v.Seq,
				mustID(t, v.UploaderDevice), mustHex(t, v.C), v.RecvTS)
			if want := mustHex(t, v.T); !bytes.Equal(got, want) {
				t.Fatalf("T = %x, want %x", got, want)
			}
		})
	}
}
```

`seedFrankedMessage` stays for the behavioural tests; the corpus is what stops the two
implementations from drifting apart in step.

Run: `/home/thim/.local/go/bin/go -C /home/thim/Repositories/dilla/.claude/worktrees/sdd-dilla-protocol test ./internal/api/ -run TestAReport -v`

- [ ] **Step 3: Record `franking_key_id` on the message**

`reports.franking_key_id` exists in §4.3, but `mls_app_messages` has no column for the key a message
was franked with, so a rotation makes every older message unverifiable. **P2-D21:**
`mls_app_messages` gains `franking_key_id BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(franking_key_id) = 16)`, and
`store.AppMessageRow` gains the matching field. Migration `00012_reports.sql`:

```sql
-- +goose Up
ALTER TABLE mls_app_messages ADD COLUMN franking_key_id BLOB NOT NULL DEFAULT x'00000000000000000000000000000000';
ALTER TABLE readable_messages ADD COLUMN franking_key_id BLOB NOT NULL DEFAULT x'00000000000000000000000000000000';

-- +goose Down
ALTER TABLE mls_app_messages DROP COLUMN franking_key_id;
ALTER TABLE readable_messages DROP COLUMN franking_key_id;
```

with `BYTEA`/`'\x000…'::bytea` on Postgres. The all-zero default marks "franked before the column
existed"; the verifier treats it as "try the current key, then every retained key" and records
`franking_key_unknown` when none matches, so an upgrade does not silently invalidate history.

**The migration is not enough: sqlc reads the `schema/` directory, not `migrations/`.**
`mls_app_messages` lives in Plan 1b's `005_messages.sql` and `readable_messages` in task 8's
`007_readable.sql`, so both files gain the column too, or the generated `AppMessageRow` and
`ReadableMessageRow` models have no `FrankingKeyID`, `h.keys.ByID(msg.FrankingKeyID)` does not
compile, and step 5's `sqlc diff` fails against the migrated database. The schema files carry the
full §4.2 shape, including the length `CHECK` the `ALTER` cannot express on SQLite:

```sql
-- appended to both 005_messages.sql and 007_readable.sql (SQLite)
  franking_key_id BLOB NOT NULL DEFAULT x'00000000000000000000000000000000'
                       CHECK (length(franking_key_id) = 16),
-- Postgres
  franking_key_id BYTEA NOT NULL DEFAULT '\x00000000000000000000000000000000'::bytea
                        CHECK (octet_length(franking_key_id) = 16),
```

`store.AppMessageRow` gains `FrankingKeyID id.ID` in `internal/store/rows.go` (**P2-D24**), and
`store.ReadableMessageRow` already carries it from task 8.

Task 8's readable upload and Plan 1b task 23's ciphertext upload both write the current key id;
this task adds the one line to Plan 1b's.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the handlers**

Create `internal/api/reports.go`:

```go
// Commitment is protocol/04's C, recomputed from a reported envelope. The
// envelope is re-encoded with k_f replaced by an EMPTY bstr, which is what makes
// the commitment bind the content without binding the key that opens it.
func Commitment(envelope []byte, kf []byte) ([]byte, error) {
	var raw []cbor.RawMessage
	if err := cborx.Unmarshal(envelope, &raw); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if len(raw) != envelopeElements {
		return nil, envErr(server.CodeEnvelopeShape, "envelope has %d elements", len(raw))
	}
	blank, err := cborx.Marshal([]byte{})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	raw[8] = blank
	body, err := cborx.Marshal(raw)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, kf)
	mac.Write([]byte("dilla frank v1"))
	mac.Write(body)
	return mac.Sum(nil), nil
}

// Tag is protocol/04's T.
func Tag(kFrank []byte, groupID id.ID, epoch, seq uint64, uploader id.ID, c []byte, recvTS int64) []byte {
	mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, kFrank)
	mac.Write([]byte("dilla frank tag v1"))
	mac.Write(groupID[:])
	_ = binary.Write(mac, binary.BigEndian, epoch)
	_ = binary.Write(mac, binary.BigEndian, seq)
	mac.Write(uploader[:])
	mac.Write(c)
	_ = binary.Write(mac, binary.BigEndian, uint64(recvTS))
	return mac.Sum(nil)
}

// verify runs both equations and returns one of five stable result strings.
// A failure is recorded, never refused: a report that does not verify is
// evidence about the reporter, and discarding it would hide that.
func (h *Reports) verify(msg store.AppMessageRow, groupID id.ID, envelope, kf []byte) (string, error) {
	c, err := Commitment(envelope, kf)
	if err != nil {
		return "envelope_malformed", nil
	}
	if msg.CommitmentC == nil {
		return "no_commitment_stored", nil
	}
	if !hmac.Equal(c, msg.CommitmentC) {
		return "commitment_mismatch", nil
	}
	// The all-zero key id means "franked before the column existed" (P2-D21), so
	// the key is unknown rather than unavailable: try every retained key, current
	// first. Without this every message older than the migration reports
	// unverifiable, which is exactly what the deviation says it prevents.
	if msg.FrankingKeyID == (id.ID{}) {
		for _, k := range h.keys.All() {
			t := Tag(k.Key, groupID, msg.Epoch, msg.Seq, msg.UploaderDevice, c, msg.Created)
			if hmac.Equal(t, msg.FrankingTag) {
				return "verified", nil
			}
		}
		return "franking_key_unknown", nil
	}
	key, ok := h.keys.ByID(msg.FrankingKeyID)
	if !ok {
		return "franking_key_unavailable", nil
	}
	t := Tag(key, groupID, msg.Epoch, msg.Seq, msg.UploaderDevice, c, msg.Created)
	if !hmac.Equal(t, msg.FrankingTag) {
		return "tag_mismatch", nil
	}
	return "verified", nil
}
```

The route table, written out for the same reason:

```go
type Reports struct {
	repo store.Repository
	keys FrankingKeys
	clk  clock.Clock
	log  *slog.Logger
}

func NewReports(repo store.Repository, keys FrankingKeys, clk clock.Clock, log *slog.Logger) *Reports {
	return &Reports{repo: repo, keys: keys, clk: clk, log: log}
}

func (h *Reports) Register(mux *server.Mux) {
	mux.HandleFunc("POST /v1/reports", h.create)
	mux.HandleFunc("GET /v1/reports", h.list)
	mux.HandleFunc("PATCH /v1/reports/{id}", h.patch)
}
```

`POST /v1/reports` requires an `enrolled` session, loads `GetAppMessage(group, seq)` (404 when
absent), runs `verify`, and writes the `reports` row with `revealed_envelope` set to **exactly the
submitted bytes**, `k_f`, `franking_key_id` copied from the message, `verification_result` and
`status = 0` (open). `GET /v1/reports` requires an instance admin and returns
`[[report_id, reporter, group_id, seq, revealed_envelope, verification_result, status, created]]` —
eight elements, and no field that would reveal anything about the group beyond the reported
message. `PATCH /v1/reports/{id}` takes `[status(uint), result(tstr)]`, requires an instance admin
and writes an audit row.

**A readable-channel message is reportable too.** `GetAppMessage(group, seq)` reads
`mls_app_messages`, which a readable channel has no rows in, so `create` resolves its target in two
steps: if `group_id` names a live `mls_groups` row it reads `mls_app_messages`; otherwise it treats
`group_id` as a **channel id** and reads `readable_messages`, which carries the same franking tuple
— `franking_tag`, `franking_key_id`, `sender`, `seq`, `created` — because task 8 computes `C` and
`T` there by exactly protocol/04's equations, with `channel_id` in the `group_id` slot and epoch 0.
`verify` is unchanged: it takes the tuple, not the table. Without this a server-readable channel
would carry a franking tag that no code path ever checks, which is the same as not having one.
`reports_test.go` gains `TestAReadableChannelMessageVerifies`, which posts a message through
`POST /v1/channels/{id}/messages` and then reports it.

```go
package deploy_test

import (
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"strings"
	"testing"

func TestTheUnitVerifies(t *testing.T) {
	if _, err := exec.LookPath("systemd-analyze"); err != nil {
		t.Skip("systemd-analyze is not installed on this box: skipping the unit verification")
	}
	// "dilla.service", not "deploy/dilla.service": go test runs a package with its
	// working directory set to the package directory, which is deploy/ — the same
	// reason read(t, "dilla.service") and read(t, "../Dockerfile") below are
	// spelled the way they are.
	out, err := exec.Command("systemd-analyze", "verify", "dilla.service").CombinedOutput()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("systemd-analyze verify: %v\n%s", err, out)
	}
	// Two warning classes are expected on any box that is not the deployment
	// target and are ignored by name: the ExecStart binary is not installed, and
	// the dillad user does not exist. Anything else fails the test, so a real
	// directive error is still caught.
	for _, line := range strings.Split(strings.TrimSpace(string(out)), "\n") {
		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
		switch {
		case line == "",
			strings.Contains(line, "is not executable"),
			strings.Contains(line, "Failed to resolve user"),
			strings.Contains(line, "Failed to resolve group"):
			continue
		default:
			t.Errorf("systemd-analyze verify: %s", line)
		}
	}
}

func TestTheUnitDoesNotSetMemoryDenyWriteExecute(t *testing.T) {

	for _, line := range strings.Split(unit, "\n") {
		trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(line)
		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "MemoryDenyWriteExecute=") {
			t.Fatalf("MemoryDenyWriteExecute is set (%q); wazero's compiler back end writes and then executes generated machine code, so the wasi path would fail at runtime, not at start", trimmed)
		}

func TestTheImageIsDistrolessDebian13PinnedByDigest(t *testing.T) {
	df := read(t, "../Dockerfile")
	if !strings.Contains(df, "gcr.io/distroless/static-debian13:nonroot@sha256:") {
		t.Fatal("the runtime base must be static-debian13:nonroot pinned by digest; the unsuffixed tag will move to a newer Debian")
	}
	if strings.Contains(df, "static-debian12") || strings.Contains(df, "distroless/static:nonroot") {
		t.Fatal("do not use static-debian12 or the unsuffixed tag")
	}
	if !strings.Contains(df, "FROM --platform=$BUILDPLATFORM") {
		t.Fatal("the builder stage must be pinned to $BUILDPLATFORM so nothing runs under QEMU")
	}
	if !strings.Contains(df, "CGO_ENABLED=0") {
		t.Fatal("the build must be cgo-free")
	}
	if !strings.Contains(df, "USER 65532:65532") {
		t.Fatal("the image must run as the nonroot UID explicitly; the base config records only User: 65532")
	}
	if !strings.Contains(df, "EXPOSE 443/tcp 7882/udp") {
		t.Fatal("the container publishes exactly 443/tcp and 7882/udp")
	}
	for _, forbidden := range []string{"setup-qemu", "apt-get", "RUN chown"} {
		if strings.Contains(df, forbidden) {

	}

		"platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64",
		"if-no-files-found: error",
	} {
		if !strings.Contains(wf, want) {
			t.Errorf("the workflow is missing %q", want)
		}
	}
	if strings.Contains(wf, "setup-qemu-action") {
		t.Fatal("setup-qemu-action is not needed: the Dockerfile cross-compiles with GOOS/GOARCH")
	}
	for _, forbidden := range []string{"|| true", "continue-on-error", "if: always()"} {
		if strings.Contains(wf, forbidden) {
			t.Fatalf("the workflow is not fail-closed: it contains %q", forbidden)
		}
	}
}

	c := read(t, "compose.yaml")
	if !strings.Contains(c, "net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start") {

	}

}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the Dockerfile**

Create `Dockerfile`:

```dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
#
# dillad is pure Go — wazero and modernc SQLite included — so the binary
# cross-compiles with GOOS/GOARCH from a builder pinned to $BUILDPLATFORM and
# nothing ever runs under QEMU. Emulation "can be much slower than native
# builds, especially for compute-heavy tasks like compilation" (Docker's own
# multi-platform guidance), and buys nothing here.
FROM --platform=$BUILDPLATFORM golang:1.27.1-alpine AS build
ARG TARGETOS
ARG TARGETARCH
ARG VERSION
ARG COMMIT
WORKDIR /src
COPY go.mod go.sum ./
RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/go/pkg/mod go mod download
COPY . .
RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/go/pkg/mod --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.cache/go-build \
    CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=${TARGETOS} GOARCH=${TARGETARCH} \
    go build -trimpath -buildvcs=true \
      -ldflags="-s -w -X main.version=${VERSION} -X main.commit=${COMMIT}" \
      -o /out/dillad ./cmd/dillad

# Pinned by digest AND by the -debian13 suffix. The distroless README warns that
# the unsuffixed tag "will change in the future to a newer version of Debian",
# and static-debian12 is still published as a different image.
FROM gcr.io/distroless/static-debian13:nonroot@sha256:e2e927ec666bae08560abb3c55d0659eceabb657f56b6782ab500a9fc7f555e3
# Explicit, because the base config records only "User": "65532" with no group.
USER 65532:65532
WORKDIR /var/lib/dilla
VOLUME ["/var/lib/dilla"]
EXPOSE 443/tcp 7882/udp
COPY --from=build /out/dillad /usr/local/bin/dillad
ENTRYPOINT ["/usr/local/bin/dillad"]
CMD ["serve", "--config=/etc/dilla/dilla.toml"]
```

`SSL_CERT_FILE` is already set by the base image, so certmagic's ACME client trusts Let's Encrypt
with no extra work — but the CA bundle is baked at image build time, which is the second reason to
pin by digest and rebuild rather than float `:nonroot`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the unit**

Create `deploy/dilla.service` — every directive verified to exist in systemd 261.3:

```ini
[Unit]
Description=dilla server (dillad)
Documentation=https://github.com/jonasthim/dilla/blob/main/docs/deploy/README.md
After=network-online.target
Wants=network-online.target

[Service]
Type=notify
NotifyAccess=main
ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/dillad serve --config=%E/dilla/dilla.toml
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=5s
# exit.Config (78) means the operator must fix dilla.toml; restarting would loop.
RestartPreventExitStatus=78
WatchdogSec=60s
# ACME issuance plus goose migrations plus the pre-migration VACUUM INTO can
# exceed the 90 s default on a large SQLite file.
TimeoutStartSec=300s
TimeoutStopSec=45s

User=dillad
Group=dillad
StateDirectory=dillad
# The default is 0755; this directory holds instances.key_history, the franking
# key and the database.
StateDirectoryMode=0700
ConfigurationDirectory=dilla
UMask=0077

# How a non-root process binds 443/tcp.
AmbientCapabilities=CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE
CapabilityBoundingSet=CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE
NoNewPrivileges=yes
ProtectSystem=strict
ProtectHome=yes
PrivateTmp=yes
PrivateDevices=yes
ProtectProc=invisible
ProtectKernelTunables=yes
ProtectKernelModules=yes
ProtectControlGroups=yes
RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_INET AF_INET6 AF_UNIX
RestrictSUIDSGID=yes
LockPersonality=yes
# MemoryDenyWriteExecute is deliberately NOT set: wazero's compiler back end
# writes and then executes generated machine code, so setting it would make the
# MLS path fail at runtime rather than at start. A blind copy of a hardening
# template is exactly how that regression arrives.

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target
```

`StandardOutput` is left at its default, which is the journal, so dillad's JSON lines are read back
with `journalctl -o cat -u dilla | jq .`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the Compose file and the Proxmox helper**

Create `deploy/compose.yaml` in full — `deploy_test.go` asserts against its text, so it cannot be
described and left unwritten:

```yaml
# dillad, single container. The image is the one CI publishes; pin it to a digest
# in production and update deliberately.
services:
  dillad:
    image: ghcr.io/jonasthim/dilla/dillad:latest
    restart: unless-stopped
    # Bridged, not host: Docker sets net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start only for
    # private networks, so a HOST-networked container running as UID 65532 cannot
    # bind 443 and fails with "permission denied". If you do switch to
    # network_mode: host, add:
    #   sysctls: { net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start: "0" }
    ports:
      - "443:443/tcp"
      - "7882:7882/udp"
    volumes:
      # The data directory MUST already be owned 65532:65532 on the host:
      #   sudo chown -R 65532:65532 ./data
      # distroless has no shell, so there is no entrypoint script to chown it at
      # start, and the container will exit with a permission error instead.
      - ./data:/var/lib/dilla
      - ./dilla.toml:/etc/dilla/dilla.toml:ro
    healthcheck:
      # dillad serves /healthz itself; the image has no shell and no curl, so the
      # check runs the binary.
      test: ["CMD", "/usr/local/bin/dillad", "doctor", "--config=/etc/dilla/dilla.toml", "--quiet"]
      interval: 60s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 120s
    stop_grace_period: 45s
```
