Create `cmd/dillad/notify_test.go` in full:

```go
package main

import (
	"net"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"testing"
	"time"
)

// dillad implements sd_notify by hand — one datagram, no dependency. Type=notify
// in the unit means systemd waits for it, so a silent failure here is a service
// that never reaches "active".
func TestNotifyReadySendsExactlyOneDatagram(t *testing.T) {
	sock := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "notify.sock")
	ln, err := net.ListenUnixgram("unixgram", &net.UnixAddr{Name: sock, Net: "unixgram"})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ListenUnixgram: %v", err)
	}
	defer ln.Close()
	t.Setenv("NOTIFY_SOCKET", sock)

	if err := notifyReady(); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("notifyReady: %v", err)
	}
	if err := ln.SetReadDeadline(time.Now().Add(2 * time.Second)); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SetReadDeadline: %v", err)
	}
	buf := make([]byte, 64)
	n, err := ln.Read(buf)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Read: %v", err)
	}
	if got := string(buf[:n]); got != "READY=1" {
		t.Fatalf("datagram = %q, want READY=1", got)
	}
	// Exactly one: a second read must time out.
	_ = ln.SetReadDeadline(time.Now().Add(200 * time.Millisecond))
	if _, err := ln.Read(buf); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("notifyReady sent more than one datagram")
	}
}

func TestNotifyReadyIsANoOpWithoutTheEnvironment(t *testing.T) {
	os.Unsetenv("NOTIFY_SOCKET")
	if err := notifyReady(); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("notifyReady with no NOTIFY_SOCKET returned %v; it must be a silent no-op", err)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Add the image job to CI**

Append to `.github/workflows/ci.yml`:

```yaml
  image:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: [go, go-lint]
    permissions: { contents: read, packages: write }
    timeout-minutes: 30
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v4.4.1
      - uses: docker/login-action@v4.6.0
        with: { registry: ghcr.io, username: ${{ github.actor }}, password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }} }
      - uses: docker/metadata-action@v6.2.0
        id: meta
        with: { images: ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}/dillad }
      - uses: docker/build-push-action@v7.4.0
        with:
          context: .
          platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64
          push: ${{ github.event_name != 'pull_request' }}
          tags: ${{ steps.meta.outputs.tags }}
          labels: ${{ steps.meta.outputs.labels }}
          cache-from: type=gha
          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max
          provenance: mode=max
          sbom: true
          build-args: |
            VERSION=${{ steps.meta.outputs.version }}
            COMMIT=${{ github.sha }}
```

job is what `scripts/check-ci-workflow.mjs` exists to catch.

existence, its pinned action versions, the absence of `setup-qemu-action`, and the four
fail-closed rules.

- [ ] **Step 7: Validate the Compose file, skipping when there is no runtime**

The dev box has no rootful container runtime by default, so this is a Go test that skips rather
than a bare command that fails — the same pattern `TestTheUnitVerifies` uses for `systemd-analyze`.
Add to `deploy/deploy_test.go`:

```go
func TestComposeConfigResolves(t *testing.T) {
	docker, err := exec.LookPath("docker")
	if err != nil {
		t.Skip("docker is not installed on this box: skipping the compose validation")
	}
	out, err := exec.Command(docker, "compose", "-f", "compose.yaml", "config").CombinedOutput()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("docker compose config: %v\n%s", err, out)
	}
	// The resolved document must still carry the two things the file exists to
	// get right, after Compose has expanded it.
	for _, want := range []string{"7882", "/var/lib/dilla"} {
		if !strings.Contains(string(out), want) {
			t.Errorf("the resolved compose document does not mention %q", want)
		}
	}
}
```

A full `up` needs a public domain or a `behind_proxy` config, so it stays the runbook step
`docs/deploy/README.md` documents for the operator and the acceptance step for W8.

## Follow-up cards (not in this plan)

Work this plan deliberately leaves undone, each with the reason it is not v1 and the shape the fix
would take. None of them blocks a task above. Cards 2 and 8 are the same missing capability seen
from two sides and are shared with Plan 1's card 4.

1. **Interaction-group attachments** — the blob routes are channel-scoped and an interaction group
   has no channel. Needs either a synthetic channel row per interaction group or a second,
   group-scoped blob surface with its own ACL. (NV9, P2-9.)
2. **Moderator deletion of an E2EE attachment** — task 11 allows a channel's manage-messages holder
   to drop a reference, but the uploader is the only one who can delete the *message*; a signed
   moderation event is the missing piece, shared with interfaces.md's follow-up card 2.
3. **Per-community search language** — the bound `regconfig` parameter is ready and the Postgres
   schema could carry `search_config`; v1 ships one `dilla_simple` configuration, because a document
   indexed under one configuration is not findable by a query parsed under another, which makes the
   community the only sound unit and a language change a full table rewrite.
4. **`pg_trgm` and the FTS5 `trigram` tokenizer** as the indexed "LIKE fallback" the spec names at
   line 470 — both are available and trusted, neither is designed here.
5. **Claim-by-hash blob forwarding** — a forward currently re-uploads. The only safe shape is the
   OCI cross-repository mount (`?from=<source_cid>` with an empty body, accepted only when the
   session can already read the source), never hash-knowledge as the credential; park behind W10.
6. **S3 blob backend** — `blobs.storage_ref` already carries a backend tag and the locator is
   derived, so adding `s3` is a new `blob.Store` implementation plus a `blobs.backend` column, not a
   migration of every row.
7. **One franking implementation** — `ds.FrankingTag` (Plan 1b, `[32]byte` key, `uint64` recvTS) and
   `api.Commitment`/`api.Tag` (task 17, `[]byte` key, `int64` recvTS) compute the same two
   protocol/04 equations in two packages with two spellings. `protocol/vectors/franking.json` keeps
   them honest, but the right shape is one `internal/frank` package both import. Park it behind W5's
   DS freeze, because moving `ds.FrankingTag` touches the commit path.
8. **Moderator deletion of a readable message's attachment** — task 11 now refuses every
   non-uploader, moderators included, which is what R29/R34 say. The capability an operator will ask
   for is a signed moderation event that drops the reference and audits it; shared with card 2 and
   with interfaces.md's own follow-up card 2.
9. **Community message retention** (R28, D14; whole-branch review I5, fix-wave ruling) — v1
   enforces the community policy's `retention_days` for attachment references only;
   `delivery_retention_days` is stored and served but not enforced, and no message is deleted by
   either (protocol/09 § Communities says so). The full fix: a DS sweep step that archivally
   deletes ciphertext rows past `retention_days` by `created` without raising the pruned floor
   (the task-8 retention ruling), `PruneReadableMessages` on both engines with its FTS rows,
   `min(30, delivery_retention_days)` as the delivery trigger, tests on both engines. It interacts
   with the pruned-floor ruling and is not needed for daily use.
10. **Purge versus a first upload** (whole-branch review I9) — the upload's reference transaction
    re-checks the tombstone, which closes the race with a purge of held bytes on both engines. A
    purge of bytes the instance never held can still commit its tombstone between that re-check and
    a first upload's commit; closing it needs `blob.Purge` to take the same per-blob lock the upload
    path takes.

## Assembly notes

This file was assembled on 2026-09-24 from the Plan 2 draft against the binding contract
`interfaces.md` and against Plan 1 as assembled the same day. The task bodies are **verbatim**
except for the call-site rewrites listed below, each of which is provable and none of which changes
behaviour.

1. **`server.WriteError` takes two arguments, not three.** Plan 1 task 6 produces
   `WriteError(w http.ResponseWriter, err error)`; `internal/auth` imports `internal/server`, so the
   reverse dependency that a request-aware `WriteError` would need does not exist (Plan 1 deviation
   ID14). Every `server.WriteError(w, r, …)` in the task bodies — **160 call sites** — is rewritten
   to `server.WriteError(w, …)`. This is the rewrite P2-1 tells the executor to perform; it has been
   performed here, so P2-1 is now only a confirming read of `internal/server` as Plan 1 landed it.
2. **`server.Errorf` takes a `Code`, not a status and a string.** Plan 1 task 6 produces
   `Errorf(code Code, format string, args ...any)` with the status carried by the `Code` value, and
   declares the constants `CodeInvalidRequest`, `CodeBindingInvalid`, `CodeUnauthenticated`,
   `CodeForbidden`, `CodeLeafNotCurrent`, `CodeModeReadable`, `CodeNotUploader`, `CodeNotFound`,
   `CodeGroupExists`, `CodeCommitConflict`, `CodePruned`, `CodeInviteInvalid`, `CodeTooLarge`,
   `CodeCommitInvalid`, `CodeCommitmentInvalid`, `CodeCommitRequired`, `CodeRateLimited`,
   `CodeStorageFull`, `CodeVersion`, `CodeInternal` and `CodeProvisionalOutsidePairing`. **75 call
   sites** whose status matches the code's own status in the 21-row table are rewritten to
   `server.Errorf(server.Code…, …)`. One site whose status does **not** match its code —
   `422` with `E_INVALID_REQUEST`, in task 10's blob `PUT` hash-mismatch arm — is rewritten to
   `server.WriteError(w, server.WithStatus(http.StatusUnprocessableEntity, server.Errorf(server.CodeInvalidRequest, …)))`,
   which is exactly the escape hatch Plan 1 task 6 produces for a non-default status.
3. **The four codes are Plan 1's, and every call site now uses a `server.Code`.** The assembly left
   four `Errorf` sites as drafted because they named codes Plan 1's closed vocabulary did not carry;
   the controller settled that on 2026-09-24: **Plan 1 task 0** writes `E_ENVELOPE_SHAPE`,
   `E_ENVELOPE_TYPE`, `E_ENVELOPE_LIMIT` (all **400**) and `E_CHANNEL_MODE` (**403** — "the
   operation is not allowed for this channel's mode", resolving P2-D30's 400-vs-403 contradiction)
   into `protocol/02`'s now 25-row `## Errors` table, and **Plan 1 task 6** declares
   `CodeEnvelopeShape`, `CodeEnvelopeType`, `CodeEnvelopeLimit` and `CodeChannelMode`. Applied here:
   task 9's helper is now `envErr(code server.Code, format string, …)` calling `server.Errorf(code,
   …)`; its thirty-three call sites pass the constants; task 8's two `envelopeKF` sites and its one
   `E_CHANNEL_MODE` site pass them too; and task 9 no longer edits `protocol/02` or
   `internal/server/errors.go` — its step 4 verifies both instead, and its `protocol/07` change
   table drops from three rows to two. `scripts/check-protocol-docs.mjs` (Plan 1 task 0 step 15)
   diffs the document against `internal/server/errors.go` in both directions and is green from Plan
   1's own commit onward.
4. **`server.DecodeBody` takes four arguments.** Plan 1 task 6 produces
   `DecodeBody(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, max int64, v any) error`. Nine call sites
   written as `server.DecodeBody(r, &req)` are rewritten to
   `server.DecodeBody(w, r, 64*1024, &req)`. The cap is `interfaces.md` §5.3's "64 KiB on every
   other CBOR route"; `w` is in scope at all nine (each is an `http.HandlerFunc` method). P2-1 named
   `server.MaxBody` for this argument; **Plan 1 task 6 produces no `MaxBody` constant**, and the
   controller confirmed on 2026-09-24 that it will not gain one — the four-argument `DecodeBody` is
   the helper task 6 defines, and every cap is §5.3's number at the call site (64 KiB by default,
   `maxEnvelopeBody = 96 * 1024` on the two envelope routes per P2-D28, and
   `max_ciphertext_bytes + 4096` on Plan 1's `POST /v1/groups/{id}/message`). P2-1's text and the
   contract section above have been corrected, and this file now names no `server.MaxBody`
   anywhere.
5. **Task numbering is unchanged** (1–18) and no task was split, merged or reordered. The headings
   already matched the skill's `### Task N: <name>` form.
6. **Migration numbers are absolute now, and the two plans agree.** The assembly left them relative
   (`00007_structure.sql` onward, two higher than the directory would ever show) because it would
   not replace one assumption with another; the controller settled the sequence on 2026-09-24.
   Plan 1 lands `00001_init.sql`, `00002_mls.sql` and `00003_messages.sql` (its deviation **B19**),
   and this plan continues from `00004` in the order its tasks run: `00004_structure.sql`,
   `00005_channels.sql`, `00006_overwrites.sql`, `00007_bans.sql`, `00008_channel_members.sql`,
   `00009_readable.sql`, `00010_blobs.sql`, `00011_voice.sql`, `00012_reports.sql`. Every filename,
